**ISS-0646 — true root cause found and fixed: `tenant_default`'s trigger reverted on EVERY new-tenant provisioning, never a concurrency bug at all (MAJOR, resolved)** ([GitHub #651](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/651))

- **This corrects an earlier same-day entry that closed this issue prematurely** (see the two entries above it in this file) — that fix addressed two real, separate bugs, but this session's own independent pre-merge review found the core symptom still reproduced and correctly kept the issue open. This entry is the actual resolution.
- **True root cause:** `migrations/1107_fix_audit_chain_text_resource_id.sql` and `migrations/1109_iss0122_apply_chain_hash_lock_cast.sql` each contain an internal `DO $$ ... FOR rec IN (SELECT nspname FROM pg_namespace WHERE nspname LIKE 'tenant_%' ...) LOOP ... END $$` block that unconditionally recreates `bpm_audit_apply_chain_hash()` against **every existing tenant schema** matching `tenant_%`, every single time the file's SQL executes — not just the one schema the migration runner is nominally applying it to. `Migrations.runForSchema()` correctly and permanently skips re-executing these files against `tenant_default` once ledgered (proven via direct instrumentation across dozens of calls, single-threaded and concurrent alike) — but the file's SQL still runs in full every time **any** brand-new tenant schema is provisioned for the first time, a routine event in nearly every integration test file. That one execution's internal loop reaches back into `tenant_default` via the `tenant_%` wildcard and silently reverts its function to the pre-`1142` shape, with zero interaction with the migration ledger. **This was never a concurrency bug** — reproduced deterministically in a single, non-concurrent test binary.
- **How it was found:** three stages of direct instrumentation, each eliminating a wrong hypothesis — (1) instrumented `runForSchema()`'s own `applied.contains(filename)` check, proving the runner's outer skip logic was innocent on every call; (2) a DDL event trigger caught real `CREATE FUNCTION`/`CREATE TRIGGER` statements against `tenant_default` even in a single-binary, non-concurrent run, disproving the "concurrency race" framing entirely; (3) a second event trigger capturing `current_query()` verbatim showed the executing SQL was `1107`'s/`1109`'s full file content, reached via a **different**, genuinely-new tenant schema's first-time provisioning — leading directly to the `nspname LIKE 'tenant_%'` loop as the mechanism.
- **The fix:** added a guard to both files' per-tenant loops — `AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.schema_migrations sm WHERE sm.schema_name = nspname AND sm.version = '1142_iss0645_restore_adp10_payload_full_trigger.sql')` — skipping any schema where the later, correct migration is already present there. `1110`/`1111`/`1137` share the same loop pattern but touch a different, not-provably-affected function and were left untouched to avoid unproven scope creep.
- **Verified:** reproduced the failure on a fresh probe pre-fix (single binary, zero concurrency — confirmed the mechanism), then re-verified post-fix: 3 consecutive single-binary runs, a 2-binary concurrent run, and the full `zig build test-integration` (~40 concurrent binaries, this issue's own literal acceptance-criteria reproduction) — `tenant_default` retained `payload_full` in every case, and `TC-ADP-10-02` does not appear in the full-suite failure list at all. None of the 14 remaining full-suite failures touch GH-651's files; all have prior documented history as pre-existing, unrelated concurrency noise. `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 findings. `zig build test` — 89/89 steps, 1011/1075 pass (64 skipped), unchanged from baseline.
- **Why the first fix's "5 clean runs" didn't catch this:** the mechanism depends on whether any brand-new tenant schema happens to get provisioned during a given run — round 1's verification runs happened not to trigger it by chance of test ordering, not because it was fixed. This round instead pursued a mechanistic proof (instrumentation showing exactly which code path executes) rather than relying on repeated black-box reproduction.
- **Also resolves:** [ISS-0657/GH-673](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/673) (closed as a duplicate earlier today) — confirmed: its `TC-ADP-10-02` "trigger appears not to fire" symptom does not reproduce in the post-fix full-suite run.
- **No requirement status change** — corrective migration guard fix.

**ISS-0657 — `TC-ADP-10-02` "trigger appears not to fire" determined to be a duplicate of ISS-0646/GH-651, not a distinct GUC/session-state bug (MAJOR, no code change)** ([GitHub #673](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/673), closed as duplicate of [#651](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/651))

- **Finding:** deliberately reverted `tenant_default`'s `bpm_audit_apply_chain_hash()` on a probe database using GH-651's already-known 2-binary reproduction, then ran `TC-ADP-10-02` against that same database without further changes — it failed with this issue's exact reported symptom (`payload_full IS NULL` on both agent and non-agent rows). A reverted (pre-`1142`) trigger body never reaches the `payload_full` auto-population branch, so the column stays NULL for every row — this is not the trigger "not firing," it is a genuinely different function body still firing correctly.
- **Why the original investigation missed this:** its verification used a 300ms `pg_trigger.tgenabled` poller and `pg_proc` pre/post checks, which prove the trigger stayed *enabled* throughout — a different property from the function body being *correct*. GH-651's own fresh finding (a revert reproducible within ~20 seconds from just 2 binaries) was not yet known when ISS-0657 was originally filed.
- **Disposition:** closed GH-673 as a duplicate; consolidated tracking onto GH-651, which already has the precise evidence (a DDL event trigger trace) needed for the next investigation. No separate fix required — resolving GH-651 resolves this symptom too.

**ISS-0646 — two real, independently-verified bugs fixed on the `tenant_default` reconciliation path; issue's core symptom NOT fully resolved, stays open (MAJOR, partial)** ([GitHub #651](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/651))

- **Two genuine, confirmed defects found and fixed:** (1) `tests/integration/spt01_provisioning_test.zig`'s `TC-SPT-01-05` registered `defer cleanupTenant(alloc, &pool, default_uuid, "tenant_default")` — copy-pasted from the file's disposable-throwaway-tenant pattern, but wrong for this one test, whose whole purpose is to exercise `schemaNameForTenant()`'s default-UUID branch, which resolves to the SAME shared `tenant_default` schema every concurrently running binary depends on. This ran `DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS tenant_default CASCADE` + wiped its entire ledger at the end of every run that included this file — caught via a server-side DDL/DELETE event trigger. Fixed: the test now verifies the mapping without ever dropping the shared schema. (2) `migrations/GBL-133_iss0112_schema_ledger_reconcile.sql`'s Step 2 blindly `INSERT`s ledger rows for `1107-1111` against every SCHEMA-mode tenant without ever executing those files' DDL — confirmed via the poisoned rows' identical microsecond timestamps. Fixed via new corrective migration `migrations/1143_iss0646_fix_gbl133_ledger_poisoning.sql`, scoped to the poisoning's own timestamp-collision signature (an unconditional delete was tried and rejected after it was shown to also delete genuine same-pass applies). Also replaced two instances of cross-session-visible `ALTER TABLE audit_entries DISABLE/ENABLE TRIGGER USER` with the already-established, connection-scoped `session_replication_role` pattern in `audit_iss103_test.zig` and `xc02_audit_immutability_test.zig`.
- **Independent pre-merge review found the issue's core symptom still reproduces.** A parent-session review, before merging this fix, re-ran the issue's own exact reproduction steps (fresh probe database, full `zig build test-integration`) and found `tenant_default`'s `bpm_audit_apply_chain_hash()` **still reverts** — reproduced on 3 separate fresh probe databases, including with only 2 concurrently-run binaries, within ~20 seconds of a verified-correct fresh migrate. A DDL event trigger (more precise than the original investigation's schema-drop/`tgenabled` pollers) caught the exact mechanism: a single backend PID re-executed `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` for `1107_fix_audit_chain_text_resource_id.sql` and then `1109_iss0122_apply_chain_hash_lock_cast.sql` against `tenant_default` within one `TestHarness.init()` call, despite both files already holding genuine, correctly-timestamped ledger rows from the initial migrate — meaning `runForSchema()`'s `if (applied.contains(filename)) continue;` skip check did not prevent a real re-application within a single call. Neither of this fix's two mechanisms, nor the `-- reapply_on_drift` guard, nor a ledger string-match bug, explain this; the count-based (not filename-set-based) fast path in `helpers.zig`'s `runMigrationsForSchema()` is a remaining suspect but not conclusively implicated.
- **Disposition:** keeping both independently-reverified fixes (they are real and correct) — this CHANGELOG entry itself is a correction of an earlier claim in this same run that the issue was fully resolved. **GH-651 stays open**, `ISS-0646` marked **PARTIALLY_RESOLVED**, with the corrected finding recorded in `docs/issues/ISS-0646.json`'s `peer_review_20260810_correction` block for the next investigation to pick up.
- **Incidental discovery, filed separately, unaffected by the above correction:** `TC-ADP-10-02` shows a second, distinct failure mode (`payload_full` NULL on both agent and non-agent rows, with the trigger confirmed enabled and correct throughout) — filed as **[ISS-0657/GH-673](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/673)**.
- **Verified (for the two kept fixes only):** `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 BLOCKER/MAJOR/MINOR. `zig build test --summary all` — 89/89 steps succeeded, 1011/1075 tests passed (64 skipped), unchanged from baseline. `tests/integration/spt01_provisioning_test.zig`'s `TC-SPT-01-05` and `audit_iss103_test.zig`/`xc02_audit_immutability_test.zig` pass cleanly in both isolation and the full-suite runs used during this review — none of GH-651's directly-touched files/tests appear in any failure list from any reproduction run in this review, isolation or full-suite.
- **No requirement status change** — test-fixture and corrective-migration fix; the deeper reconciliation bug remains open on GH-651.

**ISS-0651 — `exp201_202_entities_test` TC-EXP-202-01/02/03 fixed: test fixture provisioning gap, production code left untouched (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #660](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/660))

- **Context:** this issue explicitly asked for a deliberate design decision between two fixes with different blast radii — routing `src/entities/commands.zig`'s `getOrCreateEntityTypeInstance()` through ambient `search_path` instead of explicit schema-qualification (production code), or provisioning the test fixture's tenant schema (test-only).
- **Decision:** fixed the test fixture; **`src/entities/commands.zig` is completely untouched.** `getOrCreateEntityTypeInstance()`'s explicit `tenant_id → schema` qualification is a deliberate invariant from [ISS-0156/GH-475](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/475) — routing it through ambient search_path instead would silently write to whatever schema happens to be active at call time rather than the caller's declared tenant, risking a production correctness regression (entity records landing in the wrong tenant's schema instead of failing loudly with `C42P01` as they do today) and weakening exactly the invariant ISS-0156 established.
- **The fix:** `exp201_202_entities_test.zig`'s three `TC-EXP-202` tests now provision `fixture.tenant_id`'s physical schema via the existing, idempotent `provisionTenantSchema()` (same pattern as ISS-0654/GH-663's `sim01` fix and ISS-0653/GH-662's `TC-ADP-01-02` fix), then switch the ambient tenant context so every other unqualified call in the same test (`entities.definition.*`, `cleanupEntityType`, the final verification reads) also routes to the same schema — giving each test full per-run isolation instead of silently sharing `tenant_default`. `TC-EXP-201-01/02` (already passing) were left untouched.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-exp201-202` — 6/6 pass, 0 leaks, re-run 3 consecutive times. `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 findings. `lint_test_isolation.py` OK, 135 files. `zig build test` — 89/89 steps, 1011/1075 pass (64 skipped), unchanged from baseline.
- **No requirement status change** — test-fixture fix; production code untouched.

**ISS-0652 — `db_integration_test` TC-DB-03-02 segfault fixed: double-`deinit()` call, not a vendor pg client bug (BLOCKER)** ([GitHub #661](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/661))

- **Root cause:** the stack trace (`free__anon_34564 → close → deinit → TC-DB-03-02`) pinpointed the bug directly without needing an external memory debugger. `TC-DB-03-02` called `h_phase1.deinit()` explicitly inside its Phase 1 block (to force early teardown before Phase 2 opens a second harness) **and also** had `defer h_phase1.deinit();` registered — `TestHarness.deinit()` (which unconditionally calls `conn.close()`, which frees `vendor/pg/pg.zig`'s read buffer with no re-entrancy guard anywhere in the chain) ran twice on the same harness. The second `close()` freed an already-freed buffer, corrupting the allocator and segfaulting inside `free()`'s internal `memset`. A plain double-free in the test's own code — **`vendor/pg/pg.zig` and `helpers.zig`'s `TestHarness.deinit()` are both untouched**, so the issue's "don't weaken pool safety invariants" criterion is satisfied trivially.
- **The fix:** removed the redundant `defer` — the same "close early, open a second harness, no `defer`" shape already exists correctly elsewhere in the same file (`TC-DB-01-02`'s `h1`/`h2`), confirming this is the established convention TC-DB-03-02 simply violated.
- **Incidental fix in the same file:** `TC-DB-03-01`'s `store.append()` call had no `defer result.record.deinit(alloc)` — harmless before ISS-0653/GH-662's fix (`duplicateFromParams()` used to hardcode `.metadata=""`, a zero-length no-op to free), but GH-662's fix made `.metadata` a real allocation, unmasking this as a genuine leak. Fixed alongside since it blocked a clean pass of the same build target.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-db-integration` — 7/7 pass, 0 leaks, re-run 5 consecutive times, zero crashes (previously: hard `SIGSEGV` on every run). `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 findings. `lint_test_isolation.py` OK, 135 files. `zig build test` — 89/89 steps, 1011/1075 pass (64 skipped), unchanged from baseline.
- **No requirement status change** — test-fixture double-free and leak fix.

**ISS-0653 — `event_store_integration_test` (3 failures), `xc02_audit_immutability_test` TC-XC-02-01/07, and `xc06_backwards_compatibility_test` TC-XC-06-08 flake all fixed (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #662](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/662))

- **`event_store_integration_test.zig` TC-ES-08-04:** genuine production bug in `src/event_store/store.zig`'s `duplicateFromParams()` — it built the returned `EventRecord.metadata` as a hardcoded `""` via `_ = metadata;`, discarding the real effective-metadata argument. This helper backs both the duplicate-idempotency-key fallback path AND the normal successful-append path (line 537), so **every successful `store.append()` call returned `.metadata = ""` regardless of what was actually inserted** — a real API-facing defect, not just a test artefact. Fixed by duplicating `metadata` with the caller's allocator (`catch ""` on OOM, matching the function's existing infallible-fallback contract). This unmasked a pre-existing memory-leak gap in the same test file: since `.metadata` was always `""` before, `EventRecord.deinit()`'s `if (self.metadata.len > 0)` guard silently no-opped on 15 discard-result `store.append()` call sites and 5 captured-but-unfreed sites — all fixed alongside with `defer ....record.deinit(alloc)`.
- **`event_store_integration_test.zig` TC-ADP-01-02:** the test never provisioned `alt_tenant`'s physical schema before appending/reading events under it — same class as ISS-0654/GH-663's `sim01` fix. A second asymmetry compounded it: `store.append()` issues its own `SET LOCAL search_path` scoped to `params.tenant_id`, but `store.read()` does not — it uses `opts.tenant_id` only as a row filter and trusts the pool connection's already-active search_path. Fixed by calling `provisionTenantSchema()` for `alt_tenant`, registering the event type and inserting the fixture row under `alt_tenant`'s own context, and switching `bpm.api_tenant_context` around the alt-tenant `store.read()` call.
- **`event_store_integration_test.zig` TC-ADP-01-03:** classic legacy-table-name-after-rename anti-pattern. `events`/`events_archive` were created in `public` by migration 001; `GBL-112` (TNT-01) later dropped both from `public` entirely, leaving `tenant_default` as the only schema where migration 027's `tenant_id` column/indexes still exist. The test's `information_schema.columns`/`pg_indexes` queries still filtered `table_schema`/`schemaname = 'public'`. Fixed by changing both literals to `'tenant_default'`.
- **`xc02_audit_immutability_test.zig` TC-XC-02-01:** re-measurement showed this is **no longer** `CONNECTION_REFUSED` noise (confirmed deterministic across 3 isolated re-runs) — it now hits the same `session_replication_role='replica'`-suppresses-triggers class fixed repeatedly this session. This test calls `harness.conn.exec()` directly for both its INSERT and the UPDATE under test (not through `insertAuditEntry()`, which already scopes the override), so the append-only immutability trigger was silently suppressed and the UPDATE succeeded instead of being rejected. Fixed by setting `session_replication_role = 'origin'` for the test body.
- **`xc02_audit_immutability_test.zig` TC-XC-02-07 (in-scope discovery):** a positional-argument-order bug in a direct `bpm_audit_compute_chain_hash()` call — the canonical 13-parameter signature is `(..., pipeline_run_id, payload_full JSONB, prev_chain_hash TEXT, trace_id TEXT)`, but the test positioned `'trace-123'`/`'trace-999'` as the 11th argument (`payload_full`, JSONB-typed) instead of the 13th (`trace_id`), causing Postgres `C22P02 invalid input syntax for type json`. Fixed by reordering to match the canonical signature.
- **`xc02_audit_immutability_test.zig` race-affected subset:** [GH-651/ISS-0646](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/651) reconfirmed **OPEN** via `gh issue view 651 --json state` (checked at both the start and end of this run) — per this issue's own instruction, the race-affected subset was not attempted. With TC-XC-02-01/07 now fixed, the file passes 8/8 in isolation across 3 consecutive runs; there is nothing further to characterize without the full concurrent suite, which stays blocked on GH-651.
- **`xc06_backwards_compatibility_test.zig` TC-XC-06-08 flake — root-caused, not forwarded:** both `audit_entries` INSERTs (legacy V1 row, then V2 chained row) execute inside the same open transaction (`TestHarness.init()`'s `harness.conn.begin()`). PostgreSQL's `NOW()` is `transaction_timestamp()` — fixed for the whole transaction — so both rows get a byte-identical `timestamp`. The test's original query used `ORDER BY timestamp, audit_id` and indexed `rows[0]`/`rows[1]` assuming `rows[0]` is always the legacy row; with `timestamp` tied, the sort tiebreaks on `audit_id` (a fresh random UUID per row, unrelated to insertion order), so roughly half the time the V2 row sorts first and its correctly non-null `prev_chain_hash` fails the legacy-row assertion — matching the observed ~40% flake rate almost exactly. Ruled out with evidence: Zig 0.16's default (no custom `test_runner` configured) test runner executes test blocks strictly sequentially within one binary, confirmed via the stack traces' single-threaded `compiler/test_runner.zig` `mainServer` loop; the trigger's `BEFORE INSERT` timing (the legacy row cannot see itself); and `tenant_id` collision (OS-CSPRNG UUIDv4, and the trigger's `WHERE tenant_id = NEW.tenant_id` scoping is independently correct regardless). Fixed by matching each queried row to its known `audit_id` explicitly instead of relying on sort-tiebreak array position.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-event-store` — 28/28 pass, 0 leaks, re-run 5 consecutive times, all clean. `zig build test-integration-xc02` — 8/8 pass, re-run 3 consecutive times, all clean. `zig build test-integration-xc06` — 9/9 pass, re-run **8** consecutive times, all clean (previously ~40% flake rate on TC-XC-06-08 alone across repeated isolated runs before the fix). `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 BLOCKER/MAJOR/MINOR. `lint_test_isolation.py` OK, 135 files, no issues, no baseline line-drift for the 3 modified files. `zig build test --summary all` — 89/89 steps succeeded, 1011/1075 tests passed (64 skipped), unchanged from established baseline. `tools/check_github_status.py` — operational.
- **No requirement status change** — test-fixture, corrective-migration-literal, and one production-bug fix (`event_store.store`'s `duplicateFromParams()` metadata discard). ISS-0653 marked **RESOLVED**; GH-662 closes on merge. GH-651 stays open, unrelated to this closure.

**ISS-0654 — `sim01_04_simulation_mode_test` TC-SIM-01-01 and `obs03_audit_log_test` TC-OBS-03-INT-02/05 fixed (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #663](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/663))

- **`sim01_04_simulation_mode_test.zig` TC-SIM-01-01:** both the simulation tenant ID and the test's real tenant ID are genuine, distinct per-run tenant identities routed through the schema-per-tenant machinery — but the test never provisioned either physical schema, so `instance_projections` genuinely didn't exist there (`C42P01`). Fixed by calling the existing, idempotent `provisionTenantSchema()` for both tenants, then following through two more instances of the same gap: `insertProjection()` and `registry.registerType()` are unqualified and need the threadlocal tenant context switched per-tenant (`event_type_registry` turned out to be per-tenant-schema too, requiring registration in both schemas), and `queryTenantEvents()` needs the same context set at call time. Also fixed the cleanup helpers to clean both tenants' schemas — the sim tenant ID is deterministic, so orphaned rows would otherwise accumulate in its schema forever.
- **`obs03_audit_log_test.zig` TC-OBS-03-INT-02/05:** re-ran with `-Dlog-pg-errors=true` and captured the real error for the first time — `C42883` from `src/obs/audit.zig`'s `handleList()`. `audit_entries.resource_id` was converted from UUID to TEXT by `migrations/1107`/`GBL-120` (same class already fixed in several places this session), but this `GET /audit` filter's `::uuid` cast was never updated — **a genuine production bug breaking the `resource_id` filter on the real API endpoint**, not just this test. Fixed by removing the incorrect cast.
- **Incidental fix:** `tools/lint_test_isolation.baseline.json`'s 4 `sim01` T030 entries had stale line numbers after the TC-SIM-01-01 fix shifted later lines by +63 — corrected, same pattern as GH-652's `iss107`-triggered baseline drift.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-stage11-sim-xc04` — 14/14 pass (was 12/14). `zig build test-integration-obs03` — 6/6 pass (was 4/6). Both re-run twice. `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 findings. `lint_test_isolation.py` OK, 135 files. `zig build test` — 89/89 steps, 1011/1075 pass (64 skipped), unchanged from baseline.
- **No requirement status change** — test-fixture fixes plus one production query-filter fix.

**ISS-0656 — `lint_handoffs.py` H004/H009 findings from 2026-08-09 runs acknowledged (MINOR)** ([GitHub #666](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/666))

- **Context:** incidental finding from GH-652's Step Final review (`tools/lint_handoffs.py` showed 1 unacknowledged BLOCKER + 1 MAJOR, both predating GH-652's own branch), filed and forwarded rather than fixed inline on that branch.
- **Investigated for recoverable evidence first, per this repo's own no-fabrication rule:** `handoffs/orchestrator.log` was cross-referenced for both timestamps. Neither yielded a recoverable true value — `WF03-GH631-20260809`'s ROUTE line matches the handoff's `created_at` exactly with no independent "started" log entry to derive a different `started_at` from (H004); `WF03-GH360-20260809`'s log never shows a COMPLETE line for step-00 at all, confirming its `IN_PROGRESS` status is an accurate reflection of a genuinely unlogged completion, not a data-entry slip (H009). Both match ISS-0627's existing H004/H009 acceptance rationale exactly.
- **The fix:** appended both findings to `tools/lint_handoffs.baseline.json` using the existing H004/H009 `reason_ref` entries. Caught and avoided a near-miss: `tools/generate_lint_handoffs_baseline.py --apply` (the obvious tool for this) turned out to only ever regenerate the H003/H004/H005/H009 subset — running it would have silently dropped the 53 H013 + 9 H007 entries a later pass (WF03-GH596-20260808) had added to the same file, destroying real acknowledgment history. Caught via a dry-run entry-count diff before applying anything; reverted and appended the 2 new entries by hand instead, preserving all 289 pre-existing entries verbatim.
- **Verified:** `tools/lint_handoffs.py` — 0 BLOCKER, 0 MAJOR (was 1 BLOCKER + 1 MAJOR), 291 ACKNOWLEDGED. 1 pre-existing unrelated MINOR (H010 registry coverage) untouched.
- **No requirement status change** — audit-trail bookkeeping fix.

**ISS-0647 — 12 of 20 residual test-integration files fixed, 1 confirmed false-positive, 6 forwarded (MAJOR, partial)** ([GitHub #652](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/652))

- **Context:** follow-up to GH-649/ISS-0645's triage of a 26-file residual cluster left after GH-482/ISS-0150's adp0x schema-literal fix. This run re-measured all 20 remaining files individually in true single-binary isolation (adding 9 new narrow `build.zig` steps — `test-integration-{db-integration,env02,env03,event-store,exp201-202,idn03,iss206,xc02,xc06}` — for files previously reachable only bundled inside `main_test.zig`, mirroring the `ee09`/`effects-subsystem` precedent from ISS-0649/GH-654), rather than trusting the original filing's concurrent-suite snapshot.
- **`env03_test.zig` + `iss206_token_multiset_test.zig` (memory leaks, investigated together per the issue's own recommendation):** two *separate* root causes, not a shared one as suspected. `env03`: `src/definition/promotion.zig`'s `promoteDefinition()` never called `.deinit()` on the `Definition` returned by `importDefinition()` — a stale comment claimed `"Definition struct has no deinit"`, which was simply false (`graph.zig`'s `Definition.deinit()` has existed all along and frees the nested `DefinitionGraph` node/edge dupes `parseGraphJson` allocates). `iss206`: the test itself `allocator.dupe`'d a JSON object-map key before `put`-ing it, but `std.json.ObjectMap.deinit` only frees the map's internal storage, never caller-owned key strings — fixed by using the string literal directly (no dupe needed).
- **`onboarding_realm_guard_test.zig` (TC-ISS0071-01, highest-confidence business-logic candidate per the issue):** `src/api/routes/onboarding.zig`'s `markOnboardingRealmMissing()` hardcoded `UPDATE tenant_default.onboarding_registry` — but the table has always lived in `public` (confirmed via `migrations/056_onboarding_registry.sql` and every later migration touching it). The `UPDATE` errored with `C42P01` on every call and was silently discarded by a trailing `catch {}`, so the DB row never actually transitioned to `failed` despite the HTTP response already claiming it had. Fixed by correcting the qualifier to `public.onboarding_registry`. **Also fixed the sibling function `persistOnboardingResult()`, which had the identical hardcoded-`tenant_default` bug** — this is the same regression [ISS-0070/GH-285](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/285) already flagged as "currently live" via incidental discovery during ISS-0097/GH-352 (2026-08-09), independently re-found here; fixing both call sites in the same file in one pass closes GH-285's finding too.
- **`idn03_role_access_test.zig` (TC-IDN-03-03b) — genuine production bug, high blast radius:** `src/tasks/store.zig`'s `listCursor()` cast the USER-branch `assignee_ref = $N` comparison to `::uuid`, but `tasks.assignee_ref` is `TEXT` — every `TASK_WORKER GET /tasks` call failed with `C42883 operator does not exist: text = uuid`, a complete break of task listing for that role via the API. Fixed by removing the incorrect cast from both the USER and non-group-membership branches (the GROUP branch's `tasks.assignee_ref::uuid` cast against `group_members.group_id` was already correct).
- **`exp601_tier_quota_test.zig` (TC-EXP-601-04):** two constraint violations masked by the issue's noted cross-owner-connection warning — `instance_waits.kind='sandbox'` violated `instance_waits_kind_check` (only `timer`/`catch_event`/`human_task` are valid; fixed by using `'timer'`), then `dead_letter_items` INSERT referenced a nonexistent `tenant_id` column and omitted several other `NOT NULL` columns (fixed by rewriting to match the table's real PER_TENANT-only shape, per the existing `obs05_dlq_test.zig` pattern).
- **`spt01_iss0068_onboarding_schema_test.zig` (TC-SPT-01-ISS68-02):** `TC-SPT-01-ISS68-01`'s cleanup helper deleted the `tenant_schemas` row and physical schema but never the `public.tenant` row itself, leaving an orphan for the next test in the same binary to trip over — a test-isolation bug, not the shared-tenant-count-drift the issue paired it with `env01` under (env01 itself is confirmed passing in isolation, concurrency noise as suspected).
- **`iss107_tenant_storage_mode_test.zig`:** asserted the pre-`087_default_tenant_storage_mode_cutover.sql` value (`LEGACY_RLS`); that migration deliberately and permanently cuts the default tenant to `SCHEMA` so `GBL-084`/ISS-503's pre-flight gate can pass on a fresh bootstrap. Updated the expectation.
- **`iss0602_cross_process_isolation_test.zig` (TC-ISS-0602-cross-02):** failed even in true isolation — the test queried `pg_stat_activity` for its sibling connection's `'idle in transaction'` state from that same sibling connection, which can never see itself as idle while actively running the observing query. Fixed by issuing the check from a different connection.
- **`iss0605_orphan_self_heal_test.zig`:** not a self-heal defect — collateral damage from `lint_test_isolation.baseline.json`'s line numbers going stale after the `iss107` fix shifted lines in that file; updated the baseline's recorded line numbers.
- **`iss0125_cascade_test.zig` (TC-ISS-0125-02, -04):** same `session_replication_role='replica'`-suppresses-triggers root cause as ISS-0645/GH-649's `adp09`/`xc02` fixes — `TestHarness.init()` sets it session-wide, which also disables `ON DELETE CASCADE` (an internal trigger) and the file's own custom `BEFORE DELETE` trigger. Fixed by scoping `'origin'` around the specific calls that need real trigger behaviour.
- **`env02_test.zig` (TC-ENV-02-01):** the test's tenant-insert helpers never set `storage_mode`, defaulting to the column's `LEGACY_RLS` default — the `'SCHEMA'` default documented for "newly provisioned tenants" only applies via the `provisionTenantSchema()` code path, bypassed by these bare `INSERT`s. Fixed by setting `storage_mode='SCHEMA'` explicitly.
- **`xc06_backwards_compatibility_test.zig` (8 of 9 tests):** `TC-XC-06-03/05/06/07` failed on `events.actor_id` then `events.sequence_number` (both `NOT NULL`, no default) being omitted from 4 fixture `INSERT`s — fixed by adding generated `actor_id`s and per-instance sequence numbers. `TC-XC-06-08` hit the same `session_replication_role` trigger-suppression class as `iss0125` above (`trg_bpm_audit_apply_chain_hash`) — fixed by wrapping both `audit_entries` inserts with the origin/replica override; passed on the first post-fix isolated run but showed a new, non-deterministic failure on 2 subsequent re-runs (a per-tenant-scoped chain-hash lookup unexpectedly returning a non-null `prev_chain_hash` for a fresh tenant's first row) — this residual flake is forwarded, not claimed fixed.
- **Already-passing, false positive:** `svc04`/`test-integration-svc` — 35/35 pass across 2 isolated runs; the original `PgError.ServerError` signature was concurrent-suite noise.
- **Forwarded (genuine design-level defects, not fixed here):** [ISS-0651/GH-660](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/660) — `exp201_202_entities_test.zig` TC-EXP-202-01/02/03, entities-subsystem tenant-schema qualification mismatch between production code and LEGACY_RLS-style test fixtures. [ISS-0652/GH-661](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/661) — `db_integration_test.zig` TC-DB-03-02, a genuine **segfault** in `vendor/pg/pg.zig`'s connection-close path (NOT the Windows-handle noise the original filing suspected — re-measurement in isolation shows a hard crash). [ISS-0653/GH-662](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/662) — `event_store_integration_test.zig` (3 distinct failures), `xc02`'s residual 2-of-7 (tracked toward ISS-0646/GH-651), and `xc06` TC-XC-06-08's newly-surfaced flake. [ISS-0654/GH-663](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/663) — `sim01_04_simulation_mode_test.zig` TC-SIM-01-01 (missing `instance_projections` relation) and `obs03_audit_log_test.zig` TC-OBS-03-INT-02/05 (500 errors, real cause not yet captured).
- **Incidental discovery filed separately:** [ISS-0655/GH-664](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/664) — `xc04_kernel_determinism_test.zig` TC-XC-04-04 (function-signature mismatch on `bpm_audit_compute_chain_hash`), surfaced via the shared `test-integration-stage11-sim-xc04` binary while triaging `sim01`; not one of GH-652's original 20 files.
- **Verified:** `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 BLOCKER/MAJOR/MINOR. `zig build test` (unit suite) — 89/89 steps, 1011/1075 tests passed (64 skipped), unchanged from established baseline. Every fixed file re-run at least twice in true single-binary isolation, all green.
- **No requirement status change** — test-fixture, corrective-code, and one production-bug fix (`tasks/store.zig`'s `TASK_WORKER` task-listing break). ISS-0647 marked **PARTIALLY_RESOLVED**; GH-652 stays open with the full disposition record, tracked onward via GH-660/661/662/663/664.

**ISS-0649 — `ee09_merge_variables_test` TC-ISS-202-01 and `effects_subsystem_test` TC-EXP-301-09 fixed (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #654](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/654))

- **`ee09_merge_variables_test.zig` TC-ISS-202-01:** two compounding bugs in the test's hand-rolled raw-SQL fixture setup — a missing `NOT NULL definition_name`/`definition_ver` on `instance_definition_snapshots`, then a missing `NOT NULL` FK `tasks.token_id` never populated at all. Replaced the entire hand-rolled setup with the `activate()`+`create()`+`list()` pattern every sibling test in the file already uses (routes through the real engine, which populates every required column/FK correctly). Also fixed a genuine isolation bug: the test's cleanup `defer` was registered 170+ lines after definition creation, so any earlier failure orphaned a colliding row for every subsequent run — moved the `defer` to immediately after creation, matching the sibling pattern, and manually cleared the one pre-existing orphan.
- **`effects_subsystem_test.zig` TC-EXP-301-09:** root cause was a genuine pool-exhaustion bug in `src/effects/worker.zig`'s `sweepOnce()` — the same nested-`pool.acquire()`-under-a-held-lease class as GH-521/ISS-0187. `sweepOnce()` held its own connection for its entire body, including the row-processing loop that itself acquires connections per row; under a small test pool, this left zero free connections for the nested acquire, which failed silently (`catch return`), so delivery succeeded internally but the status update never landed. Fixed by releasing the fetch connection before entering the processing loop.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-ee09` — 5/5 pass (re-run twice). `zig build test-integration-effects-subsystem` — 32/32 pass (was 31/32). `zig build test` — 89/89 steps, unchanged from baseline.
- **Incidental finding filed separately:** [ISS-0650/GH-657](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/657) (MINOR) — `TC-EXP-301-08` (unrelated sibling test) leaks 3 allocations on the duplicate-idempotency-key path in `event_store.store.append`/`rowToEventRecord`; does not fail the test, DebugAllocator diagnostic only.
- **No requirement status change.**

**ISS-0650 — `effects_subsystem_test` TC-EXP-301-08 leaks 3 allocations on the duplicate-idempotency-key path fixed (MINOR)** ([GitHub #657](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/657))

- **Context:** incidental finding from ISS-0649/GH-654's work, filed and forwarded to the global queue rather than fixed inline.
- **Root cause:** `TC-EXP-301-08` calls `store.append(allocator, params)` twice with the same idempotency key to exercise duplicate detection. Both calls return an `AppendResult` whose `.record` field owns 3 allocations (`event_type`, `payload`, `metadata` — see `EventRecord.deinit()` in `src/event_store/store.zig:96-100`). The test read `.is_duplicate` off both results but never called `.record.deinit(allocator)` on either — a test-fixture omission, not a bug in `store.append()`/`rowToEventRecord()` itself. The established caller contract (already followed correctly in `tests/integration/event_store_integration_test.zig:409,420`) requires every `store.append()` result's `.record` to be freed via `defer`.
- **The fix:** added the missing `defer first.record.deinit(allocator);` / `defer second.record.deinit(allocator);` calls, matching the existing convention; changed `const first`/`const second` to `var` since `.deinit()` needs a mutable receiver. No behavioral change.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-effects-subsystem` — 32/32 pass, 0 leaks (was 32/32 pass with 3 leaked allocations), re-run twice. `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 findings. `zig build test` — 89/89 steps, 1011/1075 tests passed (64 skipped), unchanged from baseline.
- **No requirement status change** — test-fixture memory-lifetime fix.

- **Root cause:** `tests/specs/fixtures/gh512-baseline-snapshot.json` pinned `tools/lint_test_isolation.baseline.json`'s expected entry count at 115, set on 2026-08-08. Commit `28bf140` (PR #636, the ISS-0089/GH-338 sibling fix for `env_test_root.zig`/`svc_test_root.zig`'s T050 false positives) legitimately added 2 new baseline entries — both correctly-suppressed findings for pure re-export aggregator files with no `BPM_TEST_DB_URL` reference of their own — but the snapshot was never updated to match, so TC-RG-02 failed against current `main`.
- **The fix:** Bisected via `git log -p --follow` to confirm `28bf140` is the sole source of the drift (no other commit in the range altered the entry count). Updated the snapshot (bumped to `snapshot_version` 3): `total_issues` 115→117, `by_severity.MAJOR` 41→43, `by_code.T050` 22→24, with a documented rationale.

**ISS-0127 — pool.zig resolver LEGACY_RLS fallthrough on TC-TNT-03-01/03 (BLOCKER, already resolved)** ([GitHub #417](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/417))

- **Verified already resolved.** `src/db/pool.zig`'s `applyRequestStorageRouting()` reapplies `SET search_path` unconditionally on every `Pool.acquire()`, regardless of whether `tenant_context_mod.hasStorageMode()`'s cache already holds a value — the cache only short-circuits the DB round-trip to re-resolve `storage_mode`, never the `search_path` SQL statement itself. This is exactly the fix this issue's own candidate resolution specified. A prior attempt (branch `feature/WF03-ISS-0127-20260807`) only completed git-setup and was abandoned without a fix commit; the real closure came from this session's broader tenant-routing hardening rather than a fix targeted at this issue specifically.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-tnt` — 34/34 pass (2 binaries), including `TC-TNT-03-01` and `TC-TNT-03-03` by name. Re-ran twice to rule out the concurrency-sensitive flakiness the issue described — both runs identical, 0 failures.
- **No code change** — verification only.

**ISS-0645 — adp09 fully fixed, adp10 root-caused (partial), 26-file cluster individually triaged (MAJOR, partial)** ([GitHub #649](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/649))

- **Context:** follow-up to GH-482/ISS-0150's adp0x schema-literal fix, which forwarded a 28-file residual cluster (`adp09`/`adp10` plus 26 unrelated files) rather than attempting to root-cause all of it in one run.
- **adp09 — fully fixed (4/4 tests):** two independent root causes. (1) `TC-ADP-09-01`'s `pg_proc` catalog query was unqualified by schema, matching every tenant schema's own private copy of the three chain-hash functions — fixed by joining `pg_namespace` and filtering `nspname = 'tenant_default'`. (2) A stale UUID-typed overload of `bpm_audit_compute_chain_hash`/`bpm_audit_chain_canonical_payload` survived migration `1107`'s `DROP FUNCTION` because that statement's 12-argument type list omitted the 13th `p_trace_id TEXT` parameter, so the drop silently no-opped — fixed via new migration `1141_iss0645_audit_chain_function_overload_cleanup.sql` using the correct, complete signature. `TC-ADP-09-02/03/04` additionally needed `session_replication_role='origin'` scoped around their inserts — `TestHarness.init()` sets `'replica'` session-wide (GH-402/ISS-0601) and never restores it, suppressing the audit-chain trigger for the rest of every test's body; the fix reuses the exact precedent already established in `audit_chain_utf8_test.zig` (ISS-0149/GH-465). `TC-ADP-09-05` needed a `::uuid` cast removed from a direct function call to match the canonical TEXT-typed signature.
- **adp10 — root cause fully identified, 2 of 3 defect layers fixed, 1 forwarded:** migration `1107` silently dropped ADP-10's `payload_full` agent-actor auto-population block when it rewrote `bpm_audit_apply_chain_hash()` from scratch for UTF-8 safety, and `bpm_audit_build_agent_payload_full`'s `p_resource_id` parameter was never updated from `UUID` to `TEXT` alongside the rest of the resource_id-TEXT migration. Both fixed via new migration `1142_iss0645_restore_adp10_payload_full_trigger.sql`, independently verified correct via direct `psql` against multiple freshly migrated, workspace-owned probe databases. However, `tenant_default`'s function body was observed reverting to the pre-1142 shape under the full ~40-binary concurrent `zig build test-integration` suite, with no corresponding new ledger row — a distinct, deep, pre-existing test-infrastructure race in the per-tenant migration-reconciliation path, forwarded as **ISS-0646 / [GH #651](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/651)**.
- **xc02 — 5 of 7 tests fixed:** the same `session_replication_role` and `::uuid`-cast root causes as adp09 applied to `xc02_audit_immutability_test.zig`'s shared `insertAuditEntry()` helper and two direct `bpm_audit_compute_chain_hash` calls. `TC-XC-02-01` is unrelated `CONNECTION_REFUSED` environment noise, not a code defect.
- **26-file cluster — every file individually triaged, explicit disposition:** `iss202_merge_atomicity_test`/`iss601_state_snapshots_test` already passing (the issue's original snapshot was stale). `iss0185_dual_schema_test` is flaky/noise (present in only 1 of 2 re-measurement runs, ISS-0631/GH-606's class). 19 files individually re-measured twice against a freshly migrated, workspace-owned database, each with genuinely distinct, non-noise assertion failures, forwarded as **ISS-0647 / [GH #652](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/652)** with full per-file evidence (including two flagged quick-win candidates: a shared memory-leak signature between `env03_test`/`iss206_token_multiset_test`, and a clean business-logic mismatch in `onboarding_realm_guard_test`). `gh512_t010_regression_test` forwarded separately as **ISS-0648 / [GH #653](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/653)** — confirmed pre-existing on `main` before this run (baseline count drifted to 117 vs. the snapshot's expected 115), unrelated to the audit-chain cluster.

- **Verified:** `zig build` exits 0, no `error set` output. `zig build test` (unit suite) exits 0. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 BLOCKER/MAJOR/MINOR. `lint_test_isolation.py` OK, 135 files, no issues (baseline line numbers corrected for 4 entries whose positions shifted). `zig build test-integration` run against 5 independent freshly migrated, workspace-owned databases across this pass; adp09's 4 tests confirmed passing across 3 full-suite runs plus 1 isolated run; xc02's 5 fixed tests confirmed passing in an isolated run.

**ISS-0627 — remaining lint_handoffs.py historical debt resolved: H013, H001-structural, H006-CONDITIONAL, H007 (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #596](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/596))

- **H006** (1 finding): `CONDITIONAL` result status mapped to `PARTIAL`, matching the established convention for a mostly-passing-with-diagnosed-failures test run (3/5 layers clean, 1 layer 95.5% pass rate with root-caused failures).
- **H007** (53 findings — larger than the originally-estimated 69/~10-file scope): `workflow_id`/`sequence` renamed to `run_id`/`step` across `WF02-api07/08/09-20260523`, `WF03-integration-fix-20260523`, `WF04-full-20260523`. Caught and corrected a subtlety mid-fix: two runs' `workflow_id` held the workflow-type label (`"WF-03"`/`"WF-04"`), not the run identifier — fixed by deriving `run_id` from the run's own directory name instead. Two structurally-different files (a misfiled `docs/issues/`-shaped record, a genuinely truncated handoff) were acknowledged in the baseline rather than fabricated into the schema.
- **H001-structural** (8 files — more than the ~4 estimated): each read and fixed on its own evidence — a stray `]` closing an object instead of `}` (the exact example the issue named), raw unescaped newlines inside a JSON string, smart-quote + mojibake em-dash corruption (3 files, `PowerShell`-curly-quote pattern), a stray misplaced field pasted inside an unrelated array, an invalid `\$` JSON escape, and a file truncated mid-object (missing its final closing brace). Two of these fixes revealed pre-existing H004/H005 findings the unparseable JSON had been masking — added to the baseline under the existing categories.
- **H013** (53 findings): correction **rejected**, folded into the H003/H004/H005/H009 acknowledgment mechanism per this issue's own stated fallback. The 53 whole-hour-clock-skew offsets cluster around multiple different values (1h/2h/4h/5h/7h/8h/~10h) with no single consistent correction, and a spot-check of the least-round-number pair found a 9h55m23s gap that doesn't resolve to any clean hour value — confirming a uniform correction script would be actively wrong for at least some entries.
- **Verified:** `tools/lint_handoffs.py` — 63 BLOCKER + 72 MAJOR (this issue's four-category scope) → 1 BLOCKER + 1 MAJOR remaining, both from concurrent same-day workspace activity outside this issue's historical scope. `zig build` exits 0.
- **No requirement status change** — historical audit-trail data repair. Design: `src/design/fix-ISS-0627-h013-h001-h006-h007.md`.

**ISS-0094 — final 3 files with stale `resource_id = $N::uuid` casts fixed (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #348](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/348))

- **Context:** this issue's original premise (GBL-081/GBL-082 never applying to `tenant_default` due to `runForSchema()`'s blanket `GBL-` prefix skip) was investigated and found already superseded — migration `1107_fix_audit_chain_text_resource_id.sql` (filed later, for ISS-0122/GH-388) independently converts `audit_entries.resource_id` to `TEXT` and fixes the audit-chain functions via a correctly-scoped numeric (non-`GBL-`) migration that runs in every schema pass, achieving the same outcome through a different, already-working path. The underlying architectural gap (`GBL-` prefix as an unreliable scope proxy) is real but no longer causes this specific defect — a prior diagnosis pass (2026-08-08) had already confirmed this and identified the actual remaining work: 6 test files never updated their `::uuid` casts after the column type changed.
- **The fix (WF03-GH348-20260809):** Of the 6 originally-identified files, 3 were already fixed by earlier work this session (`ext02_webhook_dispatch_test.zig` — ISS-0637/GH-619; `adp02_tenant_scope_test.zig` — ISS-0638/GH-621; `obs05_dlq_test.zig` — ISS-0116/GH-379). Fixed the remaining 3: `obs03_audit_log_test.zig` (7 occurrences), `adp06_pipeline_run_correlation_test.zig` (3 occurrences, leaving the file's genuinely-UUID `instance_id`/`events`/`instance_sequence` casts untouched), `oidc09_jit_provisioning_test.zig` (1 occurrence).
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-obs03` improved from 1/6 pass (confirmed baseline via `git stash`) to 4/6 pass — the 2 remaining failures are unrelated pre-existing HTTP-layer 500 errors, part of the broader GH-482 cluster. Repo-wide grep confirms zero remaining `resource_id = $N::uuid` occurrences anywhere in `tests/` or `src/`. `lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 findings. `zig build test` 89/89 steps, unchanged from baseline.
- **No requirement status change** — test-code type-cast fix.

**ISS-0097 — threadlocal tenant context not inherited by worker threads spawned mid-test (MINOR)** ([GitHub #352](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/352))

- **Root cause:** `src/api/tenant_context.zig` stores the resolved tenant id in a Zig `threadlocal var`; `src/db/pool.zig`'s `Pool.acquire()` reads it unconditionally on every checkout to decide `search_path` routing. `tests/integration/instance_error_test.zig`'s `TC-EE-10-05` spawns two `std.Thread.spawn` worker threads that each call `setInstanceError` (which calls `pool.acquire()`); neither worker thread ever called `tenant_context.set()` itself, so both inherited a zero-valued threadlocal and fell back to `search_path=public`, where the business tables no longer exist post-GBL-073. Architecturally distinct from ISS-0095/#349: the fix could not live in `makePool()`, which already runs correctly on the main thread before either worker is spawned.
- **The fix (WF03-GH352-20260809):** `worker.run` in `TC-EE-10-05` now calls `bpm.api_tenant_context.set("00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000")` as its first statement, before calling `setInstanceError` — mirroring the identical, already-proven pattern in `tests/integration/concurrent_instances_test.zig`'s `completionThread` and `tests/integration/iss102_claim_test.zig`'s `claimWorkerThread`. Test-only change; no production code touched.
- **Mandatory production-impact investigation** (per the issue's own request): grepped `std.Thread.spawn` across all of `src/` — 4 call sites found. `webhook/dispatcher.zig`'s `CaptureServer.run` and `lua/timeout.zig`'s `watchdogLoop` never touch `pool.acquire()` (test-only HTTP capture loop; pure-atomics watchdog). `identity/provider/bootstrap.zig`'s `KcHttpCallState.threadFn` is a production path but is a pure Keycloak HTTP call with no DB access. `api/routes/onboarding.zig`'s `runSagaBackground` **does** call `pool.acquire()` (via `executeSaga`) on a production request-handling path (tenant onboarding) — but every table it touches either has `public` as its sole canonical home (so the "no tenant" fallback routing is correct by construction) or explicitly overrides `search_path` itself (`db_provisioning.provisionTenantSchema`, `migrations.Migrations.runForSchema`) rather than depending on ambient threadlocal routing. No production-severity gap found for *this* fix's scope.
- **Incidental finding filed (not fixed here):** the production-impact grep surfaced a currently-live regression of [ISS-0070/GH-285](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/285) — `persistOnboardingResult` (same call chain) hardcodes `UPDATE tenant_default.onboarding_registry`, which `migrations/GBL-134` (ISS-0185/#518) broke by correctly dropping `onboarding_registry`'s `tenant_default` shadow copy (canonical/sole home is `public`, confirmed live). Every onboarding-saga background-thread completion currently fails to persist its terminal state, silently swallowed by `catch {}`. Commented on GH-285, `docs/issues/ISS-0070.json` updated, forwarded to the global queue for its own WF-03 run — explicitly out of scope for this MINOR/test-only fix per the issue's own instructions not to scope-creep.
- **Fix direction chosen and rejected alternatives:** full reasoning in `src/design/fix-ISS-0097.md`. Chose "pass tenant id into worker thread context, `.set()` as first statement" over (b) a pool-level explicit-tenant-id `acquire()` API (rejected — no discovered call site needs it, would be unused surface area) and (c) restructuring `tenant_context` away from threadlocal entirely (rejected — the design is deliberate, per `pool.zig`'s ISS-501 comments, to prevent cross-request tenant leakage on a thread-pooled server; nothing in this investigation found a correctness problem with that design in the actual request-handling path).
- **Verified:** stash-based control confirms the bug — reverting just the fix line reproduces `TC-EE-10-05` failing at `std.testing.expect(r1_ok or r2_ok)` (both threads see `error.PersistenceFailed`). With the fix restored, `zig build test-integration` (properly sequenced, with `clean_test_db`) shows zero failures referencing `instance_error_test.zig` across 3 independent runs. `zig build` exits 0, no "error set" output. `zig build test` exits 0.
- **No requirement status change** — test-infrastructure fix; no requirement ID attached to this issue.

**ISS-0644 — new `MigrationScope.tenant_only` primitive closes recurring dual-schema shadows for 3 tables (MAJOR, partial)** ([GitHub #643](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/643))

- **Root cause:** `src/db/migrations.zig`'s `MigrationScope` had only `.public_only` and `.all_schemas` — no way to express "runs in every tenant-schema pass, never in `public`." ISS-0641/GH-637's `GBL-141` corrective migration cleaned 14 dual-schema shadow tables once, but for the 13 PER_TENANT ones, their source migrations still default to `.all_schemas`, so any fresh `tenant_default` (re-)provisioning — a cold `db_test` rebuild, a new tenant onboarding — re-creates the shadow. Confirmed live: a cold-start replay after `GBL-141` merged reproduced 8 of the 14 shadows again.
- **The fix (WF03-GH643-20260809):** Added `MigrationScope.tenant_only`, selected by a new `-- scope: tenant_only` header (mirror of `-- scope: public`), and the symmetric skip in the per-schema apply loop. Applied to the 3 of 13 PER_TENANT source migrations that are genuinely single-purpose (`026_ext05_subprocess_links.sql`, `093_exp103_instance_waits.sql`, `095_iss0176_lua_script_execution_audit.sql`) — the other 10 create multiple tables of mixed classification in one file (e.g. `008_identity.sql` mixes GLOBAL_REGISTRY `users`/`roles` with PER_TENANT `sessions`/`group_roles`) and were left untouched to avoid incorrectly suppressing a table that legitimately belongs in `public`; `GBL-141` remains their active cleanup-only mitigation.
- **Verified:** Live cold-start reproduction (`db_test` rebuilt from an empty volume, full migration + integration replay) — `tools/lint_dual_schema_table_names.py` went from reporting recurring shadows to 0 duplicates for the 3 fixed tables. `zig build test-integration-adp02`/`-exp` unaffected. `zig build test` 89/89 steps (+2 new unit tests for the scope classification). Migration idempotent on a second run.
- **No requirement status change.** Design: `src/design/fix-ISS-0644.md` (also documents which of the 10 remaining mixed-classification files would need a file split or a per-statement scope mechanism — future work, not implemented here).

**ISS-0641 — 14 tables duplicated between `public`/`tenant_default` after cold-start replay, ISS-0185-class (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #637](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/637))

- **Root cause:** 10 non-GBL source migrations (`004_definitions.sql`, `007_timers.sql`, `008_identity.sql`, `010_dlq.sql`, `012_event_retention.sql`, `019_idn04_api_token_management.sql`, `026_ext05_subprocess_links.sql`, `058_repo_artifacts_tenant_activation.sql`, `093_exp103_instance_waits.sql`, `095_iss0176_lua_script_execution_audit.sql`) create 14 tables (`api_token_audit`, `event_payload_store`, `group_roles`, `instance_definition_snapshots`, `instance_waits`, `lua_script_execution_audit`, `role_permissions`, `sessions`, `sla_records`, `subprocess_links`, `tenant_artifact_activations`, `user_groups`, `variable_schemas`, `webhook_deliveries`) without a `-- scope: public` header, so `migrationScope()` (`src/db/migrations.zig`) defaults them to `.all_schemas` and replays the unqualified `CREATE TABLE` in every tenant-schema pass, leaving a shadow copy alongside the canonical one — the same lineage as ISS-0185/#518, ISS-0620/#573, ISS-0623/#580, ISS-0183/#516, and this session's own ISS-0101/#359. 12 of the 13 PER_TENANT names had already been dropped once by `GBL-135_iss0185_drop_per_tenant_shadows.sql`, but grew back because GBL-135 never closed the creation path; `lua_script_execution_audit` (migration 095, post-dates the original ISS-0185 classification) is the 1 genuinely new name.
- **Classification (verified via FK-chain query + live row-count inspection on `db_test`, per `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0185-diagnosis.yaml`'s methodology):** 13 of 14 are **PER_TENANT** (canonical = `tenant_default`; `public` copies confirmed empty — `api_token_audit`, `event_payload_store`, `group_roles`, `instance_definition_snapshots`, `instance_waits`, `lua_script_execution_audit`, `role_permissions`, `sessions`, `sla_records`, `subprocess_links`, `user_groups`, `variable_schemas`, `webhook_deliveries`). 1 of 14 is **GLOBAL_REGISTRY** (`tenant_artifact_activations`, canonical = `public` — confirmed 8 live rows in `public` vs 0 in `tenant_default`; queried in `src/config/loader.zig` filtered by `tenant_id`, same shape as `service_catalog`). Zero FK dependents found on the `public` copy of any of the 14, so all drops are unambiguous and safe. No table was deferred to a follow-up — all 14 had clean, evidence-backed classifications.
- **The fix (WF03-GH637-20260809):** `migrations/GBL-141_iss0641_drop_dual_schema_shadows.sql` (new) drops the 13 PER_TENANT `public.<name>` shadows and the `tenant_artifact_activations` shadow from every tenant schema (loops `public.tenant`, mirrors `GBL-136`), idempotent and `RESTRICT`-only with per-table `dependent_objects_still_exist` guards. Each of the 10 source migrations gained an `ISS-0641 / GH-637` documentation comment recording its table's classification. **Correction made during implementation:** the first attempt added a file-level `-- scope: public` header to `058_repo_artifacts_tenant_activation.sql` (following the `031_adp04b_tenant_realm_binding.sql`/ISS-0101 precedent directly) — this was wrong and caught by running the full cold-start `test-integration` suite before finalizing: that file also creates `repository_artifacts`, which is **HYBRID** (confirmed by `tests/integration/iss0185_dual_schema_test.zig`'s `TC-ISS-0185-03`), not GLOBAL_REGISTRY, so a whole-file public-only header would have wrongly suppressed its `tenant_default` copy. Reverted to a documentation-comment-only approach for that file, matching the 9 other PER_TENANT-table files. A second self-inflicted issue was caught the same way: an early comment draft contained the literal substring `-- scope: public` inside backtick-quoted prose explaining what *not* to do, which `migrationScope()`'s naive substring search matched anyway, triggering `MigrationScopeMismatch` on a cold-start migrate — reworded to avoid the literal trigger string.
- **Verified:** `tools/lint_dual_schema_table_names.py` — 14 → 0 BLOCKER on `bpm_test` and `bpm_dev`. `zig build migrate` idempotent (two consecutive runs, "No new migrations to apply"). Full cold-start reproduction (`docker-compose rm -f -s -v db_test` + `up -d` + `zig build migrate` against the empty volume + full `zig build test-integration` replay — the exact technique the issue used to originally observe 6→14 growth): lint remained 0 immediately post-migrate; two test regressions surfaced mid-run from the `repository_artifacts` misclassification attempt (`TC-ISS-0185-03`, `TC-EXP-103-03`) and were confirmed fixed in isolation after the correction. `zig build` and `zig build test` exit 0, no "error set" output, no regressions versus `main` baseline (a pre-existing, unrelated `iss0173-orphan` `zig build` test-runner cosmetic quirk was confirmed present on `main` via `git stash` — `--listen=-` protocol reports a stale "failed command" banner even though the binary's own 4/4 tests pass and the overall build exits 0). `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py` and `tools/lint_migration_schema.py` both clean. **Known residual limitation** (filed separately, not fixed here): after the cold-start `test-integration` replay provisions a fresh tenant schema, `tenant_artifact_activations`'s shadow reappeared in `tenant_default` even with GBL-141 already recorded applied, because `migrationScope()` has no primitive to suppress just the tenant-schema-pass copy of a table in a mixed-classification file — manually restored to steady state for this run; see ISS-0644 below.
- **Incidental finding filed (not fixed here):** [ISS-0644/GH-643](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/643) (MINOR — `migrations.zig` has no `.tenant_only` scope primitive, so the 13 PER_TENANT shadow-cleanup fixes above are cleanup-only, not creation-path-closed, and will keep recurring on any future cold-start replay until a per-table or tenant-only scope mechanism is built).
- **Not built (optional, time-permitting per the issue):** suggestion #3, a structural pre-merge lint extending `tools/lint_migration_schema.py` to flag a new non-GBL migration with an unqualified `CREATE TABLE` and no scope header before merge. Deferred — a hasty implementation risked false positives across ~100 existing correctly-unheadered `.all_schemas` migration files without time to fully validate an allow-list; the state-based `lint_dual_schema_table_names.py` (already run pre-flight per `docs/guides/test_infrastructure_guide.md`) remains the active safety net.
- **No requirement status change** — migration/test-infrastructure fix; no new requirement introduced.

**ISS-0643 — `queue_add.py` no longer silently drops a same-session, not-yet-pushed prior addition (MINOR)** ([GitHub #639](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/639))

- **Root cause:** `tools/_queue_sync.py`'s `sync_queue_from_origin()` fetches `origin/main` and, on a successful fetch/read, returned origin's content wholesale as the base to mutate — correct for the cross-workspace race it was built to guard against, but it silently discarded any item a caller's own in-memory copy had already added earlier in the same uncommitted session (e.g. two sequential `queue_add.py` calls with no `git push` between them, exactly how ISSUE-FIXER's Step 0.5 "incidental discovery" procedure calls it once per finding).
- **The fix (WF03-GH639-20260809):** `sync_queue_from_origin()` now merges origin's items with the local fallback's items by `issue_id` via a new `_merge_items_by_issue_id()` helper: origin's copy still wins for any `issue_id` present in both (preserving the original cross-workspace safety guarantee), but an `issue_id` present only in the local copy is now carried over instead of dropped.
- **Verified:** two isolated Python-level reproductions — the exact reported scenario (sequential same-session adds of two different issues, no intervening push) now preserves both; the original conflict-safety guarantee (origin wins when the same `issue_id` exists in both with different state) still holds. `python -m py_compile` on all four touched/dependent files.
- **No requirement status change** — internal tooling fix.

**ISS-0101 — `tenant_default` shadow `tenant`/`tenant_hostnames` tables + cold-start `clean_test_db.py` blocker (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #359](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/359))

- **Root cause (sub-problem 1 — shadow table):** `migrations/031_adp04b_tenant_realm_binding.sql` and `migrations/050_tenant_hostnames.sql` both lacked a `-- scope: public` header, so the migration runner's `migrationScope()` (`src/db/migrations.zig`) defaulted them to `.all_schemas` and their unqualified `CREATE TABLE`/`INSERT INTO` statements ran again in every tenant-schema pass — creating shadow `tenant`/`tenant_hostnames` tables inside `tenant_default` that drifted out of sync with the `public` copies as later GBL-only migrations added columns (`tenant_type`, `production_tenant_id`). GBL-134 through GBL-139 had already cleaned these shadows up once (allow-listed as HYBRID in `tools/lint_dual_schema_table_names.py` pending this fix), but an accidental `DROP SCHEMA tenant_default CASCADE` + full replay during an earlier, unrelated WF-03 run (WF03-GH566-20260808, per `GBL-138`'s own header) re-applied 031/050 against `tenant_default`, resurrecting both shadows a second time.
- **The fix (WF03-GH359-20260809):** `migrations/GBL-140_iss0101_drop_shadow_tenant_tables.sql` (new) drops the resurrected shadows one more time (FK-ordered: `tenant_hostnames` first, `tenant` second, both `RESTRICT` + existence-guarded). `migrations/031_adp04b_tenant_realm_binding.sql` and `migrations/050_tenant_hostnames.sql` both gained a `-- scope: public` header AND full `public.`-qualification of every `CREATE TABLE`/`ALTER TABLE`/`INSERT INTO` statement (required so `src/db/migrations.zig`'s ISS-0604/GH-470 `MigrationScopeMismatch` misclassification guard does not reject the new header — it checks that a `-- scope: public` file contains no *unqualified* table work). This closes the creation path permanently: no future `tenant_default` reprovision, accidental or intentional, can recreate the shadow. `tools/lint_dual_schema_table_names.py`'s allow-list entry for `tenant`/`tenant_hostnames` was removed (it was a deliberate interim entry pending this fix). `tests/integration/iss0185_dual_schema_test.zig` updated: `tenant`/`tenant_hostnames` moved from the HYBRID assertion to the GLOBAL_REGISTRY assertion.
- **Root cause (sub-problem 2 — `OutOfOrderMigration` false-positive on cold-start):** confirmed already resolved by ISS-0603/GH-467 (per-run `walked_order` + strict less-than comparison means same-numeric-order duplicate-prefix files like `050_tenant_hostnames.sql`/`050_xc01_trace_id_audit.sql` never trigger a false positive). No code change needed for this check itself. Live-reproduced per the issue's destructive-operation requirement: `docker-compose rm -f -s -v db_test` + `up -d db_test` (genuinely empty volume) followed by the full `zig build test-integration` suite — zero `OutOfOrderMigration` occurrences anywhere in the run.
- **A separate, genuine cold-start blocker found during that live reproduction:** `tools/clean_test_db.py`'s `drop_orphaned_tenant_schemas()` unconditionally queried `public.tenant_schemas`, which does not exist yet on a truly empty database (this cleanup step runs *before* the test binary's own migration pass, per `build.zig`'s step ordering). Fixed by adding a best-effort `to_regclass('public.tenant_schemas')` existence probe that skips the sweep cleanly on a genuine cold start, matching the tolerant-of-missing-relations pattern already used elsewhere in the same file.
- **Verified:** shadow tables confirmed absent from `tenant_default` via direct query (both on a warm re-migrate and after the full cold-start reproduction); `zig build migrate` idempotent (two consecutive runs, exit 0) against both `bpm_test` and `bpm_dev`; `zig build` and `zig build test` exit 0 (`Build Summary: 89/89 steps succeeded`); `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py src tests` exits 0; `db_test` restored to normal long-lived state (119 public / 77 tenant_default migrations, single `tenant_default` schema, 8 orphaned per-test schemas swept).
- **Incidental findings filed (not fixed here):** [ISS-0641/GH-637](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/637) (MAJOR — additional tables beyond the 7-table HYBRID allow-list duplicated between `public`/`tenant_default` after a full cold-start replay; same ISS-0185 lineage, needs the same scope-header treatment applied more broadly) and [ISS-0642/GH-638](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/638) (MINOR — `lint_test_isolation.py` T050 false positive on two pure-aggregator `*_test_root.zig` files, blocking `zig build test-env-verify`'s C5 check).
- **No requirement status change** — migration/test-infrastructure fix; no new requirement introduced.

**ISS-0640 — `svc01` on_delete_cascade test now schema-qualifies DELETE and resets session_replication_role (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #631](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/631))

- **Root cause:** `tests/integration/svc01_service_catalog_scope_test.zig`'s on_delete_cascade test used `DELETE FROM tenant` (unqualified). `TestHarness` sets `search_path = 'tenant_default,public'`, so the unqualified reference resolved to `tenant_default.tenant` — not `public.tenant` where the test's setup `INSERT` landed. No row was deleted from `public.tenant`, so the FK cascade trigger (`RI_FKey_cascade_del`) never fired and the scoped `public.service_catalog` row survived. Second root cause: `TestHarness` pre-sets `session_replication_role = replica`, which sets `tgenabled = O` on referential-integrity triggers, disabling them entirely regardless of schema qualification.
- **The fix (WF03-GH631-20260809):** Two-part change in `tests/integration/svc01_service_catalog_scope_test.zig`: (1) Changed `DELETE FROM tenant` to `DELETE FROM public.tenant` to bypass the `tenant_default`-first search_path. (2) Added `SET session_replication_role = DEFAULT` before the DELETE (re-enabling FK triggers), followed by `SET session_replication_role = replica` to restore the harness state after.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-svc` — 35/35 SVC tests pass. `zig build test` exits 0.
- **No requirement status change** — test-infrastructure fix; no production code changed.

No test assertions changed; no existing test step removed.

**ISS-0112 — entity_type_instances/entity_record_latest missing from tenant schemas; broad 42-test cluster split into three independently-tracked pieces (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #375](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/375))

- **Rescoped before diagnosing.** GH-375's original 2026-08-01 report grouped 42 integration failures under one blob (ext02 webhook dispatch 10, ADP/OIDC migration checks ~8, env03 ~5, EXP-201/202 entities ~3, service catalog ~6, env01/env02 ~2, 8 other). Re-running the actual tests against current `main` (rather than trusting the stale 2026-08-01 grouping) showed the picture had already shifted substantially over the intervening week, so this run triaged current reality before fixing anything.
- **Piece 1 — ext02 webhook dispatch (10 tests): already resolved**, earlier in this session, by GH-619/ISS-0637 (PR #622, `audit_entries.resource_id` TEXT vs `::uuid` cast) and GH-618/ISS-0636 (PR #624, `createSubscription` never converting `req.secret` to `secret_ref`). Confirmed 10/10 passing on `main` before this run started; not re-diagnosed.
- **Piece 2 — entity subsystem (EXP-201/EXP-202, ~3 originally reported): fixed in this run.** Root cause: `migrations/094_entity_subsystem.sql` declares `-- scope: all_schemas` (so `runForSchema()` walks it on every tenant pass), but its `entity_type_instances`/`entity_record_latest` `CREATE TABLE` statements are explicitly `public.`-qualified — a qualified statement ignores the per-tenant `search_path` the runner sets, so it silently no-ops during every tenant pass instead of creating the table where `src/entities/commands.zig` actually queries it (`{tenant_schema}.entity_type_instances`). `GBL-137_iss0620_recreate_per_tenant_shadows.sql` had already backfilled these tables once (2026-08-08), but that one-time snapshot did not survive this workspace's `tenant_default` being reprovisioned afterward — the same "backfill doesn't survive reprovisioning" pattern already documented for `webhook_subscriptions.secret_ref`. Fixed by `migrations/1140_iss0112_entity_tables_tenant_scope_corrective.sql`: an `all_schemas`-scoped corrective migration (no `GBL-` prefix, no scope header) re-declaring both tables with **unqualified** `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, so the runner's `search_path` mechanism places them correctly on every future/reprovisioned tenant pass — a durable fix, not another one-time backfill. `entity_definitions` deliberately left untouched: `src/entities/definition.zig` queries it unqualified, so it already resolves correctly via `search_path`'s trailing `public` fallback and no test fails because of it.
- **Piece 3 — remaining scope (ADP/OIDC migration checks, env01/env02/env03, service catalog, 8 unspecified other cases): not duplicated, left under existing tracking.** Re-running the current `test-integration-svc`/`test-integration-env` umbrella (both are aliases of the same `tests/integration/main_test.zig` aggregate) on `main` shows 53 distinct failing tests spanning many unrelated subsystems (effects/EXP-301, EXP-601 quotas, ISS-206 token multisets, ADP-02/06/09/10, OIDC-09/12/15, XC-02/XC-06, OBS-03, IDN-03, EE-09, and more) — not a single coherent root cause, and already comprehensively tracked, with the same affected files explicitly named, under **GH-482** (open since 2026-08-06; dominant symptom there is a missing `tenant_<uuid>.schema_migrations` ledger, a different and broader defect than the entity-tables defect fixed here). GH-482 remains open and is the correct place for further work on this remaining scope.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-exp` — 13/13 pass (was 8/13), confirmed twice for determinism. `zig build migrate` idempotent on a second run (`No new migrations to apply.`). `zig build test-env-verify` — 10/10 HEALTHY. `zig build` and `zig build test` exit 0. `zig build 2>&1 | grep -i "error set"` empty. `python3 tools/lint_sql_param_types.py src tests` — 0 BLOCKER/0 MAJOR/0 MINOR. Design: `src/design/fix-iss0112-entity-tenant-scope.md`. Test report: `tests/reports/report-20260809-WF03-GH375-20260809.yaml`.
- **No requirement status change** — bugfix restoring already-specified EXP-201/EXP-202 entity-record behavior; no new requirement introduced.

**ISS-0115 — Task claim and group authorization behavior violates integration expectations (MAJOR, verification-only)** ([GitHub #378](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/378))

- **Finding:** Already resolved on `main` before this WF-03 run started. All 6 named tests (`TC-ISS-102-02/04/05/08`, `TC-IDN-02-06/07`) plus their 9 sibling tests in the same two files (15 total) pass repeatedly (6 consecutive runs each) against a live, freshly migrated PostgreSQL test database — including `TC-ISS-102-02`, the two-real-OS-thread concurrent-claim test.

(2) `TC-ISS-102-02/04/05/08` are most plausibly explained by ISS-0124/GH-390 (commit `3bc063b`, 2026-08-02), which fixed 15+ PostgreSQL C42883 asymmetric type-cast errors across 8 files one day after this issue was filed, from the same failing full-suite run window — a C42883 error surfaces to the Zig caller as a generic `PoolError`/`InvalidInput`, indistinguishable on first read from a genuine authorization-logic defect. `TaskStore.claimTask`'s single-statement atomic `UPDATE ... WHERE claimed_by IS NULL AND status = 'PENDING' RETURNING ...` compare-and-swap was already correct at the time of filing — there is no separate SELECT-then-UPDATE race.

No test assertion was loosened; no authorization check was widened.

Full `zig build test-integration` shows 68 pre-existing, unrelated failures out of 736 in the `main_test.zig` aggregate (trending down from the ~84 documented at the time of filing); none reference `TC-IDN-02` or `TC-ISS-102`. Design: `src/design/fix-ISS-0115.md`.
