**ISS-0116 — DLQ persistence/retry/discard audit trail silently missing; test-file `::uuid` cast bug; one test-only self-deadlock (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #379](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/379))

- **Root cause (three independent defects, layered — each one masked the next until the previous was fixed):**
  1. `tests/integration/obs05_dlq_test.zig` (`TC-OBS-05-INT-02`/`-03`) compared `audit_entries.resource_id = $N::uuid`, but `GBL-120_iss103_audit_resource_id_text.sql` (ISS-103) had already widened that column to `TEXT` — PostgreSQL rejects the comparison unconditionally with `C42883: operator does not exist: text = uuid` (same class as ISS-0637/GH-619 and ISS-0638/GH-621, now a third occurrence). Surfaced only by building with `-Dlog-pg-errors=true` (the vendored `pg` client swallows `ErrorResponse` payloads by default per ISS-0607/GH-542).
  2. Fixing (1) exposed a genuine production defect: `tenant_default.bpm_audit_resource_info` and `tenant_default.bpm_audit_action_for_change` — the SQL functions the `AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` audit trigger calls — were still the stale, `dead_letter_queue`-only, `UUID`-returning shape from `024_webhook_subscription_audit.sql`. `GBL-121_fix_audit_chain_resource_id_text.sql` (ISS-0122) had already corrected `bpm_audit_resource_info` to `TEXT`-returning with a `dead_letter_items` branch, but only in `public` — `GBL`-prefixed migrations only ever apply to `public` (`Migrations.run() == runForSchema(..., "public", ...)`), while `audit_entries`/`dead_letter_items` are `PER_TENANT` tables that only exist in `tenant_default`. The trigger survived the `072` table rename (Postgres binds triggers by relation OID, not name) and kept firing with `TG_TABLE_NAME = 'dead_letter_items'`, but the stale function had no matching branch, fell through to `resource_id := NULL`, and `bpm_audit_on_mutation()`'s `IF r_id IS NULL THEN RETURN; END IF;` silently skipped the audit INSERT — the `dlq.retry`/`dlq.discard` transitions themselves were correct and atomic, they just left no audit trail.
  3. Applying the fix for (2) exposed a second, pre-existing test-infrastructure gap: `tests/integration/helpers.zig`'s `runMigrationsForSchema()` "already migrated" fast path only re-verified a single hardcoded historical migration (`1106_...`) as a staleness proxy, not "does this schema have every migration `public` has." On the long-lived `bpm_test` container (`tenant_default` is deliberately never dropped by `tools/clean_test_db.py`), this meant any new migration filed after `1106` silently never reached `tenant_default` through the normal test-harness path.
  4. Applying the fix for (3) then exposed a third, independent bug in `TC-OBS-05-INT-03` itself: its cleanup used `SAVEPOINT s_audit_failure` on a plain `pool.acquire()` connection that was never inside a transaction — `SAVEPOINT can only be used in transaction blocks` (C25P01), previously masked because execution never reached that line before defect (1) aborted the test earlier. Wrapping the connection in `conn.begin()`/`conn.rollback()` to legalize the `SAVEPOINT` created a genuine cross-connection self-deadlock instead: the deliberate `CREATE TRIGGER ... ON audit_entries` needs to be *committed* so a second, separately-acquired pool connection (used internally by `dlq_store.discard()`) can see it fire — held open in an uncommitted transaction, its `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` DDL lock instead blocked that second connection's `INSERT INTO audit_entries` forever (confirmed live via `pg_stat_activity`, resolved with `pg_terminate_backend`).
- **The fix (4 files):**
  - `tests/integration/obs05_dlq_test.zig` — removed the stale `::uuid` cast on both `resource_id = $1` comparisons (matching the established `fix-ISS-0637.md` pattern); replaced the `SAVEPOINT`-based cleanup in `TC-OBS-05-INT-03` with a plain unconditional `defer` around `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS`/`DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` (both already idempotent — no transaction/savepoint needed, and none of the DDL needs rollback semantics since it never needs to be undone, only guaranteed to run).
  - `migrations/1139_iss0116_audit_dlq_rename_tenant_functions.sql` (new, plain numeric — not `GBL`-prefixed, so `runForSchema()` applies it to every schema including `tenant_default`) — re-creates `bpm_audit_action_for_change` (adds a `dead_letter_items` branch alongside `dead_letter_queue`) and `bpm_audit_resource_info` (`DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` + `CREATE OR REPLACE` matching `GBL-121`'s own drop-then-create pattern, since the `OUT resource_id` type changes `UUID`→`TEXT`; adds the same `dead_letter_items` branch). Harmless no-op re-application against `public` (already has this shape from `GBL-121`).
  - `tests/integration/helpers.zig` — generalized `runMigrationsForSchema()`'s fast-path staleness probe from a single hardcoded migration-version check to comparing the count of plain-numeric (non-`GBL`) migrations applied to `public` (just brought fully current, unconditionally, by the preceding `runMigrations()` call) against the count applied to the target schema; any shortfall now correctly falls through to a real `runForSchema()` call instead of skipping it.
  - `build.zig` — added narrow steps `test-integration-obs05` and `test-integration-obs06` (mirrors the `ext02`/`adp02`/`exp401-exp402` precedent) for fast iteration; both files were previously reachable only via the ~40-binary `test-integration` umbrella through `main_test.zig`'s aggregate import.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-obs05` — 3/3 pass (`TC-OBS-05-INT-01/02/03`, including two consecutive clean re-runs to rule out flakiness). `zig build test-integration-obs06` — 5/5 pass (`TC-OBS-06-INT-01..05`, including `TC-OBS-06-INT-05`, no regression). `zig build` and `zig build test` both exit 0. `zig build 2>&1 | grep -i "error set"` empty. `python3 tools/lint_sql_param_types.py src tests` — 0 BLOCKER/0 MAJOR/0 MINOR. `python3 tools/lint_test_isolation.py tests/integration` — 0 BLOCKER, 3 pre-existing MAJOR findings in `helpers.zig` unrelated to and unmoved by this change (confirmed present identically on `main`). Design: `src/design/fix-ISS-0116.md`.
- **No requirement status change** — bugfix restoring already-specified OBS-05/OBS-06 audit-trail and alert-state behavior; no new requirement introduced.

**ISS-0117 — EXP-401 definition creation accepted invalid process graphs (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #380](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/380))

- **Root cause:** `src/definition/graph.zig` defines `validateCompensationHandlers()` and `validateReversibility()`, both already returning the correct typed violations (`COMPENSATION_HANDLER_SCOPE_UNKNOWN`, `COMPENSATION_HANDLER_NOT_REVERSED`, `NON_REVERSIBLE_ACTIVITY`, `ERROR_BOUNDARY_EVENTS_MISSING`, etc.), but neither function was ever called. `src/definition/store.zig`'s three graph-mutating paths (`create`, `promote`, `update`) only ran `validateGraph`/`validateNodeAttributes`/`validateEdgeConditions`/`validateEdgeTransforms` before persistence — invalid compensation/error-boundary metadata sailed through and was persisted. A second, narrower bug: `parseErrorBoundaryConfig()` rejected an empty `on_error_event`/`on_cancel_event` string as `error.InvalidInput` before `validateCompensationHandlers()`'s dedicated `ERROR_BOUNDARY_EVENTS_MISSING` check could ever run, making that check unreachable dead code (it always short-circuited to the generic `ERROR_BOUNDARY_INVALID` instead).
- **The fix (WF03-GH380-20260809):** `store.zig` now calls `validateCompensationHandlers()` and `validateReversibility()` in all three graph-mutating paths, in the same pre-DB-write, fail-fast position as the existing four validation stages, mapping to `GraphValidationFailed`/`TransactionFailed` identically. `graph.zig`'s `parseErrorBoundaryConfig()` now lets empty `on_error_event`/`on_cancel_event` strings parse through so the specific `ERROR_BOUNDARY_EVENTS_MISSING` violation fires instead of the generic one. `build.zig`: added a new narrow step `test-integration-exp401-exp402` for fast iteration on this one file (mirrors the `ext02`/`adp02` precedent) — the file's tests were already reachable and executed under the full `test-integration` umbrella via `tests/integration/main_test.zig`'s existing `@import`, so this is a convenience addition, not a wiring fix.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-exp401-exp402` — 7/7 pass (TC-EXP-401-01..05, TC-EXP-402-01/02), including the four previously-failing `TC-EXP-401-02/03/04/05`. `zig build`, `zig build test`, and `zig build migrate` all exit 0. `lint_sql_param_types.py`: 0 BLOCKER/0 MAJOR. Design: `src/design/fix-ISS-0117.md`.
- **No requirement status change** — bugfix restoring already-specified EXP-401 validation behavior; no new requirement introduced.

**ISS-0636 — `createSubscription` now converts `req.secret` to a stored `secret_ref` (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #618](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/618))

- **Root cause:** `webhook.subscription_store.createSubscription` accepted a `CreateSubscriptionRequest.secret` field but never referenced it after reading it into the struct — the `INSERT` only ever wrote `req.secret_ref orelse ""`. Any direct caller supplying plaintext `.secret` (bypassing the HTTP layer's own pre-conversion) got a subscription with no `secret_ref`, so `dispatcher.zig`'s `secret_ref.len > 0` check never signed its webhook deliveries. `src/api/routes/webhooks.zig`'s HTTP route was already unaffected — it pre-converts `secret` → `secret_ref` via `secrets.Store.putSecret` before calling the store.
- **The fix (WF03-GH618-20260809):** Moved the same conversion (`secrets.Store.putSecret` via `webhook_keys.WebhookKeyOwner.putWebhookHmacSecret`, namespace `"webhook"`, purpose `.webhook_hmac`) into `createSubscription` itself, so every caller gets correct behavior, not just the HTTP route. The conversion runs *before* acquiring the connection used for the `INSERT`, since `putSecret` acquires its own connection internally — nesting a second `pool.acquire()` inside an already-held lease would exhaust the pool (the exact bug fixed previously in GH-521/ISS-0130). `validateCreateRequest` now also rejects `req.secret` and `req.secret_ref` both being set, mirroring the HTTP layer's existing `secret_conflict` check. `webhooks.zig`'s route is unchanged (it already passes `secret = null`, so the new conversion is a no-op there — no double-conversion).
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-ext02` — 10/10 tests pass (both TC-EXT-02-INT-05 and TC-EXT-02-INT-08 now green). `zig build test-integration-adp02` unaffected (4/5, same pre-existing TC-ADP-02-01 failure as before). Design: `src/design/fix-ISS-0636.md`.
- **No requirement status change** — bugfix in existing webhook subscription logic.

**ISS-0638 — TC-ADP-02-05 `audit_entries.resource_id` TEXT vs `::uuid` cast mismatch (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #621](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/621))

The mirrored `audit_log.entity_id = $1::uuid` comparisons on the same lines were left untouched — `audit_log.entity_id` is genuinely `UUID`-typed (confirmed via `migrations/009_audit_log.sql`) and unaffected by `GBL-120`.

**ISS-0637 — TC-EXT-02-INT-08 `audit_entries.resource_id` TEXT vs `::uuid` cast mismatch (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #619](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/619))

- **Root cause:** The issue as filed suspected `pool.Conn` losing `search_path` after `COMMIT` (mistakenly pattern-matched from an unrelated `SET LOCAL` usage in `src/db/migrations.zig`/`src/event_store/store.zig`). That theory is REFUTED — `src/db/pool.zig`'s `applyRequestStorageRouting` uses a plain session-scoped `SET search_path` on every `Pool.acquire()`, never `SET LOCAL`, and holds it for the connection's full lifetime. The actual cause: migration `GBL-120` (ISS-103) changed `audit_entries.resource_id` from `UUID` to `TEXT` in every schema, but `TC-EXT-02-INT-08` still compared `resource_id = $1::uuid`, which PostgreSQL rejects unconditionally with `C42883: operator does not exist: text = uuid` — confirmed via a new diagnostic build step (`zig build test-integration-ext02 -Dlog-pg-errors=true`) that surfaces the real Postgres wire-protocol error text otherwise swallowed by the vendored `pg` client.
- **The fix (WF03-GH619-20260809):** Removed the stale `::uuid` cast on both `resource_id = $1` comparisons in `tests/integration/ext02_webhook_dispatch_test.zig` (lines 687, 702), matching the `TEXT` column type the table has carried since `GBL-120`.
- **Verified:** `zig build test-integration-ext02` — TC-EXT-02-INT-08 passes. The suite's one remaining failure (TC-EXT-02-INT-05) is the pre-existing, separately-tracked ISS-0636/GH-618 (`createSubscription` ignores `req.secret`) — not a regression of this fix.
- **Incidental discovery:** The same `resource_id = $1::uuid` pattern independently breaks TC-ADP-02-05 in `tests/integration/adp02_tenant_scope_test.zig` — filed separately as ISS-0638/GH-621 and forwarded to the global queue; not fixed on this branch.
- **No requirement status change** — test-code type-cast fix; OBS-03/EXT-02 were already implemented. Design: `src/design/fix-ISS-0637.md`.

**ISS-0635 — Migration 1138 corrective fix adds `secret_ref`/`secret_key_id` to all tenant schemas where missing (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #616](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/616))

- **Root cause:** Migration `1134_iss0112_add_secret_ref_to_tenant_schemas.sql` was recorded as applied in `schema_migrations` but the `secret_ref` and `secret_key_id` columns were absent from `tenant_default.webhook_subscriptions`. `GBL-128` also failed to add them because it targets the `public` schema while `webhook_subscriptions` lives in the tenant schema. The gap caused `C42703: column does not exist` errors at runtime for any code path that referenced these columns.
- **The fix (WF03-GH616-20260809):** New corrective migration `migrations/1138_iss0635_webhook_secret_ref_corrective.sql` iterates over every schema in `information_schema.schemata` that contains `webhook_subscriptions`, and idempotently `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` for `secret_ref TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` and `secret_key_id UUID`. Applied to all tenant schemas during `TestHarness.init()` and on API server restart via `provisionTenantSchema`.
- **Verified:** `secret_ref` and `secret_key_id` columns confirmed present in `tenant_default.webhook_subscriptions` after `zig build migrate` + `TestHarness.init()`. 7 of 9 TC-EXT-02 integration tests PASS; the 2 remaining failures (TC-EXT-02-INT-05 and TC-EXT-02-INT-08) are pre-existing bugs (ISS-0636 and ISS-0637) exposed only now that the C42703 blocker is removed — not regressions of this fix. ISS-0635 marked RESOLVED.

The test file `tests/integration/ext02_webhook_dispatch_test.zig` was not updated — three `expectEqualStrings` assertions still referenced legacy lowercase/obsolete values: line 589 expected `'failed'` (should be `'FAILED'`), line 654 expected `'exhausted'` (should be `'FAILED'`; `'exhausted'` was remapped by migration 085 step 3), line 754 expected `'success'` (should be `'DELIVERED'`; `'success'` likewise remapped).

**ISS-0119 — Timer retry/pending/locking/rollback semantics (TC-SCH-02-02, TC-SCH-02-03) verified fixed (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #382](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/382))

- **The original defects:** (1) `processNextDueTimer()` returned `.none` instead of `.skipped_locked` when `SELECT … SKIP LOCKED` returned 0 rows in the ordinary-timer branch — causing TC-SCH-02-02 to fail with an unexpected return value. (2) A `catch break:fire_blk false` pattern in the timer-fire block silently swallowed `appendTimerFiredEventInTx` errors, preventing `TransactionFailed` from surfacing — causing TC-SCH-02-03 to fail by not observing the expected error.
- **The fix:** commit `6b042542` (WF03-GH567 / PR #572, merged 2026-08-08) fixed both: the ordinary-timer SKIP-LOCKED branch now counts `skipped_locked`, and the fire block propagates `appendTimerFiredEventInTx` errors rather than swallowing them.

**ISS-0632 — `lint_sql_param_types.py` extended to detect 42P18 (bare `$N` in `jsonb_build_object` variadic-any calls) (MINOR)** ([GitHub #610](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/610))

- **The gap:** `lint_sql_param_types.py` only detected C42883 patterns (asymmetric `col::text = $N` casts and integer literals vs TEXT columns). It did not detect bare `$N` parameters used inside variadic-any functions (`jsonb_build_object`, `json_build_object`, `format`, `row`) where PostgreSQL cannot infer the parameter type at prepare time, producing `ERROR 42P18: could not determine data type of parameter $N` at runtime — the same class of error that produced ISS-0120/GH-383.
- **The fix:** four additions to `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py`: a `VARIADIC_ANY_FUNCS` frozenset, a `FUNC_CALL_RE` pattern matching variadic-any function calls in SQL strings, a `BARE_PARAM_IN_VARIADIC_RE` pattern matching uncast `$N` inside those calls, a `_extract_paren_block()` helper that handles nested parentheses, and a `lint_variadic_params()` function integrated into `lint_file()` as a new MAJOR-severity rule. Bare `$N` inside these functions now emits `[MAJOR] 42P18: bare parameter $N in jsonb_build_object call (needs explicit ::type cast)`.
- **Verified:** `python3 tools/lint_sql_param_types.py src tests` exits 0 with 0 BLOCKER/0 MAJOR/0 MINOR on the current codebase (the one known occurrence — `$2` in `markRestoredOrphanInTx` — was already fixed by ISS-0120/GH-383). Synthetic detection test confirmed: a temporary file containing a bare `$2` in `jsonb_build_object` emits `[MAJOR]`, and `$2::text` does not. `zig build test` exits 0 (all suites passed). Test report: `tests/reports/report-20260809-WF03-GH610-20260809.yaml`.
- **No requirement IDs involved** — linter tooling improvement, not a requirement change. Design: `src/design/iss0632-gh610-lint-42p18.md`.

**ISS-0120 — EXP-402 `markRestoredOrphanInTx` 42P18 error fixed by adding `::text` cast to SQL parameter `$2` (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #383](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/383))

- **The bug:** `markRestoredOrphanInTx` in `src/engine/reconstruction.zig` used `$2` in a `jsonb_build_object(...)` call where PostgreSQL could not infer the parameter's data type, producing `ERROR 42P18: could not determine data type of parameter $2` and causing TC-EXP-402-02 to fail at runtime.
- **The fix:** changed `$2` to `$2::text` in the `jsonb_build_object` SQL expression to supply an explicit cast, removing the ambiguity. No logic change — parameter value and usage are identical; only the type hint is added.
- **Verified:** `zig build` exit 0, `zig build test` exit 0, `python3 tools/lint_sql_param_types.py src tests` 0 BLOCKER/0 MAJOR/0 MINOR. TC-EXP-402-01 and TC-EXP-402-02 both pass with no 42P18 errors. Test report: `tests/reports/report-20260809-WF03-GH383-20260809.yaml`. Design: `src/design/iss0120-gh383-exp402-param-cast.md`.
- **No requirement IDs involved** — SQL parameter typing fix, not a requirement change.

**ISS-0630 — 9 `-- scope: public` migrations wrote unqualified tables, breaking fresh-database bootstrap at 011 (BLOCKER)** ([GitHub #605](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/605))

- **The bug:** commit `a2a8c68` (ISS-0185 / GH-518, merged 2026-08-08) added `-- scope: public` headers to 9 numeric migration files as part of a dual-schema-shadow cleanup, but did not qualify those files' `CREATE TABLE` / `INSERT INTO` statements with `public.`. The scope-mismatch guard (`declaresUnqualifiedTableWork()` in `src/db/migrations.zig`, built by ISS-0604/GH-470 to catch exactly this defect class) correctly rejects any fresh apply of these files: `zig build migrate` against a genuinely empty database failed at `migrations/011_webhook_subscriptions.sql`, and pre-seeding past 011 reproduced the identical error at the next affected file, `022_obs06_alerting_state.sql` — confirming a systemic pattern across all 9 files.
- **Why it went unnoticed:** every workspace's `db_test` container has run continuously since 2026-08-06, before `a2a8c68` merged on 2026-08-08, so `public.schema_migrations` already recorded all 9 files as applied before the defect entered their content. `zig build migrate` skips already-applied files, so the guard never evaluated the broken content on any long-lived shared database — only a genuinely fresh database triggers it. This is the same `shared_db_container_trap` pattern already documented in `docs/anti-patterns.md`.
- **The fix:** all 9 migration files edited in place to qualify every `CREATE TABLE` / `ALTER TABLE` / `INSERT INTO` statement with `public.` — no corrective/backfill migration was needed, since the statements are already idempotent: `migrations/011_webhook_subscriptions.sql`, `022_obs06_alerting_state.sql`, `038_oidc_claim_mapping_config.sql`, `039_jit_provisioning_config.sql`, `041_oidc15_realm_deletion_tracker.sql`, `042_oidc16_26_agent_lifecycle_foundations.sql`, `049_repository_service_catalog.sql`, `056_onboarding_registry.sql`, `094_entity_subsystem.sql`.
- **Self-caught correction in `094_entity_subsystem.sql` — one of the 9 files mixed genuinely-public and genuinely-per-tenant work under a single file-level scope header.** The initial pass qualified this file's `event_type_registry` seed insert with `public.` alongside its three genuinely-public entity tables, but `event_type_registry` is one of the 21 designated per-tenant business tables (`tools/lint_migration_schema.py`'s `BUSINESS_TABLES` / TNT-02) that must resolve through `search_path`, never through an explicit `public.` qualifier — the PR's own `Source linters` CI job (`M001` rule) caught this immediately. Corrected by reverting `event_type_registry` to unqualified and changing the file's header from `-- scope: public` to `-- scope: all_schemas` (this file's true scope: three idempotent `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.*` statements safe to re-run on both passes, plus one seed insert that genuinely needs the per-tenant pass). Verified end-to-end against a fresh database: the three entity tables land in `public`, `event_type_registry`'s three seed rows land correctly in the per-tenant schema (`tenant_default`), and `zig build migrate` remains exit 0/115-applied with zero scope-mismatch errors.
- **Regression gate added — this is the second occurrence of this defect class** (see ISS-0603/GH-467 and ISS-0604/GH-470, which built the scope-mismatch guard itself after the first occurrence). A new `fresh_database_migration` CI job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs `zig build migrate` against a genuinely empty PostgreSQL 15 service container on every PR, judged by exit code only, so a future `-- scope: public` file with unqualified statements fails CI immediately instead of lying dormant on every long-lived shared database.
- **Verified independently by both BACKEND-DEV and ORCH** against real throwaway databases (not the shared `db_test` container): `zig build migrate` against a fresh, empty database now exits 0 and applies all 115 migrations, including all 9 previously-failing files, with no scope-mismatch error; a second run against the same now-seeded database confirmed idempotency (`No new migrations to apply`). The existing long-lived `bpm_test` database was also confirmed to still exit 0 with no new migrations applied, proving the edits are a no-op where the files were already ledger-recorded. `zig build` and `zig build test-lua` both exit 0.
- **Discovered while investigating, but does not resolve, GH-482/ISS-0150.** GH-482's original filed premise (a missing per-tenant `schema_migrations` ledger) was independently re-confirmed stale/disproven by this run — already fixed by prior work (PR #519 and the ISS-0182–0185/GH-515–518 siblings). Re-attempting GH-482's mandated fresh-database re-measurement (per `src/design/iss0150-gh482-test-integration-svc-closeout.md`) is what surfaced this defect: the measurement itself could not run on a fresh database until ISS-0630 was fixed. **GH-482/ISS-0150 remains OPEN**, explicitly blocked on this fix until now and unblocked as of this run — a separate follow-up run is required to complete the fresh-database re-measurement of `test-integration-svc` and classify its 35 residual failing test names. A related MINOR flake, `db_integration_test.TC-DB-02-04` (connection-pool boundary test, intermittent under concurrent-binary contention), was split out as its own issue, [ISS-0631/GH-606](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/606), and left open — unrelated root cause, out of scope for this run.
- **No requirement IDs involved** — production migration-hygiene fix, not a requirement change. Design: `src/design/iss0630-migration-scope-qualification-fix.md`.

**ISS-0131 — C40P01 deadlock at TC-TNT-01-01 (`provisionTenantSchema` concurrent migration race) verified fixed (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #424](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/424))

- **Root cause:** `provisionTenantSchema()` acquired a separate pool connection for each of Steps 2, 4, 6, and 6a, allowing up to five independent PostgreSQL connections per logical provisioning call under full-suite concurrency. The advisory lock scope closed before Step 2 began (ISS-0612 diagnosis), leaving the inter-step window unserialised and creating an AccessShareLock / ShareLock cycle with concurrent binaries.
- **Fix (ISS-0612 / GH-556, commit 2dc07ddf, 2026-08-07):** `provisionTenantSchema()` now acquires one pool connection (`lock_conn`) and holds it — together with the session-scoped advisory lock — across all of Steps 2–6a. Steps 2, 4, 6, 6a reuse `lock_conn` sequentially; only `runForSchema` (Step 5) uses its own internal connection (required by signature; safe because the lock stays held on `lock_conn`). Pool.release() gains `pg_advisory_unlock_all()` as defence-in-depth.
- **Verification (WF03-GH424-20260809, 2026-08-09):** `zig build test-integration` run with the ISS-0612 fix already merged to `main`. TC-TNT-01-01 PASSED with zero C40P01 occurrences. ISS-0129 regression tests RT-3/RT-4/RT-5 (`iss0129_migration_run_advisory_lock_test.zig`) PASSED — no regression introduced.

**ISS-0624 — LUA-11 (variable read/write) and LUA-13 (structured logging) restored to a real, runnable implementation (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #591](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/591))

- **The gap this closes:** since the ISS-0153 / GH-471 downgrade, both requirements' host-API entry points correctly gated on capability and validated argument types, but neither touched real production state. `read_variable` always returned `nil` regardless of any prior `write_variable` call, and there was no pending-writes buffer or instance-state field anywhere on `ExecutionContext`. `platform.log` gated correctly and then did nothing — `StructuredLogger.log()` was fully implemented but had zero callers reachable from `ExecutionContext` or `host_api`.
- **LUA-11 — real instance-state model with atomic commit-on-success / discard-on-failure semantics.** `ExecutionContext` (`src/lua/executor.zig`) gains a `pending_writes` staging map: `write_variable` stages into it rather than mutating instance state directly, `read_variable` reads through the staged value (read-after-write within the same script sees its own uncommitted write), and `executeSource` applies the staged map to `InstanceState` atomically only on script success — a `platform.fail` call or an uncaught runtime error discards the entire staged map, leaving instance state exactly as it was before the script ran. Per-instance isolation holds: two instances' writes land only in their own `InstanceState`, never cross-contaminating.
- **LUA-13 — structured logging now genuinely wired.** `ExecutionContext` gains a `trace_id` field and a `structured_logger` reference; `platform.log` (`src/lua/host_api/log.zig`) now constructs a real `StructuredLogEntry` (level, message, context table, instance ID, actor ID, trace ID) and calls the real `StructuredLogger` instead of pushing `nil`. A `null` `structured_logger` (the default on every pre-existing construction site) is a fail-open no-op, so this is additive — no existing caller's behaviour changes unless it opts in.
- **21 new test cases** (`src/lua/iss0624_lua_11_13_test.zig`, wired via `src/lua_test_root.zig`): 12 for LUA-11 (commit-on-success, discard-on-failure via both `platform.fail` and runtime error, read-after-write, table values, nil-drop-on-write, cross-instance isolation, capability-denied-without-grant, safe-no-op on an all-defaults context) and 9 for LUA-13 (level/message/context/instance/actor/trace capture, all four log levels, unrecognised-level error, capability gate, per-script trace isolation, invocation ordering, fail-open default). All 21 passed in every one of 6 consecutive clean `zig build test-lua` runs (112/112 each).
- **Regression fix surfaced during implementation:** the shared `runWithGrants` test helper in `src/lua/execution_test.zig` and `src/lua/capability_enforcement_test.zig` previously pointed every test at one shared `dummy_instance_state` singleton. Once `write_variable` started committing real data, a write from one test survived into the next test's fresh `std.testing.allocator` instance and corrupted its DebugAllocator bucket bookkeeping. Both helpers now construct a real, per-call `InstanceState` freed via `defer state.deinit()`.
- **Incidental merge-conflict reconciliation — two independent fixes for the same flaky test, combined rather than picked.** This branch was cut before GH-600/ISS-0629 merged to `main`. Both branches independently hit and fixed the same flake in `TC-LUA-10-03` (the host-external watchdog stopping a tight loop with the in-VM timeout hook disabled) with two different, complementary root causes: this branch's own diagnosis was that the tight loop's iteration count (200M) was too low for the JIT to reliably exceed the wall-clock deadline before completing, requiring a bump to 2B iterations; GH-600/ISS-0629's diagnosis was a scheduling race against the watchdog thread's poll interval, requiring a yield-spin retry loop. Neither fix alone was sufficient for both failure modes, so both were kept and verified together: 4 clean `zig build test-lua` runs during reconciliation plus TEST-RUNNER's own 6 runs (10 total), zero flakes on `TC-LUA-10-03` across all of them.
- **Verification:** `zig build test` exit 0 (full suite, 273 test-bearing files). `zig build test-lua` clean across 6 independent runs, 112/112 every time. TEST-RUNNER report: `tests/reports/report-2026-08-09-WF03-GH591-20260809.yaml`. ORCH independently mutation-tested the atomic-commit logic by hand (disabled it, confirmed 6 tests genuinely fail, restored the exact original, confirmed a clean rebuild).
- **LUA-11 and LUA-13 restored to RELEASED.**

**ISS-0629 — `TC-LUA-10-03` intermittently flaky watchdog-timing assertion in `src/lua/limiter_wiring_test.zig` (MINOR)** ([GitHub #600](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/600))

- **The flake:** the test read `watchdog_state.hasFired()` immediately after `lua_pcall` returned, with a zero-tolerance assertion and no allowance for the LUA-10 watchdog thread's own ~10ms poll interval (`timeout.zig`'s `POLL_INTERVAL_NS`) plus ordinary OS scheduling delay before the watchdog thread got to run and observe its already-past deadline — a race between the test's post-hoc read and the watchdog's independent polling schedule, not a defect in the watchdog mechanism itself.
- **The fix:** the single zero-tolerance assertion is replaced with a bounded 1000ms yield-spin retry loop (`std.Thread.yield()` against `milliTimestamp()`, both already used elsewhere in the same test file) that gives the watchdog thread a fair window to catch up to a deadline that has, by construction, already passed. No production file is touched (`src/lua/timeout.zig` and the public `WatchdogState`/`WatchdogHandle` surface are unchanged); no failure mode the original assertion caught is masked — a genuinely non-firing watchdog still fails the test after the retry window elapses.
- **Verified:** 19 consecutive clean `zig build test-lua` runs post-fix across three independent agents/checks (BACKEND-DEV 8, ORCH 3, TEST-RUNNER 8), against a pre-fix failure rate of roughly 40-67% observed in two independent pre-fix samples.
- **No requirement IDs involved** — test-only fix, not a requirement change. Design: `src/design/iss0629-tc-lua-10-03-flake-fix.md`. Test report: `tests/reports/report-2026-08-09-WF03-GH600-20260809.yaml`.

**ISS-0628 — LUA-16 `ScriptErrorPayload.stack_trace` was always empty for real LuaJIT runtime errors (MINOR)** ([GitHub #595](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/595))

- **The bug:** `executor.zig`'s `lua_pcall(L, 0, 1, 0)` call installed no message handler (`errfunc=0`). `captureStackTrace` ran only *after* `lua_pcall` returned — by which point the erroring Lua call frames had already unwound, so `lua_getstack(level=0)` always returned 0 and `stack_trace` was unconditionally empty for every genuine runtime error.
- **The GH-595 issue text itself was wrong about the fix.** It suggested installing `debug.traceback` as the `errfunc`. That is not reachable: `stdlib.zig`'s `loadSafeStdlib` deliberately never calls `luaopen_debug` (SBX-1 invariant — `debug`, `io`, `os`, `package`, `jit`, `ffi`, `bit`, and `coroutine` are never opened in the sandbox), so `debug.traceback` does not exist as a sandboxed global. The actual fix installs a custom `lua_CFunction` message-handler closure (`errfuncHandler`, via `lua_pushcclosure`) that reuses the same `lua_getstack`/`lua_getinfo` walk `captureStackTrace` already implemented, invoked from *inside* the handler while the failing call frames are still live — before `lua_pcall` unwinds them. The handler writes the captured trace into a new private `LUA_REGISTRYINDEX` key (`STACK_TRACE_KEY` / `readStackTrace` in `host_context.zig`, mirroring the existing `FAILURE_REASON_KEY`/`readExplicitFailure` pattern) and passes the original error value through unchanged.
- **Push ordering:** the handler closure is pushed via `lua_pushcclosure` *before* the compiled chunk (`luaL_loadbuffer`'s result) is pushed for calling; its absolute stack index is captured immediately via `lua_gettop` and passed as `lua_pcall`'s 4th argument, replacing the previous hardcoded `0`.
- **Two additional defects found and fixed during implementation, beyond the original design:**
  1. **`lua_getinfo` selector was wrong for function names.** The existing selector string `"Sl"` (source + current line) does not populate the `name`/`namewhat` fields LuaJIT needs to report a frame's function name — the walk silently produced frames with no name. Fixed to `"Sln"`, which adds the `n` selector required to populate names.
  2. **Proper tail calls collapse intermediate stack frames.** Lua 5.1/LuaJIT implement proper tail calls: a function called in tail position does not grow the call stack, so an intermediate frame in a `f3 -> f2 -> f1` tail-call chain becomes invisible to `lua_getstack`/`lua_getinfo` entirely — not merely mislabeled. Both integration test fixtures (`tests/integration/iss0625_lua_12_15_16_test.zig`) had to be rewritten from tail-call to non-tail-call recursion (e.g. wrapping the recursive call so its result participates in a further expression) so the intermediate frames stay live and visible for the trace walk to capture.
- **Test changes:** `TC-ISS-0625-LUA-16-int-01`'s final assertion flipped from `stack_trace.len == 0` to `> 0`, with a new content assertion that the trace contains the erroring function's name; its stale title/comments (previously citing `debug.traceback` as "the proper fix" and referencing the wrong issue number) corrected. New `TC-ISS-0628-LUA-16-stacktrace-01` added, using a distinct non-shared fixture, asserting the trace contains the named failing function and has at least as many frame markers as the chain's nesting depth. `TC-ISS-0625-LUA-16-02` (the existing idle-stack unit test, which legitimately expects an empty trace when there is no active call) was left unchanged, as required.
- **Verified by mutation test, twice independently** (once by BACKEND-DEV during implementation, once by ORCH during re-verification): reverting `lua_pcall`'s 4th argument to the literal `0` makes both `TC-ISS-0625-LUA-16-int-01` and the new `TC-ISS-0628-LUA-16-stacktrace-01` genuinely fail on an empty `stack_trace`, exactly as predicted; restoring the fix returns both to green.
- **Test results:** `zig build test` (full unit suite) exit 0; `zig build test-lua` 91/91 (one known, already-tracked, pre-existing flake in `TC-LUA-10-03` — see ISS-0629/GH-600 below — unrelated to this fix, observed intermittently and not a new failure); `zig build test-integration-iss0625` 10/10, including both LUA-16 stack-trace tests. No regression in other `host_context.zig`/`executor.zig` consumers (limiter wiring, capability enforcement, execution tests, stdlib, host API).
- **Incidental fix (Unblock-Everything):** a pre-existing, unrelated compile blocker in `tests/integration/iss0625_lua_12_15_16_test.zig` (a `createSandboxedState` call-signature mismatch that prevented the whole integration test file from compiling) was fixed via a new `StateProbe` wrapper so this run's own acceptance tests could run at all.
- **Incidental discovery (filed and forwarded, not fixed here):** ISS-0629 / [GitHub #600](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/600) (MINOR) — `TC-LUA-10-03` (`src/lua/limiter_wiring_test.zig`) is an intermittently flaky watchdog-timing test, unrelated to LUA-16. Added to the global queue for its own future run.
- **Requirement status:** LUA-16 was already `RELEASED` (as part of the GH-592/ISS-0625 batch — see the 2026-08-08 entry below); this run fixes a defect found in that release rather than releasing it for the first time. `implemented_in` extended to include `src/lua/host_context.zig`.
- **Design:** `src/design/iss0628-lua16-errfunc-stacktrace.md`. Test report: `tests/reports/report-20260809-WF03-GH595-20260809.yaml`.

**ISS-0631 — TC-DB-02-04 pool-boundary test no longer flakes under concurrent-binary connection pressure (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #606](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/606))

- **Root cause:** `Pool.init()` opens all `pool_size` connections eagerly. TC-DB-02-04 called it with `pool_size=200` against a `db_test` PostgreSQL instance capped at `max_connections=250`. When `zig build test-integration` runs all binaries in parallel, 20+ concurrent test binaries hold 1–5 connections each, exhausting the free-slot budget before TC-DB-02-04's 200-connection pool can open completely — PostgreSQL rejects with "too many connections" and `Pool.init` returns `ConnectionFailed` instead of succeeding, making the test non-deterministic.
- **Fix (`tests/integration/db_integration_test.zig`):** TC-DB-02-04 now queries `pg_stat_activity` and `SHOW max_connections` via a lightweight one-shot connection before calling `Pool.init`. It computes `safe_pool_size = min(200, max_conn - current_conn - 5)` (runtime probe, not a hardcoded constant). If `safe_pool_size < 2` the upper-boundary live test is skipped with a diagnostic; otherwise `safe_pool_size` is used for the `Pool.init` call. The `pool_size=201 → InvalidPoolSize` boundary check (no network call) is unchanged. The `safe_pool_size` variable is typed `u8` to avoid integer-overflow issues on platforms where the subtraction could wrap.
- **Fix (`docker-compose.yml`):** `db_test` service `max_connections` raised from 250 to 500. This alone is not sufficient (concurrent demand can still exceed any fixed ceiling in extreme load), but it materially reduces flake frequency in practice.
- **Verified:** TC-DB-02-04 passed in all 5 consecutive `zig build test-integration` runs post-fix (TEST-RUNNER acceptance criterion). `zig build test` exit 0. No regression in other pool or database tests.
- **Design:** `src/design/iss0631-tc-db-02-04-pool-boundary-fix.md`. Test report: `tests/reports/report-2026-08-09-WF03-GH606-20260809.yaml`.

### Added

**ISS-0627 — permanent acknowledgment mechanism for the H003/H004/H005/H009 subset of `lint_handoffs.py`'s historical bookkeeping debt (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #596](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/596) — narrowed scope, see note below)

- **The problem this solves:** after ISS-0626's mechanical repair (previous entry below), `tools/lint_handoffs.py` still reported 250 BLOCKER + 110 MAJOR + 1 MINOR findings, none of them fixable by writing a plausible value — H003 (148, `completed_at` precedes `started_at` with no whole-hour-offset signature), H004 (34, `started_at` precedes `created_at`), H005 (5, status `COMPLETED` with `completed_at` never set), and H009 (38, a PENDING/IN_PROGRESS step has later COMPLETED steps in the same run — the pipeline visibly advanced past a step it never closed) all share one property: no field anywhere in the affected file recovers the true value. Guessing one would fabricate audit-trail evidence, which CLAUDE.md's own "a wrong-guess fix is worse than an honestly-flagged gap" principle forbids — but leaving these 225 findings permanently unacknowledged meant every future PR touching `handoffs/` would re-trip the same historical BLOCKER/MAJOR gate forever, with no way to tell a genuinely new defect from an already-reviewed historical one.
- **What was added — a third outcome, not a suppression.** `tools/lint_handoffs.py` gained an `ACKNOWLEDGED` bucket: a finding matched against `tools/lint_handoffs.baseline.json` (new file, 225 dated, reasoned, attributed entries — 148 H003 + 34 H004 + 5 H005 + 38 H009) is excluded from the BLOCKER/MAJOR exit-code gate but is still individually printed on every run under its own `[ACKNOWLEDGED]` label, with the finding's own message, the shared per-code reason text, and who/when it was accepted. Nothing is hidden or deleted from the report. The baseline was generated once by a new reviewed script, `tools/generate_lint_handoffs_baseline.py`, which reuses `lint_handoffs.py`'s own detection functions rather than reimplementing them, so the baseline's keys are guaranteed to be built the same way the linter itself builds them.
- **H009's key needed more than message text.** A plain `severity|code|path|message` key (the scheme H003/H004/H005 use safely, since their messages embed the actual timestamp values) is provably insufficient for H009: the message only embeds a *count* of later-completed steps, not *which* steps, and this repo's own history shows run directories have had step files added/removed/reorganized over time — two genuinely different orphan situations (different bypassing steps) can produce byte-identical H009 message text. `tools/lint_handoffs.baseline.json`'s H009 keys therefore append a sorted fingerprint of the actual later-completed file paths, so a new orphan situation with the same count but different membership is correctly treated as unacknowledged. Verified with a dedicated differential test against the real corpus.
- **A new `--no-baseline` flag** (mirroring `tools/lint_test_tenant_provisioning.py`'s existing flag) disables the acknowledgment filter entirely, for auditing the mechanism itself or reviewing the full historical debt on demand.
- **This adds a mechanism; it does not weaken detection.** `tools/lint_handoffs.py --no-baseline` is byte-for-byte unchanged from before this run: still 250 BLOCKER + 110 MAJOR + 1 MINOR across the full corpus. `--changed` mode (GH-594's merge-base diff logic) is untouched and composes independently — the two answer different questions ("did this branch introduce this finding" vs. "has this specific historical finding been reviewed and permanently accepted") and are verified to compose correctly rather than being merged into one mechanism.
- **Verified counts, independently re-measured by ORCH:**

  | Mode | BLOCKER | MAJOR | MINOR | ACKNOWLEDGED |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Default (baseline active) | 63 | 72 | 1 | 225 |
  | `--no-baseline` | 250 | 110 | 1 | — (mechanism disabled) |
  | `--changed` (this run's own branch) | 0 | 0 | 0 | — |

- **Scope note — GH-596/ISS-0627 originally covered 292 findings across 6 remaining categories (H001-structural, H003, H004, H005, H006-CONDITIONAL, H007, H009, H013). This run resolves the mechanism for 4 of them (H003/H004/H005/H009 = 225 findings) only.** H013 (53 findings — needs an explicit policy decision on whether whole-hour timezone-offset correction counts as recoverable or must join this same acknowledgment mechanism), H001-structural (~4 files — per-file bracket/delimiter judgment, not a class of finding a shared mechanism can safely resolve), H006-CONDITIONAL (1 finding — needs an explicit enum-mapping decision), and H007 (69 findings across ~10 files — a schema-migration/backward-compat decision) remain open, tracked in the same `docs/issues/ISS-0627.json` / GitHub #596, exactly as that issue's own acceptance criteria anticipated ("this is deliberately NOT a single run"). GH-596 stays open, retitled to reflect only this remaining scope.
- **No requirement IDs involved** — pure tooling/bookkeeping, not a `BPM_Platform_Functional_Requirements.md` item.

## [Unreleased] — 2026-08-08

### Fixed

**ISS-0626 — mechanical repair of the safe subset of `lint_handoffs.py`'s historical bookkeeping debt across `handoffs/` (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #594](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/594))

- **The gap this closes:** the 2026-08-05 pipeline audit already documented in this file's own `CLAUDE.md` (log append rate 44%→0.4%, registry coverage 3.4%, timestamp validity 8.6%) had never been remediated — it had only been measured. `python3 tools/lint_handoffs.py` was reporting 263 BLOCKER + 195 MAJOR + 1 MINOR findings (459 total) across 2270 historical handoff files, none of them caused by any currently-active run.
- **Triage split the debt into two issues, not one blind repair.** ISSUE-FIXER re-verified every finding category by hand against `tools/lint_handoffs.py`'s actual check logic before deciding what could be fixed without inventing data:
  - **H002** (9 files, all in `WF02-iss105-token-model-schema-20260611`) — `result` was a JSON-string-encoded object instead of an object; `json.loads()` round-trips it cleanly. Safe, purely additive.
  - **H008** (88 files) — UTF-8 BOM prefix; stripping it is a byte-level operation that does not change content. Safe.
  - **H010** (1868 of 2270 handoffs missing from `handoffs/registry.json`, 82.3%) — each missing handoff already carries its own `created_at`/`status` in its own file; reconciliation only adds registry entries sourced from the handoff's own recorded fields, never invents data. Safe.
  - **H006** (3 files) — `FAILED`→`FAIL` and `PARTIAL_PASS`→`PARTIAL` are unambiguous renames onto the legal `result.status` enum; folded into the same script. The third file's value, `CONDITIONAL`, has no unambiguous 1:1 mapping and was left untouched.
  - Everything else — H001's structural-typo subset, H003, H004, H005, H007, H009, H013 (292 of the original 459 findings) — has no source anywhere in the affected file that recovers the true value (e.g. an inverted `completed_at`/`started_at` pair with no recoverable original timestamp). Fixing these would mean fabricating audit-trail data, which CLAUDE.md's own "a wrong-guess fix to an audit trail is worse than an honestly-flagged gap" principle rules out. **Deliberately split out to `docs/issues/ISS-0627.json` ([GitHub #596](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/596)) for case-by-case future handling** — this is a second phase, not incomplete work from this run.
- **Fix:** `tools/repair_handoff_bookkeeping.py` added, covering H002 unwrap, H008 BOM-strip, H010 additive registry backfill, and H006's two unambiguous renames. Every write is verify-before-commit: a repaired file is only kept if it re-parses and a byte-diff shows only the targeted bytes changed; anything that fails that check is rolled back automatically rather than left in a half-fixed state. Applied against the real `handoffs/` corpus (not just a dry run).
- **Verified counts, independently re-measured twice** (once by the implementing agent's own selftest against synthetic fixtures, 6/6 mutation-checkable specs passed; once by ORCH re-running `python3 tools/lint_handoffs.py` cold against the post-fix corpus):

  | Check | Before | After | Note |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | H002 | 9 | 0 | fully resolved |
  | H008 | 88 | 3 | 3 files are BOM+structural-typo overlap — correctly unfixable by a BOM-only strip, safely rolled back rather than partially repaired |
  | H010 | 1868 missing (82.3%) | 83 missing (3.7%) | remaining 83 are legitimately skipped per no-invented-data rules |
  | H006 | 3 | 1 | the 1 remaining `CONDITIONAL` value is intentionally retained pending ISS-0627 |
  | H001 | 11 | 9 | 2 genuinely fixed (encoding corruption); the other 9 are structural typos, out of scope for this issue |
  | **Total BLOCKER** | **263** | **250** | |
  | **Total MAJOR** | **195** | **110** | |

- **`handoffs/registry.json` grew from 305 to 2094 entries** and was confirmed to remain valid JSON after the backfill.
- **Follow-up tracked separately:** `docs/issues/ISS-0627.json` / [GitHub #596](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/596) covers the remaining 250 BLOCKER + 110 MAJOR findings (H001-structural, H003, H004, H005, H006-CONDITIONAL, H007, H009, H013) — each needs either a permanent, dated acknowledgment mechanism (for timestamp/status categories with no recoverable true value) or per-file human/CODE-DESIGNER-reviewed judgment (for the small structural-typo and schema-migration subsets). This is a deliberate, planned second phase, following the same phased-issue pattern already used for GH-495 → ISS-0624/ISS-0625.
- **No requirement IDs involved** — this is pure tooling/bookkeeping repair, not a `BPM_Platform_Functional_Requirements.md` item.
- **A note on this PR's diff size:** touches roughly 2000 individual `handoffs/*/*.json` files. Every change is mechanical (BOM strip, JSON-string-to-object unwrap, additive registry entry, or an unambiguous enum rename) and was produced and verified by `tools/repair_handoff_bookkeeping.py`, not by hand-editing — the script's own selftest is the review artefact, not a file-by-file human read of ~2000 historical JSON files.
- **Incidental fix (Unblock-Everything), found by this run's own PR check:** CI's `Pipeline bookkeeping` job (`python3 tools/lint_handoffs.py --changed`) failed on this PR — not because the repair introduced a new defect, but because touching a file for one reason (e.g. an H008 BOM strip) pulled that file into `--changed`'s *file-level* scope, exposing its separate, pre-existing, already-tracked-in-ISS-0627 findings (H003/H004/H007/H009/H013) as if they were new. `tools/lint_handoffs.py` now compares each touched file's findings against its merge-base copy, per finding code, and reports only codes that are genuinely new on the branch (`preexisting_file_finding_codes` for single-file checks, `preexisting_run_finding_codes` for the run-level H009/H013 checks). This is not a suppression list and invents no data — it is a same-file, same-code presence check computed fresh from git history on every run, so a finding that gets genuinely fixed later stops being "pre-existing" the moment someone's branch fixes it. Verified: full-corpus (non-`--changed`) totals are unchanged at 250 BLOCKER/110 MAJOR/1 MINOR (confirming the underlying checks were not weakened); `--changed` on this branch now reports 0 BLOCKER/0 MAJOR/1 MINOR (the 1 MINOR is the same legitimate, documented H010 registry gap); a direct unit check against `_lint_parsed_handoff` confirms a genuinely new H003 is still detected and not filtered.

**ISS-0169 tranche 2 — LUA-08 (instruction limit), LUA-09 (memory limit), LUA-10 (wall-clock timeout) wired into the executor for the first time; script code could previously erase or forge its own resource limiter (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #495](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/495) — narrowed scope, see note below)

**ISS-0625 — LUA-12 (service call), LUA-15 (platform.fail), LUA-16 (runtime error capture) production wiring + regression + integration tests (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #592](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/592))

- **Three MUST requirements restored to RELEASED** after the ISS-0153 / GH-471 downgrade flagged that the prior LUA-06..16 RELEASED stamp was not backed by anything runnable. This batch (sibling to ISS-0624 / GH-591 which closed LUA-11/13) delivers the runtime behaviour the acceptance criteria text actually requires.
  - **LUA-12 (service call):** `ExecutionContext` now owns a `*const ServiceCatalog` field (`src/lua/host_context.zig`); `platform.call_service` validates `svc_id` against the catalog before any HTTP path, returns a structured `{status_code, headers, body}` Lua table on success (single-table return shape, replacing the old `(string, number)` tuple), and a structured error table on validation/network failure. `src/lua/service_catalog.zig` `lookup()` now has real callers (zero before this run).
  - **LUA-15 (structured failure):** `platform.fail` no longer writes `__failure_reason__` / `__failure_details__` / `__explicit_failure__` as script-writable `_G` globals — those are migrated to private keys under `LUA_REGISTRYINDEX` following the established `host_context.zig` pattern (`bpm.failure_reason`, `bpm.failure_details`, `bpm.explicit_failure`). `executor.executeSource` distinguishes `.ExplicitFailure` from `.RuntimeError` on the failed-`pcall` branch using a non-script-reachable registry read, so an explicit failure is now observationally distinct from any other runtime error.
  - **LUA-16 (runtime error capture):** `executor.captureStackTrace` (lua_getstack/lua_getinfo, per design §21.2) and `events.ScriptErrorPayload` construction are wired on the `.RuntimeError` branch. `ScriptErrorPayload` is constructed **only** when its required fields can be populated (LUA-08 instruction-count and LUA-09 memory-peak sources); a zero-valued `instruction_count` in a payload is a worse test outcome than missing, so the conditional keeps the surface honest.
- **Three commits, one fix.** `c5a12c9d` (production wiring: ExecutionContext.service_catalog, registry-channel failure, captureStackTrace scaffold); `d0e74f73` (regression tests + ServiceCatalog `endpoint_url` leak fix); `52c85455` (9 integration tests + production segfault fix in `host_context.zig::tableToScriptValue` — `lua_pushnil` had shifted the stack so `lua_next(L, idx)` iterated over nil and crashed in `lj_tab_next`; corrected to `lua_next(L, idx - 1)` with explanatory comment).
- **Verification.** `zig build test`-equivalent unit suite exit 0, `zig build test-integration-iss0625` 9/9 pass (TC-ISS-0625-LUA-12-int-01..02, LUA-15-int-01..04, LUA-16-int-01..03), 0 leaks. Pre-checks (backend services, infrastructure health, env-gate) all PASS. TEST-RUNNER mutation-tested the registry-channel path: forging `__failure_reason__` via `_G` is correctly ignored (TC-ISS-0625-LUA-15-int-03). Two independent CODE-DESIGN-VALIDATOR passes for the Type E design. Test report: `tests/reports/report-2026-08-08-WF03-GH592-20260808.yaml`. Release decision: `docs/status/release-WF03-GH592-2026-08-08.yaml`.
- **Follow-up (filed and queued, out of scope for this run):** ISS-0626 / GH-595 (MINOR) — LUA-16 `ScriptErrorPayload.stack_trace` is always empty on LuaJIT 2.1 because `lua_getstack` returns 0 after `lua_pcall` unwinds call frames; fix is to install `debug.traceback` as the `errfunc` argument to `lua_pcall` so the trace is captured while frames are still alive. Identified and filed by TEST-DESIGNER during Step 4, does not block the LUA-12/15/16 release.

**ISS-0173 — deleted the orphan `src/oidc/jwks.zig` and its dead re-export in `src/main.zig:78` (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #501](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/501))

- **The OIDC-side instance of the ISS-0172 build-wiring blind spot.** `src/oidc/jwks.zig` (538 lines) was orphaned on three independent axes: no `addTest` root reached it, it was not registered as a named module in `build.zig`, and its sole re-export `pub const oidc_jwks = @import("oidc/jwks.zig");` at `src/main.zig:78` had zero reachable callers across `src/` and `web/`. A bare `@import` re-export is **not** proof the file compiles — Zig resolves top-level `var` types lazily and does not semantically analyse function bodies that are never referenced, so the file's 5 hard Zig 0.16 errors (`std.Thread.Mutex` removed; `std.ArrayList(T).init` no member on the unmanaged form; `deinit`/`append` now take an allocator argument) were invisible to `zig build` until someone ran `zig test src/oidc/jwks.zig` directly.
- **State A DELETE chosen over State B (KEEP + RENAME + WIRE).** Workspace caller census confirmed zero callers, so the lowest-risk, lowest-LoC option is to delete both the file and the re-export. State B design (with `std.Io.Mutex` threading and unmanaged `ArrayList` migration) is documented in `src/design/iss0173-gh501-jwks-zig016-fix.md` §4 for any future intent that resurrects ISS-402's token cache.
- **Fix:** `src/oidc/jwks.zig` deleted via `git rm`; `src/main.zig:78` re-export line removed; `tests/unit/iss0173_oidc_jwks_orphan_test.zig` added with 4 regression tests (`TC-ISS-0173-01..04`, per-test UUID isolation) that fail the build if either the file or the re-export returns; `build.zig` gained a `test-iss0173-orphan` step wired into both `test_engine_step` and `test_step`.
- **Three-phase deliberate-mutation verification.** Phase 1 (RED): 4/4 unit tests fail before the fix. Phase 2 (GREEN): all 4 pass after the fix; `zig build` exits 0; `zig build test` 949/1013 (64 pre-existing skipped, 0 failures). Phase 3 (mutation): re-adding the re-export line in a scratch wrapper causes `zig build` to exit non-zero with `unable to load 'jwks.zig': FileNotFound`; reverting restores GREEN. All evidence captured at `scratch/_iss0173_*.log`.
- **No DB, Keycloak, or HTTP impact.** ISS-402's real OIDC token-validation cache is at `src/identity/provider/oidc/jwks_cache.zig` (wired in `build.zig:268`); the deleted `src/oidc/jwks.zig` was a mis-named pre-ISS-402 duplicate with no callers. Zero functional surface lost.
- **Follow-up (filed and forwarded, out of scope for this run):** generalised dead-`pub const X = @import(...)` linter (`tools/lint_dead_reexports.py`) — currently catches only `tests/unit/iss0173_oidc_jwks_orphan_test.zig`'s subsystem-level invariants; the regression test will fail the build if this specific re-export returns, but does not generalise to other potential orphan re-exports in `src/main.zig` / `src/bpm.zig`.
- **Verification:** TEST-RUNNER Step 5 PASS. Release decision: APPROVED. Release record: `docs/status/release-WF03-GH501-20260808-2026-08-08.yaml`. PR (squash-merged with branch deletion).

**ISS-0172 — 8 test roots used a type-only pin pattern that hid real compile errors from CI; fixing it surfaced a third, previously-undiscovered bug (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #500](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/500))

- **Root cause had 3 distinct blind spots, not the 2 originally filed.** A bare `_ = Module.Type;` reference resolves a type's *name* but not its struct/union/enum *field types* (Zig resolves those lazily); `refAllDecls` on a `mod.zig`-style aggregator does not descend into a leaf file's function bodies at all; and `refAllDecls` does not descend into a struct's own *methods* even when called directly on the leaf file, because a method is a declaration of the struct, not of the module. All three were confirmed empirically against this Zig 0.16 install, on both `src/lua_test_root.zig` (single-file shape) and `src/simulation_test_root.zig` (mod.zig-aggregator shape).
- **The 2 originally-filed bugs** (`instruction_limiter.zig`'s discarded `c_int` from `lua_sethook`, `memory_limiter.zig`'s nonexistent `std.Thread.Mutex`) were confirmed already fixed by prior ISS-0169/ISS-0174 work before this run started. **Strengthening the pin pattern surfaced a third, previously-invisible bug of the same class:** `src/lua/structured_logger.zig`'s `StructuredLogger.log()` called `std.io.getStdErr()`, removed in Zig 0.16 — fixed to `std.debug.print`, matching this codebase's established stderr idiom.
- **Fix:** `refAllDecls` is now called directly on every leaf file (never only on a `mod.zig` aggregator), plus a `pinModuleTypes(T)` helper (`_ = @sizeOf(T)` + `refAllDecls(T)` on the type itself) applied consistently across all 8 named test roots (`lua`, `simulation`, `transition`, `oidc`, `oidc_jit`, `config_idp`, `core_modules`, `repository`). `tools/lint_test_wiring.py` gained a new advisory (non-blocking) check flagging type-only pins, validated against the pre-fix git history version of `lua_test_root.zig`. `lua_test_root.zig`'s header comment, which had overclaimed its own prior mutation-verification coverage, was corrected.
- **Verification:** mutation-tested for real, twice independently (once by the implementing agent across both failure shapes on 2 different test roots, once by TEST-RUNNER injecting a fresh error into a third location) — confirmed the strengthened pin genuinely catches real compile errors the old pattern would have silently passed through. `zig build` exits 0; `zig build test` (full unit suite) exits 0 with identical pass/skip counts before and after (965/1029 passed, 64 pre-existing skips, 0 regressions); `zig build test-env-verify` HEALTHY 10/10. PR [#589](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/589).

**ISS-0181 — retired the 184 baselined T010 hardcoded-UUID findings by migrating integration fixtures to `TestHarness.newUuid()` / `newUuidString(allocator)` (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #512](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/512))

- **The collision-class problem ISS-0121/#387 was meant to solve, finally retired.** 110 hardcoded UUID sites across 45 test files were replaced with `TestHarness.newUuid()` (binary UUID → Zig struct field) or `newUuidString(allocator)` (SQL-bound string → heap slice with matching `defer alloc.free(...)`). 51 sites were marked RETAIN with a written reason, classified by `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0181-gh512-diagnosis.yaml` per the GH-512 design's §R1 (definition sentinel = 6), §R2 (platform-admin actor = 13), §R3 (creator/conventional = 20 + doc-identity = 34). 1 additional RETAIN was added in commit `22ac3fc0` (the canonical platform-admin UUID string `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001` appears as a substring-search target inside the regression test's TC-RG-02 — design §R1, not a fixture identity).
- **`tools/lint_test_isolation.baseline.json` T010 BLOCKER count: 184 → 74 (–110).** The remaining 74 are all RETAIN with written reason. Baseline was regenerated in place; the new `regenerated_by` note documents the +1 RETAIN. Per-rule snapshot fixture at `tests/specs/fixtures/gh512-baseline-snapshot.json` locks the post-fix shape (total=115, by_code={T010:74,T020:11,T030:6,T050:22,T060:2}, platform_admin=14).
- **New regression lock:** `tests/integration/gh512_t010_regression_test.zig` runs 3 invariants — TC-RG-01 (T010 BLOCKER count ≤ 74 ceiling), TC-RG-02 (baseline shape matches snapshot), TC-RG-03 (`zig build test` exits 0). All 3 PASS. Wired into `build.zig` as `test-integration-gh512`, gated by the integration-test barrier.
- **One self-introduced regression, fixed and verified.** Commit `c8b79467` (batch 2b — `ext02_webhook_dispatch_test.zig`) introduced `defer allocator.free(unknown_delete.subscription_id_slice)` referencing a non-existent field on `api.routes.webhooks.HandlerResult`. TEST-RUNNER Step 5 caught it on first execution; BACKEND-DEV fixed in commit `06b8b327` (the defer target was changed to a captured local variable that owns the heap allocation). The 14 runtime failures on `test-integration-others-internal` are pre-existing on `origin/main` (zero diff vs main for those files) and are filed-and-forwarded, not regressions caused by this change.
- **Verification:** TEST-RUNNER Step 5 PASS — primary `test-integration-gh512` 3/3, tertiary `zig build test` exit 0, `tools/lint_test_isolation.py` exit 0, `tools/verify_test_env.py` HEALTHY (10/10). Release: `docs/status/release-WF03-GH512-20260808-2026-08-08.yaml`. See `src/design/iss0181-gh512-t010-migration.md` for the 5-batch migration plan and S1/S2 substitution rules.

**ISS-0176 — LUA-07's second acceptance criterion (manifest_hash in the execution audit record) closed; LUA-07 now RELEASED** ([GitHub #504](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/504))

- **The gap this closes:** LUA-07 (MUST, Capability manifest validation) has two acceptance criteria. Criterion 1 (manifest hash binds script source to its declared capabilities) was already satisfied by ISS-0169 tranche 1. Criterion 2 (the manifest hash appears in the execution audit record) was not — `src/lua/` produced `manifest_hash` on `ScriptResult`, but nothing consumed it, so an operator auditing a script execution had no way to tell which script source and capability set actually ran. ISS-0169 correctly stopped at the `src/lua/` boundary and filed this engine-side gap as its own follow-up rather than scope-creeping onto that branch.
- **Fix:** added `executeScriptForAudit` (`src/engine/lua_script_audit.zig`) plus `migrations/095_iss0176_lua_script_execution_audit.sql`, giving the engine a call path that reads `manifest_hash` off `ScriptResult` and persists it to a queryable execution audit record.
- **Scope caveat — read carefully before assuming normal execution is covered:** this is deliberately minimal, human-operator-confirmed wiring, not the full engine integration. `executeScriptForAudit` is real code, but its only caller is the new integration test (`tests/integration/iss0176_lua07_audit_manifest_hash_test.zig`) — **not** the engine's normal `SERVICE_TASK`-with-script process-execution flow, which remains unbuilt (tracked as a future roadmap item, `src/design/lua-integration.md` §25). Scripts do not yet run with audit-hash persistence as part of ordinary process execution; this fix only proves the persistence mechanism itself is correct and queryable. See the `note` field on `LUA-07` in `docs/requirements.yaml` for the same caveat recorded against the requirement.
- **Verification:** two CODE-DESIGN-VALIDATOR passes. TEST-RUNNER independently performed a real mutation test — breaking the persistence write made the new integration test fail, confirming the test is a genuine regression guard rather than a tautology. PR [#588](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/588).
- **Requirement status:** `LUA-07` set to `RELEASED` (both acceptance criteria now evidenced).

**ISS-0178 — no CI job built from a clean checkout, so untracked-but-required build inputs went undetected until someone manually cloned the repo (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #507](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/507))

- **The gap this closes:** every existing build path — local developer machines and the cached CI workspace alike — could satisfy a required build input from a file version control did not actually track, so "the file is present" and "the file is tracked" were indistinguishable from inside CI. Two instances of exactly this class had already reached `main` undetected and were only found by a human running a manual `git clone` (ISS-0175/#503 — `luajit_relver.txt` git-ignored but required; ISS-0177/#506 — path-dependent dynasm escaping, only observable from a differently-named directory). ISS-0178 was the unaddressed third acceptance criterion of ISS-0175's own fix, forwarded as its own run rather than folded in.
- **Fix:** added a new `clean_checkout_lua_build` job to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` that checks out the repository into a fresh location with no restored build cache and no reused workspace, then runs `zig build test-lua` to completion. It runs on every pull request targeting `main`, alongside the existing cached "Build and unit tests" job, so a regression of this class is caught before merge.
- **Verification:** confirmed via mutation testing locally — re-introducing the ISS-0175 defect (re-gitignoring `luajit_relver.txt`) turns the new job red while the existing cached job stays green, proving the job actually exercises the tracked-vs-present distinction it exists to catch. Independently confirmed on PR [#585](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/585)'s own CI run, where the new job executed for real against a genuinely clean checkout and passed (1m9s), alongside all other checks green.

**ISS-0183 — 11 `repository_test.zig` blocks failed on a `content_hash` bytea/uuid type mismatch; fixing it unmasked 4 further pre-existing bugs (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #516](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/516))

- **Primary defect:** fixtures wrote `gen_random_uuid()` into `repository_artifacts.content_hash`, a `bytea` column — PostgreSQL rejected the assignment (C42804). Fixed all 7 sites to write a genuine digest value instead; the first attempt reused `$1` with an added `::text` cast, which broke with C42P08 (`$1` was already inferred `uuid` elsewhere in the same statement) — corrected by adding a dedicated placeholder per query rather than reusing `$1`.
- **Fixing the type mismatch unmasked 4 independent, previously-invisible bugs the transaction rollback had been hiding:** `TC-REPO-04-01` referenced `$2` with only one bound parameter (C42P18 off-by-one); `TC-REPO-06-01` inserted into a non-existent `event_type_name` column (real column `name`) and passed `'1.0'` (text) for an `INTEGER schema_version`; `TC-REPO-05-01`/`05-02` queried `form_schema_registry`, renamed to `repository_form_schemas` by migration 072 months ago (repo-wide rename applied); `TC-REPO-11-02` asserted `rows.len==1` on an `INSERT...SELECT` with no `RETURNING` clause (always 0 rows — added `RETURNING version_id`).
- **`GBL-139` reconciliation migration:** re-verifying `TC-REPO-02-01` found it was not "0 rows" as originally filed — `GBL-138` (ISS-0623's earlier reconciliation this session) had replayed only `GBL-134`'s 11-name shadow-table list, never `GBL-136`'s separate 8-name follow-up list (which includes `repository_artifacts`), so `GBL-136`'s fix had silently reverted for those 8 tables. `migrations/GBL-139_iss0183_reconcile_gbl136_tenant_default_reprovision.sql` re-applies `GBL-136`'s exact 8-table list and safety logic verbatim (idempotent, `RESTRICT`-only). Closed 6 of 8 tables' duplication; `repository_artifacts` and `artifact_versions` remain duplicated by design (live FK, `RESTRICT`-blocked — same documented exception `GBL-136` already carried).
- **Verification:** new dedicated `zig build test-integration-repository` target added (21/21 pass, reproduced twice). Full `zig build` exit 0, no "error set" output. `zig build test` (unit suite) 944/1008 pass, 64 pre-existing skips, 0 fail. `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py src tests` 0 BLOCKER/MAJOR/MINOR. Independently re-verified by TEST-RUNNER (not just ISSUE-FIXER's self-report), including direct confirmation that `GBL-138`'s table list genuinely omitted `GBL-136`'s 8 names. PR [#584](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/584).
- **Incidental finding, correctly out of scope:** `zig build test-integration-exp601` `TC-EXP-601-04` fails on a Postgres check-constraint violation (`instance_waits_kind_check` rejects fixture literal `'sandbox'`) — a different symptom layer of the already-open, already-queued ISS-0622/GH-576; recorded as an `additional_findings` entry on that issue and commented on GH #576, not fixed here.

**ISS-0623 — forensic investigation into shared `bpm_test`'s `tenant_default` schema being fully reprovisioned mid-lineage; confirmed no code defect (MAJOR)** ([GitHub #580](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/580))

- **No in-repo code defect found.** `tenant_default`'s entire 75-row `schema_migrations` ledger was replayed from scratch in a 2.5-second burst at 2026-08-08 08:19:53–08:19:55 UTC, silently resurrecting 18 GLOBAL_REGISTRY shadow tables that GBL-134/GBL-135/GBL-136 had already dropped. Two candidate mechanisms were ruled out with direct evidence: a stray `bpm_drop_tenant_schema(nil-uuid)` call (every call site binds a fresh `tenant_id` immediately before use, so a nil-UUID drop is unreachable) and a stale cross-workspace binary (this workspace's `bpm_test` container is confirmed genuinely separate — different port, different `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` — from the sibling checkout's).
- **Root cause traced to this same workspace's own handoff records.** `WF03-GH566-20260808`'s BACKEND-DEV Step 03 verified GBL-136 via an ad-hoc, unlogged `psql` action against the shared `bpm_test` database while investigating a `BPM_DB_URL`/`BPM_TEST_DB_URL` mixup; that same run's TEST-RUNNER Step 04 handoff self-reports "an accidental full DB drop mid-run while investigating" — a timeline fully consistent with the observed reprovisioning window.
- **Cleanup:** `migrations/GBL-138_iss0623_reconcile_tenant_default_reprovision.sql` re-applies GBL-134's and GBL-135's exact, already-validated DROP logic (same table lists, same `RESTRICT`-only posture) against the resurrected `tenant_default`. Applied to both `bpm_test` and `bpm_dev` (a confirmed idempotent no-op on the latter, which was already clean). `python3 tools/lint_dual_schema_table_names.py` confirmed exit 0 (0 duplicates) against both databases after cleanup.
- **Prevention:** a new `docs/anti-patterns.md` entry documents the underlying risk — an agent verifying a migration against a shared, long-lived database reaching for ad-hoc, unlogged direct `psql` commands instead of the tracked migration tooling — so the same silent-reprovisioning failure mode is recognized faster next time.
- **Verification:** `zig build`, `zig build test`, and CI (`Build and unit tests`, `Source linters`, `Pipeline bookkeeping`, `Platform status`) all green on PR head commit. PR [#583](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/583).

**ISS-0620 — GBL-134 dropped `tenant_default` copies of 4 tables that were actually PER_TENANT canonical, not GLOBAL shadows (BLOCKER, forward-fix)** ([GitHub #573](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/573))

- **A misclassification in the migration that ISS-0185 introduced.** GBL-134's `v_tables` allow-list unconditionally dropped `tenant_default.entity_definitions`, `entity_record_latest`, `entity_type_instances`, and `artifact_activation_groups` as if they were GLOBAL_REGISTRY shadow copies — but `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0185-diagnosis.yaml`'s own `classification_table` places all 4 in the PER_TENANT bucket (canonical home `tenant_default`), the opposite of what GBL-134 assumed. The migration had already executed against the shared `bpm_test` database and workspace r-co-2's `bpm_dev` database before this was discovered (incidentally, while diagnosing the unrelated GH-566/ISS-0617).
- **This is a forward-fix, not a rewrite.** GBL-134 already ran and cannot un-run itself. `migrations/GBL-137_iss0620_recreate_per_tenant_shadows.sql` recreates the 4 tables' structure in `tenant_default` (and future tenant schemas) using `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` / `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` exclusively — additive-only, confirmed idempotent (`zig build migrate` is a genuine no-op on re-run against both affected databases). GBL-134's header comment was corrected in place (22-line addition documenting the misclassification); none of its executable DO block was changed.
- **No data recovery was possible or attempted — a documented negative finding, not a deferred action.** No WAL archiving is configured in this environment (`archive_mode=off`) and no pre-drop backup exists. Both confirmed-affected databases are local development infrastructure only (`localhost:5453/bpm_test`, `localhost:5452/bpm_dev`); no evidence any CI, staging, or production database was ever exposed.
- **Two independent CODE-DESIGN-VALIDATOR passes**, the first a genuine FAIL (missing "Public interface" / "Error taxonomy" sections) followed by a substantive rework that closed the gap.
- **Verification:** `zig build test-env-verify` reported HEALTHY 10/10 (including the C4 schema-baseline check, 112/112 migrations matching the ledger). RELEASE-VALIDATOR independently re-verified all safety properties and triggered CI directly to confirm green at the exact PR head commit before a genuine `pull_request`-triggered run existed; a follow-up run (31251961091) after Step Final's rebase confirmed green again, natively, on the final merged code. See `docs/status/release-WF03-GH573-20260808-2026-08-08.yaml` for full rationale. PR [#581](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/581).
- **Incidental finding, correctly out of scope:** ISS-0623/GH-580 (MAJOR) — `bpm_test`'s `tenant_default` schema was separately reprovisioned by an unrelated mechanism, resurrecting 18 previously-dropped GLOBAL_REGISTRY tables. Confirmed structurally unrelated to the 4 tables this fix concerns; filed and forwarded to the global queue, not fixed here.

**ISS-0617 — exp601 tier-quota fixture wrote to the wrong PostgreSQL schema; quota override silently never applied** ([GitHub #566](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/566))

- **Two compounding defects in `tests/integration/exp601_tier_quota_test.zig`.** An unqualified `INSERT INTO repository_artifacts` resolved to `tenant_default.repository_artifacts` instead of `public.repository_artifacts` under the test harness connection's `search_path` (same class as ISS-0144/GH-454), so the resolver's pool connection — `search_path = public` only — could never join to the fixture row. Separately, several fixture writes went through `harness.conn`'s rolled-back transaction, invisible to the pool connections `quota_policy`/`quota_middleware` actually read through.
- **Fix:** schema-qualified the `repository_artifacts` and `tenant_artifact_activations` INSERTs to `public.*`, and moved fixture writes for public-schema-reachable tables from `harness.conn` to pool-acquired connections. `exp601_tier_quota_test.zig` was also wired into `build.zig` for the first time (`test-integration-exp601`), closing a CI-invisibility gap; an unrelated memory leak in `TC-EXP-601-03` was fixed in the same commit.
- **Verification:** `TC-EXP-601-01`, `03`, `05` now pass, confirming the resolver correctly finds the tenant's tier-profile override (10) instead of the platform default (200000) — the exact symptom ISS-0617 was filed against. `TC-EXP-601-02`/`04` still fail for two separate, pre-existing, out-of-scope reasons (ISS-0622/GH-576, filed and queued; and an untraced `instance_waits` insert error already flagged by the original diagnosis). RELEASE-VALIDATOR APPROVED; see `docs/status/release-WF03-GH566-20260808-2026-08-08.yaml`. PR [#577](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/577).

**ISS-0618 — scheduler silently swallowed lock collisions and transaction failures instead of surfacing them** ([GitHub #567](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/567))

- **Two independent gaps in the same reporting pattern.** `src/scheduler/scheduler.zig`'s `processNextDueTimer()`: (1) its ordinary-timer `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` query returned 0 rows both when no timers were due and when a due timer was locked by another process — indistinguishable, so a lock collision was silently reported as "nothing to do" instead of `skipped_locked`. (2) The `fire_blk` block (added under ISS-303 specifically to prevent uncontrolled error propagation from inflating fired/skipped counters) caught every failure — including a genuine, test-verifiable database constraint violation — and converted it to a plain `.none`, so `SchedulerError.TransactionFailed` was never reachable from this path.
- **Fix 1:** when the locking query returns 0 rows, a second lock-agnostic existence query (same predicate, no `FOR UPDATE`) now distinguishes "genuinely no due timers" from "due timer exists but locked."
- **Fix 2:** only the `appendTimerFiredEventInTx` call site inside `fire_blk` changes — on failure it still rolls back and still calls `handleTimerFireError` (preserving ISS-303's retry/DLQ bookkeeping unchanged), then returns `SchedulerError.TransactionFailed` directly.
