Every other `fire_blk` call site is untouched, deliberately more narrowly scoped than a blanket change would be — the design explicitly reasoned against widening what `TransactionFailed` means for every caller of `pollDueTimers`.
- **A genuine double-claim, correctly handled.** A race in `gh_claim.py` let two workspaces independently diagnose and fix this same issue on the same branch. The competing fix took the blanket-propagation approach the design doc had explicitly reasoned against; this run's more precisely-scoped, already-validated fix was kept after re-confirming (independently, by reading both diffs) that it was the better-scoped one, and after re-verifying neither approach regressed `tests/integration/sch303_timer_dlq_test.zig`. No work was silently lost — the merge preserved the competing workspace's full audit trail under a suffixed path.
- **Also fixed a newly-exposed latent self-deadlock:** `TC-SCH-02-02` held a row lock via `defer`-at-scope-exit while calling `cancelInstance()` (a different connection, same row) before that defer fired — unreachable until this fix made the preceding assertion pass. Fixed by releasing the lock explicitly once its purpose was served, before `cancelInstance()` runs.
- **Verification:** `TC-SCH-02-02`/`TC-SCH-02-03` and the `TC-SCH-303-03`/`04` regression suite each passed across 4 total independent runs (2 by BACKEND-DEV, 2 by TEST-RUNNER), with zero hangs and zero regressions. Design: `src/design/iss0618-scheduler-lock-and-error-signaling.md`.

**ISS-0184 (GH #517) — triaged 93 `zig build test-integration-svc` failures into 11 clusters; fixed the small direct-fix candidate (C3)** ([GitHub #517](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/517))

- **The filed issue's own 62/31-file baseline was stale.** Re-measuring against a freshly migrated database found 93 real failures — the surface had shifted after 10 adjacent GH issues resolved on 2026-08-07. The original issue's 85x C22021 "invalid byte sequence" hypothesis was also a misdiagnosis: those are `audit_chain_utf8_test.zig`'s expected, caught-and-handled internal errors proving its own guard works (all 4 of that file's tests pass).
- **Triaged into 11 clusters**, per the issue's own acceptance criteria ("diagnosed to a named root cause and either fixed or split into its own issue"): 3 reused/reopened existing issues after collision searches (ISS-0094/GH#348, ISS-0112/GH#375, ISS-0100/GH#357 — each queued), 2 already covered by other open/queued issues (ISS-0185, ISS-0183), 4 genuinely new issues filed (ISS-0616–0619/GH#565–568), 1 noted-but-not-filed (2 OS-level socket errors, non-deterministic infra flakiness of an already-repeatedly-fixed class), and 1 direct-fix candidate (C3, fixed in this run).
- **C3 fix:** `tests/integration/event_store_integration_test.zig`'s `TC-ES-03-01` leaked 3 heap allocations per run — `EventRecord.deinit()` already existed and was correct, but the test never called it on either `store.append()` result. Added `defer first.record.deinit(alloc)` / `defer second.record.deinit(alloc)`, changing `first`/`second` from `const` to `var` (required — `deinit` takes a mutable pointer, confirmed via a standalone compile check before committing). A sibling sweep found this same unfreed-`AppendResult.record` gap at all 34 `store.append()` call sites in the file — intentionally left the other 32 out of scope (they don't block this run's own acceptance criteria, per CLAUDE.md's Unblock-Everything vs. adjacent-defect boundary) and flagged them for a future pass rather than silently expanding this one.
- **Verification:** leak count 52 → 49 (−3, exactly matching the 3 targeted allocations), confirmed across 4 independent runs (1 by BACKEND-DEV, 3 by TEST-RUNNER). `TC-ES-03-01` did not appear in any failure output. Design: `src/design/iss0184-c3-event-store-test-leak.md`.
- **Gate maintenance, not a new defect:** `zig build test-env-verify`'s C5 check spuriously failed with 46 "new" BLOCKER findings — `tools/lint_test_isolation.baseline.json`'s line numbers had drifted out of sync with the current file content (unrelated commits shifted lines since the baseline was last regenerated), breaking line-keyed suppression exactly as that file's own regeneration note warns can happen. Resynced the baseline per its documented procedure, verifying 0 content-level change (225 findings both before and after, matched ignoring line number) before committing — the underlying 225 hardcoded UUIDs remain tracked, unfixed debt (ISS-0181/GH #512, already queued for its own run).

**ISS-0185 — 45 business tables duplicated between `public` and `tenant_default` with diverging columns** ([GitHub #518](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/518))

- **The systemic root cause of every recurrent "column does not exist" failure on `tenant_default.tenant` since GBL-119.** A copy of 45 tables was being created in every tenant schema by early non-GBL migrations, and every later GBL-only `ALTER` landed on the `public` copy alone; `TestHarness.search_path = tenant_default,public` then resolved any unqualified reference to the stale `tenant_default` shadow. The two copies had diverged in columns — `public.tenant` had `tenant_type`, `tenant_default.tenant` did not — and the symptoms kept being patched at the call site (ISS-0089, ISS-0126, ISS-0144, ISS-0150 RC-3) without removing the duplication itself.
- **Classification-first design, before any DDL.** `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0185-diagnosis.yaml` enumerates each of the 45 names and assigns it one of three canonical homes: `GLOBAL_REGISTRY` (canonical home = `public`), `PER_TENANT` (canonical home = `tenant_default`), or `HYBRID` (intentionally present in both). The HYBRID allow-list is 9 names whose `public` and `tenant_default` shapes are explicitly distinct by design.
- **Drop the stray copies, not the canonical ones.** Two SQL migrations carry the design, both idempotent and dry-run-guarded:
  - `migrations/GBL-134_iss0185_drop_global_registry_shadows.sql` — iterates `public.tenant` and drops every GLOBAL shadow from each tenant schema (24 names dropped in the canonical run).
  - `migrations/GBL-135_iss0185_drop_per_tenant_shadows.sql` — drops 12 specific per-tenant-only names from `public` (drops them, not the tenant copies).
  - Both migrations use `to_regclass(...)` and `IF EXISTS` guards so they are safe to re-run and a no-op once the schema is already clean.
- **Post-fix regression test.** `tests/integration/iss0185_dual_schema_test.zig` (5 cases, all PASS): public-only, tenant-default-only, HYBRID, intersection-count = 9, and ledger-recording-of-GBL-134/135. Wired in via `build.zig:2006` as `test-integration-iss0185` so it can be run on its own as a hard gate.
- **Linter guards the re-introduction path.** `tools/lint_dual_schema_table_names.py` rejects any new migration that `CREATE TABLE`s a name already present in the other schema (BLOCKER severity). Reachable from CI via `make lint-migrations`; documented in `docs/anti-patterns.md`.
- **Verification — no new failures, branch is strictly better than `main`.** Main baseline (no GBL-134/135 applied) on the same DB: 634 pass, 9 skip, 89 fail, 1 crash (733 total). Branch with the fix: 638 pass, 9 skip, 85 fail, 1 crash (733 total). The branch fixes 4 pre-existing failures and introduces 0. Per `tests/reports/report-20260808-WF03-GH518-20260808.yaml` and `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0185-baseline-comparison.yaml`.
- **Scope boundary — file the recurring silent-shadow anti-pattern as its own ISS entry.** The diagnosis is recorded in `docs/anti-patterns.md` so the same find-the-shadow-call-site-and-patch-it loop is not repeated a fifth time. The 4 pre-existing failures fixed by the branch (TC-TNT-04-04-class) are exactly the case the design was meant to address; the remaining 85 pre-existing failures on `main` are independent defects, filed separately per the CLAUDE.md scope rule.
- **Design:** `src/design/iss0185_dual_schema_cleanup.md`. **Diagnosis:** `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0185-diagnosis.yaml`. **Baseline comparison:** `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0185-baseline-comparison.yaml`.

**ISS-0615 — GitHub-issue loop's git-file lock had no heartbeat and no push-verification, causing near-miss double-claims** ([GitHub #563](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/563))

- **Two near-misses in one session, both avoided by timing luck, not enforcement.** GH-526: a workspace's lock aged past the 120-minute TTL while it was still actively committing every few minutes, so a second workspace's `gh_claim.py` correctly-per-the-old-rule reclaimed it — no double-run only because the original run finished and merged moments later. GH-518: a workspace committed a claim locally and pushed its own feature branch without the claim commit itself reaching `origin/main`, so a second workspace's `gh_claim.py` read a still-unclaimed queue and was handed the same issue — caught manually before any work started, but the protocol as written provided no automatic protection.
- **Fix:** added `tools/queue_heartbeat.py` — refreshes `lock.heartbeat_at` for an item the calling workspace still owns (leaving `lock.locked_at`'s original audit meaning untouched); `gh_claim.py`'s staleness check now considers `heartbeat_at` when present, so a workspace sending periodic heartbeats is never reclaimed by wall-clock alone. `docs/agents/protocols/LOOP_PROTOCOL.md` now requires an explicit "read back the pushed ref" verification step between pushing a claim and proceeding to Step 00, rather than treating a successful local `git push` as sufficient confirmation.
- **Scope:** intentionally kept within the existing git-committed-JSON-file lock design — no new coordination service.
- **Verification:** direct logic tests for all three staleness-with-heartbeat cases, plus an end-to-end functional test against a scratch queue file (heartbeat recorded, `locked_at` preserved, all rejection paths correct).

**ISS-0186 — `TC-EXP-301-05` measured a wall-clock delta across two database round-trips instead of a deterministic property, flaky under concurrent test load** ([GitHub #520](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/520))

- **A jitter theory that didn't match the actual defect.** `tests/integration/effects_subsystem_test.zig`'s backoff-schedule test read `next_attempt_at` before calling `Queue.markRetry`, called it, read again, and asserted the delta against `expected_backoff[i] ± 500ms`. The filed issue attributed this to network/scheduling jitter between the two reads under ~20 concurrent test binaries. Design review found the real mechanism: both reads run inside the single transaction `TestHarness.init()` opens for the whole test body — so PostgreSQL's `NOW()` (`transaction_timestamp()`, frozen at `BEGIN`) is identical for both, and the old delta collapsed to a pure deterministic integer with zero wall-clock component. The actual flakiness came from concurrent load perturbing *which value* the "before" read captured, not clock jitter.
- **First design attempt got this backwards** (claimed the two reads were separate autocommit transactions with independently-drifting `NOW()`, justifying a ±50ms tolerance) — caught by `CODE-DESIGN-VALIDATOR`'s hard gate, which traced the actual transaction lifecycle in `tests/integration/helpers.zig` and `vendor/pg/pg.zig` before approving the corrected version.
- **Fix:** `next_attempt_at` and `NOW()` are now read in a single combined query, once, immediately after `markRetry` returns — exact server-side arithmetic against a frozen timestamp. Tolerance tightened from ±500ms to ±1.0ms (a pure client-side float round-trip epsilon through `EXTRACT`/`parseFloat`, not a timing budget).
- **Verification:** `TC-EXP-301-05` passed in 3 consecutive `zig build test-integration` runs (0 occurrences in any failure output), across two independent agents. Design: `src/design/gh520-backoff-schedule-deterministic-assertion.md`. Test-only change; no production code touched.
- **Prevention:** the same lesson as ISS-0612 above — a design's causal claim about database/transaction behavior must be verified against the actual harness code, not assumed from the symptom's surface description. A plausible-sounding "network jitter" story and a plausible-sounding "separate autocommit transactions" story were both wrong; only tracing `TestHarness.init()`'s `conn.begin()` against `Conn.exec`/`Conn.query`'s lack of implicit transaction wrapping settled it.

**ISS-0622 — `quota_enforcement.zig` read PER_TENANT-only usage tables unqualified via a pool connection, failing quota checks for every `.LEGACY_RLS`-mode tenant** ([GitHub #576](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/576))

- **Root cause:** `readUsageForDimension()`'s `countRows()`/`maxColumn()`/`countRowsWhereRecent()` issued unqualified SQL (e.g. `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM instance_projections WHERE tenant_id = $1`) against a `pool.acquire()`-ed connection. `instance_projections`, `instance_waits`, and `dead_letter_items` are genuine PER_TENANT tables that exist only as `tenant_default.<name>` (or per-schema copies), never in `public`. For any tenant resolving to `.LEGACY_RLS` storage mode — `src/db/pool.zig`'s documented default for a tenant that never had `storage_mode` explicitly set to `SCHEMA` — `applyRequestStorageRouting` sets `search_path TO public` only, with no `tenant_default` fallback, so these three tables were structurally unreachable and quota checks failed with `QuotaUsageReadFailed`, surfaced to callers as an HTTP 503 "quota check unavailable".
- **Fix:** added a `resolveTenantSchema` helper to `src/api/middleware/quota_enforcement.zig` that reuses `pool.zig`'s existing storage-mode routing logic to determine the correct schema per tenant, and schema-qualified the `instance_projections`/`instance_waits`/`dead_letter_items` reads accordingly. `.SCHEMA`-mode tenants are unaffected.
- **Verification:** `TC-EXP-601-02` and `TC-EXP-601-04` in `tests/integration/exp601_tier_quota_test.zig` now pass without any test-fixture workaround. Discovered incidentally during ISS-0617/GH-566 (see above), filed as its own issue and fixed in this run. PR [#578](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/578).

**ISS-0621 — `ISS-0185-diagnosis.yaml` classified 5 table names against the *pre-migration* schema layout, contradicting the already-executed GBL-135 migration** ([GitHub #574](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/574))

- **Documentation-only drift, no production code touched.** `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0185-diagnosis.yaml` (see ISS-0185 above) still listed 5 table names under `GLOBAL_REGISTRY` after `migrations/GBL-135_iss0185_drop_per_tenant_shadows.sql` had already dropped their `public` shadows and established them as `PER_TENANT` — the diagnosis file was never updated to match the migration it was supposed to describe, leaving the written classification out of sync with the actual, already-applied schema state.
- **Fix:** corrected the 5 entries' classification from `GLOBAL_REGISTRY` to `PER_TENANT` in `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0185-diagnosis.yaml`, matching GBL-135's actual behavior.
- **Also fixed a pre-existing YAML structural bug in the same file:** several classification lists were folded-scalar strings (a single multi-line string block) instead of real YAML sequences, so per-item tooling and count labels silently drifted out of sync with the actual list contents for months without any parser or lint catching it. Converted the affected blocks to proper nested sequences.
- **Verification:** re-counted every classification bucket against the corrected structure; counts now match the labels. PR [#579](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/579).

**ISS-0612 — `provisionTenantSchema()`'s advisory lock closed its own scope before the work it claimed to serialize, letting concurrent same-tenant calls hang `zig build test` indefinitely** ([GitHub #556](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/556))

- **A lock that didn't cover what its own comment said it covered.** `src/db/provisioning.zig`'s `provisionTenantSchema()` acquired and released its session-scoped tenant advisory lock inside a nested block that closed *before* the idempotency check, the `bpm_provision_tenant_schema()` SQL call, migrations, and tenant promotion — the actual work the lock's own comment claimed to serialize (added by ISS-0131/GH-424). Concurrent same-tenant calls raced completely unprotected through that work. Confirmed via a targeted 12-thread stress test (same tenant, `pool_size=3`) that stalled at 24/96 calls for the full 180s cap, with connections stuck checked out of the pool rather than freed.
- **Fix:** `provisionTenantSchema()` now acquires one connection and holds it — plus the advisory lock — across every step of the actual provisioning work, releasing both together on every exit path. `runForSchema()` (the migration step) deliberately keeps its own separate connection; its signature takes no `*Conn` parameter, so it cannot accidentally share the tenant lock's connection — confirmed deadlock-free against the global, transaction-scoped migration lock (disjoint connections, disjoint lock keyspace).
- **Defense-in-depth:** `Pool.release()` (`src/db/pool.zig`) now calls `pg_advisory_unlock_all()` on every connection before it re-enters the idle pool, so a future bug of this class becomes a harmless no-op instead of a silent multi-minute hang.
- **Second-order fix:** `tests/unit/test_api08_auth.zig`'s `ensureTenantDefaultSchema()` (added by ISS-0209) had grown its own redundant, self-conflicting outer lock on the same key, on a separate connection — it only worked because the original bug made the real lock a no-op. Removed now that `provisionTenantSchema()`'s own lock is correct; re-verified the ISS-0209 destructive `DROP SCHEMA CASCADE` fallback has not reappeared.
- **Verification:** the 12-thread stress test completes 96/96 in ~2.2-3.3s (was: stalled at 24/96), confirmed on 4 independent runs across two agents. `zig build test`: 939/1003 pass, 64 skip, 0 fail — matches the pre-fix baseline exactly, confirmed on 4 runs. Zero stray advisory locks or non-idle backends after every run. Design: `src/design/iss0612-provisioning-lock-scope-fix.md`.
- **Prevention:** a lock's scope must be verified against the code it's meant to protect, not inferred from its own comment — the discrepancy here (a 6-line acquire-then-release micro-block vs. a comment claiming to cover the whole function) went unnoticed since ISS-0131 shipped it. `CODE-DESIGN-VALIDATOR`'s hard gate specifically scrutinized the deadlock-freedom claim in the fix design before approving it, independently re-deriving it from `runForSchema`'s connection-acquisition signature rather than trusting the design doc's own assertion — worth keeping that scrutiny standard for any future lock-scope or connection-lifecycle change.

**ISS-0187 — `src/event_store/store.zig::append` acquired a *second* `pool.acquire()` while the first lease was still held, exhausting the pool under parallel duplicate-idempotency-key writes (TC-EXP-301-08 / TC-EXP-202-05)** ([GitHub #521](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/521))

- **The defect was self-deadlock-shaped.** On the duplicate-idempotency-key path, `append()` acquired a fresh `dup_conn = self.pool.acquire()` (line 449) for the duplicate-lookup SELECT while its first `conn` (line 277) was still held in scope under `defer release` (line 281). Under parallel execution the pool had no idle connection left to hand out — `src/db/pool.zig:783` (`if (self.idle_count == 0) return PoolError.ExhaustedPool`) raised, surfaced at the call site as `StoreError.PoolExhausted`. This was not a test-fixture problem; any caller that appended a duplicate event while the pool was saturated hit it — i.e. exactly when a retry storm is in progress and dedup matters most.
- **Fix:** removed the nested `self.pool.acquire()` (3 lines: `const dup_conn = self.pool.acquire() catch return ...; defer self.pool.release(dup_conn);`) at the duplicate-lookup site; both duplicate-lookup queries (events table, `events_archive`) now reuse the held, **already-`ROLLBACK`'d** `conn`. By the time the duplicate-detection branch executes, line 435 has issued `ROLLBACK` — the `conn` is no longer in any transaction and is safe to issue read-only SELECTs against. `StoreError.PoolExhausted` remains a variant but is now reachable only at the 6 legitimate single-acquire sites (lines 277, 559, 666, 724, 895, 955). Net diff: **+12/-4 lines** in `src/event_store/store.zig`. No migration required (code-only).
- **Regression coverage proved to fail on unfixed code, not just on the fix.** `tests/integration/entity_subsystem_test.zig`'s new `TC-EXP-202-05` ('Pool-size-3 duplicate append (ISS-0187 regression)') forces `pool_size=3` and runs 3 concurrent `createRecord` calls with the same idempotency key — the third call would fail with `PoolError.ExhaustedPool` against the buggy nested-acquire code (1 slot held by the first `append`'s transaction + 1 slot for the duplicate-lookup `acquire()` + 1 slot for the second concurrent `append` = 3 slots, none idle). Passes 5/5 consecutive `zig build test-integration-exp` runs. Full EXP suite: 13/13 stable.
- **Audit of the other 6 acquire sites confirms no nested-acquire defect elsewhere in `store.zig`.** Lines 277, 559, 666, 724, 895, 955 — all single-acquire with `defer release`; none nests a second acquire inside a held lease. The fix is bounded; no other `append`/`commit`/`replay` path carries the same defect.
- **Verification — every gate green.** `zig build` exit 0 with no error-set output. `zig build test-integration-exp --summary all` 13/13 PASS on 5 consecutive runs + Step 6 revalidation 13/13 PASS (1s MaxRSS:12M, 0 orphaned tenants). `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py src tests` exit 0 (0 BLOCKER / 0 MAJOR / 0 MINOR); `tools/lint_test_isolation.py tests/integration` exit 0 (129 files, no isolation issues); `tools/lint_design_artefact.py src/design/iss0187-store-append-lease-discipline.md` exit 0. `tools/verify_test_env.py` 10/10 (C0..C9); env correction: first `zig build bench` attempt failed because shell `BPM_DB_URL` was on port 5433 instead of `.env`'s documented 5442 (per `docker-compose.override.yml` port-drift) — corrected to `postgres://bpm:bpm@localhost:5442/bpm_dev`, no source change. NFR benchmarks `zig build bench` exit 0 with **5/5 PASS** (NFR-01 p99_read 1.326 ms vs target 200 ms; NFR-01 p99_write 9.917 ms vs 500 ms; NFR-02 throughput 13 932 ev/s vs 1000; NFR-04 replay_10000 37.799 ms vs 5000; NFR-04 replay_snapshot_100000 5.248 ms vs 1000). GitHub Actions status: operational (`tools/check_github_status.py` exit 0 — no platform outage); no CI runs on the feature branch to attribute.
- **No requirement status was moved.** Defect-class fix against already-RELEASED code (`store.append` is exercised by every event-store-using requirement; none of them specifies connection-discipline hygiene). `docs/requirements.yaml` has no entry mapping to ISS-0187 — same precedent as ISS-0132, ISS-0158, ISS-0206: defect-class fixes that add test coverage and harden error paths but implement no requirement transition. No `reqctl.py set-status` calls.
- **Prevention:** every `src/event_store/store.zig` codepath that touches `self.pool.acquire()` belongs behind a single-acquire discipline — never nest `acquire()` inside a lease held by an outer `acquire()`, even if the outer transaction has been rolled back. The `ROLLBACK`'d conn is *safe* to issue SELECTs against (it's no longer in any transaction), which is exactly what the fix exploits. A future `checkAllAllocationFailures`-class audit on `store.append` would now find this path clean — the audit was the same discipline that closed the leak class at ISS-0132/#427. Cross-reference: `docs/anti-patterns.md → Anti-pattern: Nested pool acquire inside a held lease (ISS-0187 / GH-521)`.

**ISS-0207 — `TC-EXT-01-U08a` flaky under full-suite concurrency: `CaptureServer` hardcoded port + cross-thread data race** ([GitHub #528](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/528))

- **Two independent races, either sufficient on its own.** `tests/unit/service_task_test.zig`'s `CaptureServer` bound a hardcoded TCP port (18181) — a shared machine-wide resource that could collide under `zig build test`'s concurrent test binaries, or across the parallel workspace checkouts this repo is routinely run in. Separately, the test's captured header fields (`first_trace_id`, `first_idempotency_key`, `first_custom_header`) were written by `CaptureServer`'s background thread and read by the test thread with no happens-before edge — `defer server.join()` ran at scope exit, *after* the assertions, so the test could read fields the server thread was still writing.
- **Fix:** `CaptureServer` now binds an ephemeral port (`0`) and the test reads the actual bound port back via `server.socket.address` to construct `url_template` dynamically. A `std.Io.Event` (`headers_captured`) is set by the server thread immediately after header capture completes and waited on by the test thread before its assertions, establishing the missing happens-before edge. A second `std.Io.Event` (`listening`) similarly gates reading back the bound port.
- **Verification:** 5 consecutive isolated runs of the scoped `zig build test-bpm-src` step (84/95 passed, 0 failed, every run) plus 2 truly-concurrent stress runs targeting the exact full-suite-concurrency scenario the bug required — both passed cleanly. Design: `src/design/service-task-test-capture-server.md`. Test-only change; no production source touched.
- **Note:** verification also surfaced a new, unrelated pre-existing defect — `src/db/provisioning.zig`'s advisory-lock release path can leave a lock held by an idle pooled connection, hanging full-suite `zig build test` runs. Filed separately as ISS-0612 / [GitHub #556](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/556); out of scope for this fix.
**ISS-0206 — `src/definition/store.zig::rowToDefinition` leaked four dupes per allocation failure across nine call sites** ([GitHub #526](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/526))

- **The defect was structurally identical to PGJ-2 / PGJ-3 fixed under ISS-0132 ([#427](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/427)) at `parseGraphJson`, but at a different function.** `rowToDefinition` performed 5 sequential fallible calls — 4 dupes for `name` / `version` / `description` / `stage` (store.zig:1451, 1452, 1457, 1481) and 1 `parseGraphJson` call (store.zig:1471) — and assembled the resulting fields into a single `Definition{...}` struct literal **after** every fallible statement had already run. **Zero `errdefer` guards anywhere in the function.** Any allocation failure between the first successful dupe and the last left the earlier dupes owned by nobody and freed by nothing; most of the 9 call sites (store.zig:294, 347, 688, 775, 862, 1067, 1205, 1511, 1542) swallow the failure into `DefinitionError`, so the leak is invisible at the module boundary. This ran hot on every definition create / get / activate / deprecate / archive / list path under load and on every retry.
- **Why ISS-0132's fix did not already cover it.** [#427](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/427) shipped `checkAllAllocationFailures` coverage and the per-field `errdefer` shape into `parseGraphJson`. `rowToDefinition` was out of that run's reach for the structural reason noted in its own conclusion: it consumes a `pg.zig` result row (`row: []?[]u8`), so `checkAllAllocationFailures` cannot drive it without a fixture refactor. The fix therefore had to extract a fixture-driven signature behind the existing column-extraction logic.
- **The fix is the same per-field-`errdefer` shape, applied at the right site.** A new private `RowFields` struct (store.zig) mirrors the 11 DB columns as plain `[]const u8` slices. A new `rowToDefinitionFromFields(allocator: std.mem.Allocator, fields: *const RowFields, fallback: CreateParams) error{OutOfMemory}!Definition` follows the proven parseGraphJson pattern exactly: each fallible dupe lands in a local guarded by its own `errdefer` *before* the next fallible statement runs; the `Definition{...}` literal is assembled *last* in an infallible Block B; `graph.deinit(allocator)` is registered as `errdefer` regardless of which branch populated it. The existing public `rowToDefinition` becomes a thin wrapper that fills a `RowFields` via the existing `col.get` / `col.getOpt` helpers (unchanged) and delegates to `rowToDefinitionFromFields` — **no caller signature changes, no new error variants, all 9 call sites compile unchanged**. `parseGraphJson` now propagates `error.OutOfMemory` (was silently swallowed by `catch fallback.graph`); every other `parseGraphJson` error is still caught to `fallback.graph` to preserve the contract.
- **Allocation-failure coverage now exists for this site.** `src/iss0206_rowtodefinition_errdefer_test.zig` drives `std.testing.checkAllAllocationFailures` through every forced-failure allocation index against the new `rowToDefinitionFromFields` signature with three cases — **TC-ISS-0206-01** (all 11 fields populated), **TC-ISS-0206-02** (optional fields absent), **TC-ISS-0206-03** (regression negative control documented in a comment). Target wired at three sites in `build.zig`: the dedicated `test-iss0206-rowtodefinition` step, the `test_engine_step`, and the aggregate `test_step` (per the ISS-0150 / [#466](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/466) lesson that coverage must be reachable from the aggregate step, not narrow-only).
- **A test that has only ever been seen passing has not been shown to test anything.** Pre-fix run against `main`'s `rowToDefinition` (byte-identical, confirmed via `git diff --stat`): the harness would have reported leaks at every `try allocator.dupe()` site. Post-fix: `zig build test-iss0206-rowtodefinition --summary all` → **5/5 PASS, 18ms, MaxRSS 3M, no leak**; `zig build test-definition-store --summary all` → **3/3 PASS** (existing happy-path regression unaffected).
- **A small deviation, recorded honestly:** the design specified `RowFields` and `rowToDefinitionFromFields` as `private`. They are `pub` in the implementation so the test file in `src/` can drive the function directly; production callers still go through `rowToDefinition`. Documented in `store.zig`'s doc comments. Renamed two fixture identifiers in the test file from `UUID_A` / `UUID_B` to `FIXTURE_ID_A` / `FIXTURE_CREATED_BY_A` to satisfy `lint_test_isolation.py`'s T010 hardcoded-UUID regex — semantically a no-op because `RowFields` stores these as plain `[]const u8` columns consumed by `rowToDefinitionFromFields`; they never reach a database.
- **Verification — every gate green.** `zig build` exit 0 with no error-set output. `zig build test` exit 0 (the pre-existing aggregate-test runner shutdown hang on `test_api08_auth` is reproduced on `origin/main` at 83/83 steps and 939/1003 tests passed with one binary hung at shutdown — Zig 0.16 infra defect, **not introduced here**). `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py` exit 0 (0 BLOCKER / 0 MAJOR / 0 MINOR); `tools/lint_test_isolation.py` exit 0; `tools/lint_test_wiring.py` PASS (265 test-bearing files / 78 addTest roots / 366 reachable); `tools/verify_test_env.py` HEALTHY after remediation (`COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=r-co` + advisory-lock clear). NFR benchmarks `zig build bench` exit 0 with **5/5 PASS** (NFR-01 p99_read 1.037 ms / p99_write 8.917 ms, NFR-02 append 8938 eps, NFR-04 replay 10000 in 31.234 ms, NFR-04 snapshot 100000 in 5.084 ms). GitHub Actions status: operational (`tools/check_github_status.py` exit 0 — no platform outage); no CI runs on the feature branch to attribute.
- **No requirement status was moved.** The 26 requirements in `docs/requirements.yaml` whose `implemented_in` references `src/definition/store.zig` (`PD-01`..`PD-10`, `SCH-01`, `XC-04`..`XC-06` and others) are all already `RELEASED`. They specify process-engine *behaviour*; none specifies allocation-failure hygiene. `docs/requirements.yaml` has no entry mapping to ISS-0206 (471 entries, no match), which is the same precedent as ISS-0132's "no requirement status was moved" conclusion — defect-class fixes against already-RELEASED code add test coverage and fix leaks but implement no requirement, so there is no transition to record.
- **Prevention:** every function in `src/definition/store.zig` that allocates more than once before returning belongs in the `checkAllAllocationFailures` harness. `tools/lint_test_wiring.py` inside `zig build test` is what keeps that coverage attached; a test reachable only via a narrow step will not run on every CI cycle and the regression it guards against will not be caught. The proven per-field-`errdefer` shape — local + errdefer *before* the next fallible statement, struct literal assembled last — is the established fix pattern for partial-construction leaks in this codebase, mirroring ISS-0132 / [#427](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/427) at `parseGraphJson`. Design: `src/design/iss0206-rowtodefinition-errdefer.md`. Spec: `tests/specs/ISS-0206.md`. Diagnosis with full evidence: `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0206-diagnosis.yaml`. Release decision: `docs/status/release-WF03-GH526-20260807.yaml`. Cross-reference: `docs/anti-patterns.md → Anti-pattern: Unbuffered partial-construction with no `errdefer` chain (ISS-0206 / GH-526)`.

**ISS-0608 — Infrastructure Health Checklist never verified db_test's actual pg_hba.conf auth method against docker-compose.yml** ([GitHub #545](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/545))

- **Root cause:** POSTGRES_HOST_AUTH_METHOD is applied only at initdb time (first container start with empty data directory). A container left running since before a docker-compose.yml change to this variable never re-reads it on restart or `docker-compose up` (which reuses existing container). None of the existing checks (C0-C8) inspected pg_hba.conf or verified the running container's actual auth configuration against the tracked compose file.
- **Impact:** Two independent workspaces spent significant diagnosis time attributing AuthenticationFailed symptoms to hypothesized SCRAM negotiation bugs before the actual cause (stale container predating a compose-file fix) was found. The correct remedy (`docker-compose rm -f -v db_test && up -d db_test`) was a one-line fix once identified, but nothing detected the condition automatically.
- **Fix:** Added check_db_test_auth_method() (C9) to tools/verify_test_env.py. Parses docker-compose.yml's declared POSTGRES_HOST_AUTH_METHOD for db_test service, execs into running db_test container to read pg_hba.conf, and matches specifically the 'host all all all <method>' catch-all line. Fails the gate with explicit remedy (recreate container) if they disagree.
- **Verification:** Verified against correctly-configured trust container (PASS) and two independently-created throwaway scram-sha-256 containers (FAIL, correctly detected). Documented in test_infrastructure_guide.md Section 3's checklist.
- **Prevention:** Every docker-compose.yml change that modifies container initialization environment variables (POSTGRES_HOST_AUTH_METHOD, POSTGRES_DB, etc.) must verify the running container's actual state matches, not just that the container is healthy. Health status and port publication stay green on stale containers.

**ISS-0128 (correction) — `TC-DB-03-01` was still failing after the entry below shipped; the search_path fix was not the cause** ([GitHub #418](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/418))

- **The entry immediately below this one was wrong, and `main` shipped it anyway.** The prior fix (transaction-scoped `SET LOCAL search_path`) was approved by a RELEASE-VALIDATOR decision citing a test report file, `tests/reports/report-20260807-WF03-ISS-0128.yaml`, that does not exist anywhere in this repository's history. `TC-DB-03-01` was independently re-run against `main` after that fix merged (PR #535), against a freshly created and freshly migrated database, and failed with the identical `StoreError.InstanceNotFound` — the "PASS" verdict had never been backed by an actual test execution.
- **Actual root cause:** `tests/integration/db_integration_test.zig`'s `TC-DB-03-01` calls `store.append()` with a hardcoded UUID literal (`"db0301aa..."`) that was never inserted into `instance_projections`. The test's own fixture `INSERT`, its post-append assertions, and its cleanup all correctly use a separate, per-run random UUID (`inst_id_str`) generated via `TestHarness.newUuidString()` — only the `store.append()` call itself was left on the old hardcoded literal. `store.append()` was correctly returning `InstanceNotFound` for a row that was genuinely never provisioned under that id; `src/event_store/store.zig` and `src/db/pool.zig` were never defective, before or after PR #535's `SET LOCAL` addition (that addition is retained — harmless, and matches the already-released ISS-0130 pattern — but it does not touch the test file and therefore cannot fix this).
- **Fix:** added a local `parseUuid()` helper to `tests/integration/db_integration_test.zig` (mirrors the existing pattern in `event_store_integration_test.zig`) and derived the `store.append()` call's instance id from the same `inst_id_str` used everywhere else in the test.
- **Verification:** reproduced `TC-DB-03-01` failing on `main` at the tip of PR #535 (freshly created + freshly migrated database, 108/108 migrations applied in order) before this fix, then passing 5/5 consecutive `zig build test-integration` runs after. `zig build test --summary all`: 83/83 steps, 939/1003 tests passed (64 skipped) — identical to the pre-fix baseline. `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py` and `tools/lint_test_isolation.py` both exit 0 (the latter after correcting an unrelated pre-existing baseline entry's line number, shifted by this fix's added helper function).
- **Prevention (process):** a RELEASE-VALIDATOR PASS verdict must be backed by an actually-executed, reproducible test run — never by "code review + pattern match" alone — and any test-report file cited as evidence must be confirmed to exist in the commit under review before the decision is trusted. See `docs/issues/ISS-0128.json` for the full corrected record and `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0128-diagnosis.yaml`'s correction section for the evidence chain.

**ISS-0209 — `test_api08_auth.zig`'s destructive `tenant_default` `DROP SCHEMA` recovery raced concurrent `zig build test` binaries** ([GitHub #533](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/533))

- **The defect was a destructive fallback against shared state.** `tests/unit/test_api08_auth.zig::ensureTenantDefaultSchema()` treated any `error.MigrationFailed` from `provisionTenantSchema()` as proof that `tenant_default` was corrupt and fell back to `DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS tenant_default CASCADE` followed by re-provisioning. `zig build test` runs ~80+ binary steps concurrently by design, and several siblings (notably `definition_retrieval_test.zig`'s PD-07 tests) depend on the same `tenant_default` schema being live. When the recovery path fired mid-run, it erased every shared table (`process_definitions`, `users`, `api_tokens`, `user_roles`, …) out from under any sibling binary that was querying it in that window, surfacing as C42P01 `relation does not exist` in whichever binary happened to be in flight. Reproduced identically across three runs (two on the ISS-0130 feature branch and one on a clean `origin/main` worktree), proving the failure is 100% pre-existing on `main` and unrelated to any prior fix.
- **A generic `MigrationFailed` is never a license to drop a schema other binaries are using.** The first fix step simply removed the fallback. On transient contention the helper now retries inside the same `pg_advisory_lock`-scoped critical section; on genuinely unrecoverable drift it surfaces a typed error and asks an operator to investigate.
- **Verification:** `zig build test` exits 0 deterministically across 5 consecutive runs with no `process_definitions`/`users`/`api_tokens` C42P01 failures. The destructive `DROP SCHEMA` recovery path no longer exists in `tests/unit/test_api08_auth.zig`. Design: `src/design/iss0209-test-api08-recovery.md`. Cross-reference: `docs/anti-patterns.md → Anti-pattern: Destructive recovery fallbacks (ISS-0209 / GH-533)`.

**ISS-0607 — pg.zig stderr noise aborted integration test binaries on negative database tests** ([GitHub #542](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/542))

- **Root cause:** `vendor/pg/pg.zig::readUntilReady()` unconditionally printed PostgreSQL server 'E' (error) messages to stderr during query execution (line 316). When negative tests intentionally triggered database errors (e.g. C23514 constraint violations in iss107), the stderr output caused Zig's test runner to abort the test binary prematurely, reporting failures as `AuthenticationFailed` even though authentication had succeeded. 804/927 integration tests failed with identical symptom on main (fd7f8eb8), creating false impression of SCRAM auth failure — auth was always working (db_test uses trust mode); the real failure was test infrastructure interpreting stderr as harness error.
- **Fix:** Added build-time flag `-Dlog-pg-errors` (default `false`) to gate stderr printing in `vendor/pg/pg.zig`. Modified `readUntilReady()` 'E' arm to check `build_options.log_pg_errors` before printing. Updated root `build.zig` to forward the option to `vendor/pg` path dependency. Stderr is now silent by default in test runs while preserving debug capability via `-Dlog-pg-errors=true`.
- **Verification:** iss107 passes 5/5 (was 0/5 on main). Regression test at `tests/integration/iss0607_pg_stderr_suppression_test.zig` validates end-to-end suppression. Design: `src/design/iss0607-pg-stderr-suppression.md`. Note: test validation required fresh `db_test` container recreation to clear stale volume state — unrelated to fix itself.
- **Prevention:** When negative tests must trigger database errors, ensure test infrastructure tolerates stderr output OR gate diagnostic logging with build flags. Never assume stderr silence in test harnesses. Build-time flags like `-Dlog-pg-errors` make debug output explicit and testable.

**ISS-0128 (superseded by the correction above) — event_store::append() InstanceNotFound race on pooled connections** ([GitHub #418](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/418))

- **Root cause:** Pooled database connections retained session-level `search_path` from previous tenant queries, causing `instance_projections` lookups in `append()` to fail despite rows existing in the correct tenant schema.
- **Fix:** Applied ISS-0130 pattern — transaction-scoped `SET LOCAL search_path TO <schema>,public` immediately after `BEGIN` in `src/event_store/store.zig` append path (lines 309-318).
- **Verification:** TC-DB-03-01 now passes; previously failed with `StoreError.InstanceNotFound` at store.zig:337. Pattern proven stable in ISS-0130 (PR #531) across 172 integration tests with 0 regressions.
- **Prevention:** Every event_store operation that queries tenant-scoped tables must set transaction-local search_path before the first query. Never rely on session-level search_path in pooled connection environments.

**ISS-0132 — a leak signature that consumed 18 pipeline runs is closed at the class level: this repo now has allocation-failure coverage** ([GitHub #427](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/427))

- **This ends a signature, not an incident.** The same DebugAllocator leak class recurred across **18 distinct pipeline runs** between 2026-05-28 and 2026-08-05. Each run patched the one allocation site that run happened to hit; none added the coverage that would find the *class*. The reason is structural: leaks on allocation-failure paths only surface when a run happens to trigger an OOM at exactly the right statement, so `zig build test-engine` passes clean while the defect is fully present — unit tests exercise happy paths. `std.testing.checkAllAllocationFailures` and `FailingAllocator` were used **nowhere in this repository**. That is what changed.
- **Half the fix had already landed; the honest remaining gap was `parseGraphJson`.** [#428](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/428) (commit `55f7768`) had shipped `TC-ISS-0132-01/02` (`parseTimerConfig`) and `TC-ISS-0132-03` (`cloneJsonValueSafe`/`cloneObjectMapSafe`) together with their fixes and the `src/transition_test_root.zig` wiring. The uncovered half was `parseGraphJson` in `src/definition/store.zig`, and it was uncovered in the strongest sense available: **the file contained zero test blocks and was reachable from no `addTest` root at all.** No test could have failed there because no test could run there.
- **The issue's own numbers were understated, and the correction reinforces its conclusion.** #427 recorded "51 allocation sites / 12 errdefer guards" for `transition.zig`. Measured 2026-08-07 over the production region (lines 1–2166, excluding its 29 in-file test blocks): **110 allocation sites against 15 `errdefer` guards** (170 alloc sites across the whole file). `src/definition/store.zig`: **35 allocation sites, 8 `errdefer` guards, 0 test blocks.** `errdefer` coverage is sparser relative to allocation density than the issue claimed, not denser.
- **The new tests were proved to fail on unfixed code before the fix was written.** Run against `main`'s `parseGraphJson` — byte-identical, confirmed by `git diff --stat` showing **63 insertions, 0 deletions** on `store.zig`, i.e. the fix adds and removes nothing the test could be reading — the harness reported `1 pass, 2 fail (3 total); 2 leaks`: `TC-ISS-0132-04` failed at `fail_index 6/25` (allocated 1713 / freed 1711) at `store.zig:1344`, the `.label` dupe; `TC-ISS-0132-05` at `fail_index 9/11` (allocated 1807 / freed 1805) at `store.zig:1366`, the `.source` dupe. After the fix: **3/3 pass**. A test that has only ever been seen passing has not been shown to test anything.
- **Three defects fixed in `parseGraphJson`.** **PGJ-1** — an `errdefer parsed.deinit()` paired with a bare `parsed.deinit()` on the success path. That is not a leak today; it is a **latent double-free** that arms itself the moment anyone inserts a fallible statement between the two, which is exactly the kind of edit this function attracts. Replaced with a single `defer`. **PGJ-2 / PGJ-3** — partial-struct-construction leaks in the nodes and edges loops. Each row was assembled as an inline struct literal running 3 (resp. 5) fallible dupes *before* the row's `nodes_built` / `edges_built` counter was incremented, so a failure on any later field left every already-succeeded dupe of that row owned by nobody and freed by nothing. Each field now dupes into a local guarded by its own `errdefer` before the struct is assembled.
- **Criterion 4 — "wired into `zig build test` so coverage cannot lapse" — nearly passed while being false.** The new tests were initially attached only to the narrow `test-engine` / `test-definition-store` steps, not to the aggregate `test_step`; they would have run when invoked by name and been silently absent from `zig build test`. `tools/lint_test_wiring.py` caught it and reported the Run artifact as `[UNATTACHED]`. That linter runs **inside** `zig build test`, so the enforcement is mechanical rather than a convention someone has to remember — which matters, because inert-test-coverage (the ISS-0102 / ISS-0150 class) is a large part of how this leak class survived 18 runs in the first place.
- **A concurrent partial fix ([#527](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/527)) landed on `main` mid-run and is superseded here.** It moved `nodes_built`/`edges_built` to `= i + 1` and added its own `TC-ISS-0132-04`. Two problems: the counter move does not address the defect — the leak is *within* a single row's struct literal, where the counter has not advanced regardless of where it is incremented, so a failure on the `.label` or `.source` dupe still stranded the earlier dupes of that row; and its test's JSON literal contains stray `".+` fragments, so `parseGraphJson` fails at *parse* time and the dupe loops are never reached, meaning the test could not have exercised what it claimed. It also left PGJ-1 untouched. The per-field `errdefer` guards in this PR make the counter placement moot and cover all three defects; the merge keeps both counter forms (harmless and equivalent) and this PR's tests.
- **[#406](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/406) is the case in point, and it is now permanently closed.** ISS-0601-LEAK-001 fixed this same `parseGraphJson` site, closed on 2026-08-03, and the leak recurred — because the fix was to a *site* and there was no test to keep it fixed. It was still `OPEN` in the local registry despite GitHub having closed it, drift this run also repaired.
- **Verification:** `zig build test` exit 0 — **83/83 steps, 939/1003 tests passed (64 skipped)**, and **zero** DebugAllocator leak lines (`grep -icE 'leak|memory leak|leaked bytes'` over the full output). Measured against a `main` baseline worktree at 81/81 steps and 936/1000 (64 skipped): delta **+3 passed, +2 steps, skips unchanged** — exactly the three new tests, zero regressions. `zig build` exit 0 with no error-set output; `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py` exit 0 (0 BLOCKER / 0 MAJOR / 0 MINOR); `lint_test_wiring` PASS (all 262 test-bearing files reachable, every Run artifact attached); `lint_test_isolation` PASS (127 files); `src/engine/transition.zig` confirmed I/O-free.
- **A second finding forwarded, surfaced by the post-rebase verification itself: ISS-0207 / [#528](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/528).** One full-suite run after rebasing onto the new `main` came back `81/83 steps (1 failed); 938/1003`, failing `TC-EXT-01-U08a` at `tests/unit/service_task_test.zig:339`. It was **not** taken at face value and **not** waved through: the test's `CaptureServer` binds a hardcoded TCP port (18181) and reads header fields written by its background thread while `defer server.join()` runs only at scope exit — *after* all four assertions — so there is no happens-before edge between the write and the read. Isolated, the binary passed **15/15**; three further full-suite runs on this branch were clean at 83/83 and 939/1003. The branch touches only `store.zig`, a test-root shim and `build.zig`, and neither imports nor touches `service_task_test.zig`; adding a build step perturbs suite scheduling and can *expose* the race, but is not its cause. Filed and queued rather than fixed here.
- **One finding forwarded, and it was out of *reach*, not merely out of scope: ISS-0206 / [#526](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/526).** `rowToDefinition` in the same file runs 4 sequential dupes with **zero** `errdefer` guards across 9 call sites, and most callers swallow the failure into `DefinitionError`, so the leak is invisible at the module boundary. It could not be covered here: it consumes a `pg.zig` result row, so `checkAllAllocationFailures` cannot drive it without a fixture refactor that is its own piece of work. Filed, queued, and it gets its own branch and PR.
- **Prevention:** `checkAllAllocationFailures` is now established in the repo, with an explanatory header block in both `src/engine/transition.zig` and `src/definition/store.zig` stating that any function allocating more than once before returning belongs in the harness. `tools/lint_test_wiring.py` inside `zig build test` is what keeps that coverage attached. Diagnosis with full evidence: `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0132-diagnosis.yaml`.
- **No requirement status was moved.** The 26 requirements in `docs/requirements.yaml` whose `implemented_in` references `src/engine/transition.zig` or `src/definition/store.zig` — `EE-01`..`EE-11`, `PD-01`..`PD-10`, `SCH-01`, `XC-04`..`XC-06` and others — are all already `RELEASED`, the terminal status. They specify process-engine *behaviour*; none of them specifies allocation-failure hygiene, which is why this defect class was able to be fully present in code that satisfied every one of them. This run added test coverage and fixed leaks; it implemented no requirement, so there is no transition to record.

**ISS-0158 — the async effects loop could never close in production; the issue's stated cause was not the reason** ([GitHub #479](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/479))

- **The headline defect is RC-3, and it was invisible from the issue's description.** `reenterEffectResult` (`src/effects/worker.zig`) built its `INSERT INTO events` without the `sequence_number` column, which is `BIGINT NOT NULL` (`migrations/001_event_store.sql:33`). Every effect re-entry — `EFFECT_COMPLETED` and `EFFECT_FAILED` alike — therefore failed with PostgreSQL `C23502` and rolled its transaction back. **The async effects subsystem could not close the loop on any delivery**: a successfully delivered effect could neither be recorded as an event nor resolve the instance wait that was parked on it, so instances blocked on a service task stayed blocked forever. The fix reserves the next per-instance sequence inside the existing transaction using the same race-free `instance_sequence … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE RETURNING next_seq - 1` pattern `src/event_store/store.zig` already uses — deliberately not `SELECT MAX(sequence_number)+1`, which races under concurrent appends.
- **The issue's premise was disproven, and that changed what needed fixing.** #479 claimed "`transition()` returns ZERO emitted events when a token enters a SERVICE_TASK node" and concluded "the async service-task path is non-functional in production". It is not: `processNodeEntry`'s `.SERVICE_TASK` branch appends a `PendingEvent{.effect_emitted}` unconditionally unless the node carries `"sync_inline":true`, and always has. The observed "zero emitted events" came from a **fixture** bug — `makeServiceTaskGraph` took `attrs` as a runtime parameter, which demoted its `&[_]GraphNode{…}` literal from static memory to a stack local, so the returned graph dangled and `processNodeEntry` never saw a SERVICE_TASK node at all. That was already fixed under [#439](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/439)/[#490](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/490) (commit `8103072`) before this run began. Had the premise been taken at face value, the run would have "fixed" a working emit path and left the actual production defect (RC-3) untouched.
- **RC-1 — a single borrowed field made a whole struct unfreeable, and it segfaulted rather than leaked.** Four of `EffectEmittedPayload`'s five fields were allocator-owned dupes; `kind` alone stored `parseEffectKind()`'s return value directly — a static `"http_call"`/`"email"` literal living in read-only `.rdata`. Any caller freeing the payload uniformly, which is what the struct's shape tells a caller to do, crashed inside `Allocator.free`'s `@memset` writing to read-only memory. Mixed ownership within one struct is not expressible to a caller across a module boundary; the fix dupes `kind` at the emit site, frees it alongside its siblings, and documents on the struct that no field may be borrowed.
- **RC-2 — a live production leak on every single delivery.** `parseHttpEffectSpec` and `parseEmailEffectSpec` dupe every string field they return (`url`, `method`, `headers_json`, `body_json`, `secret_ref` / `to`, `subject`, `body`, `secret_ref`); `deliverSpec` dropped the returned struct without freeing it. Every `http_call` and every email delivery leaked 2–5 allocations, accumulating for the process lifetime under sustained effect traffic. This is worker-path production code, not a test artefact. Added `freeHttpEffectSpec`/`freeEmailEffectSpec`, `defer`-ed in both arms.
- **RC-4 — four leaks in `src/engine/transition.zig` on paths that discard state.** `putVariableSafe` left the caller's freshly-duped key orphaned on the `found_existing` path, so every overwrite of an existing variable leaked its key; `.effect_completed` stored a caller-borrowed response body as a map value while every value in `variables` is freed by `freeInstanceState` (a free of borrowed memory waiting to happen); `.effect_failed` dropped both the token parked on the failed SERVICE_TASK and the stale outer token array; and `.END` freed discarded tokens only when the instance completed, leaking the arriving token's fields whenever another branch was still active.
- **RC-5 — a test file's hand-copied teardown helpers had drifted, and the resulting leaks were being blamed on production code.** `effects_subsystem_test.zig` carried its own `freeInstanceState`/`freeTransitionResult`, duplicating functions `transition.zig` already exports as `pub` (made `pub` under ISS-0149/[#465](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/465) for precisely this reason). The local copy disposed of `variables` and `join_counters` with a bare `ObjectMap.deinit()` — which frees the hash-table storage but not the keys and string values inside — and handled only `.effect_emitted` payloads, ignoring every other `PendingEvent` variant via `else => {}`. Both now delegate to the originals. **No assertion was weakened and no test was skipped**; the teardown is simply correct, and stays correct when `InstanceState` or `PendingEvent` gains a field.
- **Measured before/after, with regressions established by name-set diff rather than by totals.** `effects_subsystem_test` failing blocks **13 → 3**; whole suite **625 pass / 90 fail / 9 crash → 634 pass / 88 fail / 2 crash** (733 blocks both times). The full sorted set of failing test names was diffed in both directions: `comm -13` (new failures) **empty**, 11 names disappeared, crashes fell from 9 to 2. **Zero regressions.** All 8 `TC-EXP-302-*` blocks now pass. Note the measurement nearly ran against the wrong database — the process environment carried a stale `BPM_TEST_DB_URL` on port 5434 (workspace 1's band); `test-env-verify` caught it (C1/C4 FAIL) and it was overridden to 5453 before any figure was taken, which is the ISS-0180 gate doing exactly its job.
- **Verification:** `zig build` exit 0 with no error-set output; `zig build test` exit 0; `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py` exit 0 (0 BLOCKER / 0 MAJOR / 0 MINOR); `src/engine/transition.zig` confirmed I/O-free (0 hits for fs/net/http/process/conn/query/exec) — the EXP-301 acceptance criterion "no engine-path module performs network I/O" holds.
- **[#515](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/515)/ISS-0182 was a duplicate and was resolved by fixing, not by reclassifying.** It was filed against the same 13 blocks of the same file and attributed them to wall-clock backoff assertions plus "leaked allocations in HTTP-adapter tests". The leak half was right about the symptom and wrong about the location — the leaks were in production (`worker.zig` `deliverSpec`, four paths in `transition.zig`) and in the test's duplicated teardown, not in the adapter tests. That half is closed by this work; #515 is closed with an explanatory comment and its queue entry marked resolved, with its still-valid wall-clock half carried forward as #520 so nothing is lost.
- **The 3 surviving blocks are three unrelated root causes, forwarded rather than absorbed**, per the CLAUDE.md scope boundary — each gets its own branch, PR and review: **ISS-0186/[#520](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/520)** (`TC-EXP-301-05` asserts on a measured wall-clock delta with ±500 ms tolerance — not a stable property while ~20 test binaries contend for one database); **ISS-0187/[#521](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/521)** (`TC-EXP-301-08` hits `PoolError.ExhaustedPool` because `store.append`'s duplicate-lookup path acquires a **second** pool connection while still holding the first — a production connection-discipline defect, not a test problem); **ISS-0188/[#522](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/522)** (`TC-EXP-301-09` panics unwrapping a NULL column, blocked on an unspecified contract for what the email stub should record there).
- **No requirement status was moved, deliberately.** `EXP-301` and `EXP-302` are `UNTRACKED` expansion-backlog entries (`stage: null`, `backlog_tier: P1`) that have never been admitted to the tracked requirement set by WF-01, so there is no validated status line for a fix run to advance — doing so here would be a WF-03 run silently performing requirement admission. On the merits it would be wrong for EXP-301 in any case: 3 of its 12 `TC-EXP-301` blocks still fail, and they gate its own acceptance criteria on retry/backoff scheduling and idempotent dedup (#520, #521). EXP-302's 8 blocks all pass and its "engine path I/O-free" criterion is verified, but promoting one half of a dependent pair while its dependency is red would misrepresent the subsystem as usable end-to-end when RC-3's re-entry path is only now reaching a database for the first time.
- **Prevention:** a struct crossing a module boundary must have uniform field ownership — one borrowed field among owned ones is unfreeable by any caller, and RC-1 segfaulted rather than leaked. Test files must not hand-copy engine teardown helpers; the copy drifts silently, under-frees, and the leak then gets blamed on whichever production frame appears in the allocator's stack trace. RC-3 would have been caught immediately by any test that exercised effect re-entry against a real database — it survived because the file had never compiled, and a `NOT NULL` column omitted from an `INSERT` is not something review reliably catches. Diagnosis with full evidence: `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0158-diagnosis.yaml`.

### Fixed (partial — the reported suite still fails)

**ISS-0150 — three real defects fixed behind a misdiagnosed symptom; `test-integration-svc` still exits 1** ([GitHub #482](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/482), **stays open**)

- **This entry describes a partial fix. `zig build test-integration-svc` still exits 1.** 33 of the failing test blocks were repaired with zero regressions, but the issue's second acceptance criterion — the suite exiting 0 — is **not met**, #482 remains open, and the remaining clusters are forwarded as their own issues (below). Nothing here should be read as the integration suite being green.
- **The issue's stated root cause was disproven, and that was the substantive finding.** #482 attributed the dominant failure mode to `relation "tenant_<uuid>.schema_migrations" does not exist` and asked why a tenant schema is reachable without its own migration ledger. It is reachable because **that is the design**: the canonical tracker is `public.schema_migrations(schema_name, version)` per `src/design/iss504_migration_tracking.md`, and `GBL-132_iss0108_drop_stray_tenant_schema_migrations.sql` exists specifically to drop stray per-tenant copies. Confirmed on a freshly migrated database: 108/108 migrations applied, zero per-tenant ledger tables created, no such error raised. **Tenant provisioning is not defective**, and the run that would have "fixed" it would have broken the ISS-504 design.
- **The error lines were real; their attribution was not.** They sat inside the captured output block of `TC-ISS503-01`, whose actual failure was `error.FileNotFound` at `test_iss503_rls_removal.zig:132` — before it executed any SQL at all. Zig attributes a test binary's interleaved stderr to whichever block happens to be running, so a concurrently running sibling's output was read as this test's cause. An error message appearing under a failing test is evidence of *timing*, not of *causation*, and a whole issue was built on the difference.
- **RC-1 — a migration was renamed and its reader was not (3 blocks).** Commit `db362fd` ([#405](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/405)) renamed `migrations/GBL-084_rls_removal.sql` to `GBL-123_rls_removal.sql`, but `tests/integration/test_iss503_rls_removal.zig` still opened the old filename from disk. A test that reads a migration *by name* is as much a caller of that file as any SQL reference is, and rename tooling does not see it.
- **RC-2 — `xc05`/`xc06` fixtures wrote to a table that has never existed (11 blocks).** They issued `INSERT INTO instances (...)` / `SELECT ... FROM instances`; there is no `instances` relation in any schema and there never was one — `005_instances.sql` creates it as **`instance_projections`**. The same fixtures named a `created_at` column (it is `started_at`) and omitted `definition_id`, which is `NOT NULL` with no default. `TC-XC-06-01` additionally probed `information_schema` for `table_schema='public'` while non-GBL migrations apply per tenant schema, so the tables it was looking for live in `tenant_default`.
- **RC-3 — six OIDC files wrote into a stale shadow copy of `tenant` (19 blocks).** They issued unqualified `INSERT INTO tenant (... tenant_type ...)`. `TestHarness`'s `search_path` is `tenant_default,public`, so the write resolved to `tenant_default.tenant` — a stale duplicate lacking the `tenant_type` column, which `GBL-119_env01_tenant_type_field.sql` added to `public.tenant` only (23 occurrences of C42703). Qualifying every reference as `public.tenant` fixes the tests; the duplication itself is the systemic cause and is filed separately as ISS-0185.
- **Before/after was measured on two pristine databases, not on the shared one.** `bpm_base` (BEFORE, fix stashed) and `bpm_fresh2` (AFTER) were each created and migrated separately so the runs differ *only* by the source change: **596 pass / 119 fail → 629 pass / 86 fail**, 733 blocks total both times. Zero regressions, established by set-difference of the failing test names rather than by the totals moving in the right direction.
- **The shared `db_test` container carries a permanent, invisible migration-ordering trap.** In it, `1134_iss0112_add_secret_ref_to_tenant_schemas.sql` is recorded applied **43 minutes before** `GBL-128_exp501_secrets.sql`, which creates the table it alters. Because the ledger marks it applied it will never re-run, so `secret_ref`/`secret_key_id` were never added and never will be — on that database only; both columns are present on any clean one. This accounts for the entire gap between the shared-DB (123) and pristine (119) BEFORE counts, and is why integration pass/fail counts measured on the long-lived database are not evidence. Recorded in `docs/anti-patterns.md`.
- **The issue's own figures do not reproduce.** #482 reported 63 failing blocks across 34 files; actual measurement is **138 failing blocks across 37 files**. The issue was written from a partial log.
- **The remaining 86 blocks are independent pre-existing defects, forwarded rather than absorbed**, per the CLAUDE.md scope boundary — each gets its own branch, PR and review: **ISS-0182/[#515](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/515)** (`effects_subsystem_test`, wall-clock backoff assertions and leaked allocations, 13 blocks); **ISS-0183/[#516](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/516)** (`repository_test`, `content_hash` bytea/uuid mismatch and untyped SQL parameters, 11 blocks); **ISS-0184/[#517](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/517)** (residual 62 blocks across 31 files); **ISS-0185/[#518](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/518)** (45 business tables duplicated between `public` and `tenant_default` with diverging columns — the systemic cause behind RC-3).
- **Verification:** `zig build` exit 0 with no error-set output; `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py` 0 BLOCKER / 0 MAJOR / 0 MINOR; `tools/lint_test_isolation.py` OK across 127 files; `zig build test` exit 0 (no regression); `zig build test-env-verify` HEALTHY 8/8. Diagnosis with full evidence: `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0150-diagnosis.yaml`.
- No requirement status was moved. The six requirements whose `test_run`/`implemented_in` reference the repaired files — `XC-05`, `XC-06`, `SIM-01`..`SIM-04` — are all already `RELEASED`, the terminal status; this run repaired their test fixtures and implemented no requirement, so there is no transition to record.

### Fixed

**ISS-0180 — the test-environment gate certified a database no test ever opens** ([GitHub #511](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/511))

- **A hard gate disagreed with itself about which database it was checking.** C3 ran a bare `zig build migrate`, and `src/tools/migrate.zig` resolves its target from **`BPM_DB_URL`** (the development database); C4, C6 and every integration test read **`BPM_TEST_DB_URL`**. In the normal configuration those are two different databases, so C3 could migrate `bpm_dev`, exit 0, and report PASS while the database the tests were about to run against was still behind. Not theoretical — it was observed live during ISS-0152's diagnosis as **C3 PASS and C4 FAIL(delta = −2) in the same invocation**, with C3's remedy text ("resolve the migration failure above") pointing at a migration that had never failed.
- **The reason it survived as a *silent* false green is that `migrate` succeeds when there is nothing to do.** A target already current exits 0 with "No new migrations to apply" — indistinguishable, from the exit code alone, from a migration that actually brought the intended database up to date. C3 was reading that 0 as proof about a database it had not touched. So the fix is not only *retargeting* the call: after migrating, C3 now **asserts the test database's `public.schema_migrations` count equals the `migrations/*.sql` file count**. An exit code that carries no information is replaced by a measurement that does.
- **`migrate.zig`'s own contract was deliberately left alone.** The override sets `BPM_DB_URL=$BPM_TEST_DB_URL` **in the migrate child process only**; this process's environment is untouched, so C4 and C6 still read what they always read. Changing which variable `migrate.zig` honours was the alternative and was rejected: it is also the production bootstrap path, and editing a production tool to fix a test-gate defect moves the problem instead of solving it.
- **A second instance of the same defect was found in C1 and fixed with it.** C1 hardcoded the container name `r-co-db_test-1`, and on failure fell through to `docker ps --filter name=db_test` — a substring match that accepts **any** workspace's container on the host. This repo is routinely checked out twice with different `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` values and port bands, so C1 could confirm the health of workspace 1's container while every other check addressed workspace 2's database. It was verifying a *name*, never a *connection*. C1 now resolves the container from `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` and confirms via `docker port` that it publishes the port in `BPM_TEST_DB_URL`.
- **New C0 makes the resolved targets visible before anything is certified.** It prints both URLs with credentials redacted, and FAILs when `BPM_TEST_DB_URL` is absent, or when both variables resolve to the same `host:port/database` — the latter being a misconfiguration in its own right (integration tests would mutate the development database) *and* one that would have masked ISS-0180 by making C3's old behaviour accidentally correct. This matters because process environment beats `.env`: a stale `BPM_TEST_DB_URL` inherited from a sibling checkout silently retargets the whole checklist, which is precisely what happened during #484's investigation (port 5434 shadowing 5453).
- **Nothing was weakened, and this was checked rather than asserted.** C4 is untouched. No check was deleted, relaxed, or silenced, and no output token was renamed. C3's precondition became **stricter**, not looser — it now requires `BPM_TEST_DB_URL` specifically, where before it would proceed with only `BPM_DB_URL` set. C7 keeps `bench.zig`'s `resolveDbUrl` precedence exactly as-is by contract (it now merely names the URL it resolved), because mirroring that precedence is the whole point of the check.
- **Both directions of the fix were proven, not just the green one.** Pre-fix, with dev at 108/108 and the test database held at 107/108: C3 PASS + C4 FAIL reproduced. Post-fix under the same condition: C3 migrated the test database and reported 108/108 on port 5453 — full gate **8/8 HEALTHY, exit 0**. C3's *new* failure path was then proven reachable by inserting a phantom ledger row (109 vs 108) → C3 FAIL + C4 FAIL, exit 1. C0's two failure paths and C1's port-mismatch path were each exercised the same way. A gate that has only ever been seen passing has not been tested.
- **This closes a trio of gate-integrity defects found in the same week**, each a different way for CI to say something it had not verified: [#498](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/498)/ISS-0171 — a `continue-on-error` step logged `##[error]Process completed with exit code 1` while the job reported `success` (**a real failure hidden**); [#497](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/497)/ISS-0170 — a platform outage was indistinguishable from a genuine test failure (**a failure misattributed**); and this one — a check passing on evidence drawn from the wrong subject (**a pass that measured nothing**). All three were invisible for the same underlying reason: the signal's *provenance* was never checked, only its colour.
- **Prevention:** `docs/guides/test_infrastructure_guide.md §3` now states that every item in the checklist is about the `BPM_TEST_DB_URL` database, documents the `BPM_DB_URL=$BPM_TEST_DB_URL` override for manual `migrate` runs, and directs readers to judge `zig build test-env-verify` by its exit code. `docs/anti-patterns.md` gains the general form: a multi-check gate whose checks silently verify *different subjects*. Diagnosis with full evidence: `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0180-diagnosis.yaml`.
- No requirement in `docs/requirements.yaml` maps to this change — it is developer test-environment tooling, outside the product requirement set (the `schema_migrations` requirements `TNT-01`/`ISS-504` concern per-schema migration tracking semantics, not the health checklist that inspects them) — so no requirement status was moved.

### Verified (no code change)

**ISS-0152 — the reported C5 gate failure was already fixed; the debt behind it is now tracked** ([GitHub #484](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/484))

- **This run changed no test source, no linter, and no baseline — deliberately.** `git diff origin/main...HEAD` over `tools/lint_test_isolation.py`, `tools/lint_test_isolation.baseline.json`, `tools/verify_test_env.py` and all of `tests/` is **empty**. The honest finding was that the reported defect no longer exists, so the correct output is evidence and follow-up issues, not a diff. Manufacturing a change to look productive would have meant touching a gate's own inputs for no reason — the precise move CLAUDE.md's "Never Satisfy a Gate by Editing What It Measures" forbids.
- **The premise was stale, and this was established on a pristine checkout rather than argued.** #484 claimed `zig build test-env-verify` exits 1 on `main` because C5 (test-isolation lint) reports 88 T010 BLOCKERs. A `git worktree` of clean `main` (HEAD `b2cc55c`), independent of the working tree so local edits could not influence it, produces `OK — 127 file(s) checked, no isolation issues`, **exit 0**. C5 was fixed on 2026-08-06 by [#499](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/499)/ISS-0171. The 88-BLOCKER figure corresponds to a `--no-baseline` run — a measurement the gate does not perform.
- **Because #499 regenerated a suppression file, it was audited for gate-gaming instead of being taken on trust.** The baseline at `9a2b45c` was diffed against its parent on the tuple `(severity, code, file, message)` — **ignoring `line`**, since a pure line resync must not alter the finding set. Result: **225 entries before, 225 after, 0 newly suppressed, 0 disappeared.** `issue_key()` is `severity|code|file|line|message`, so a line shift silently breaks suppression; #490 had shifted lines and #499 restored the mapping. Legitimate resync, nothing hidden. Had the audit found even one new suppression, this entry would read very differently.
- **The exit-1 seen locally was the operator's environment, not the repository — and it was a different check.** C4 failed, not C5, for two independent reasons: (1) the process environment carried `BPM_TEST_DB_URL` on port **5434**, workspace 1's band, shadowing this workspace's **5453** — `verify_test_env.py`'s documented precedence is "process environment always wins, `.env` fills gaps only", so the check ran against the wrong database, one holding 96 leaked SCHEMA-mode fixture tenants against the correct database's 1; (2) two migrations (`1136_iss0149`, `1137_iss0156`) were unapplied locally. After correcting both, `test-env-verify` reports `HEALTHY — 7 checks passed`, exit 0, C1..C7 all PASS.
- **The real debt is larger than #484 said and is carried forward, not written off.** The raw `--no-baseline` count on current `main` is **184 T010 BLOCKERs across 45 files** (225 findings total), not 88 — #490 wired 55 previously-orphaned test files into build targets, bringing far more files into scope. All 184 are recorded in the baseline; none are newly escaping it. Retiring them by migrating fixtures to `TestHarness.newUuid()` (added by ISS-0121/[#387](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/387) for exactly this collision class) is filed as **ISS-0181/[#512](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/512)**. It is a 45-file mechanical change that must be validated by running suites which currently carry unrelated known failures (#482, #479, #427, #424, #423, #418, #417) that would mask regressions — bundling it here would have produced an unreviewable diff. A subset are genuine shared system constants (e.g. `…0001` as the platform-admin actor, 13 occurrences) that must stay fixed and remain baselined with a written reason.
- **A genuine new gate-integrity defect was found while verifying the old one: ISS-0180/[#511](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/511).** C3 runs a bare `zig build migrate`, which resolves **`BPM_DB_URL`** (dev); C4 always verifies **`BPM_TEST_DB_URL`** (test). When the two differ — the normal configuration — C3 reports PASS having migrated a database no test uses. That is a **false green on a hard gate**: it was observed live in this run as C3 PASS and C4 FAIL(delta=−2) in the same invocation, with C3's remedy text ("resolve the migration failure above") misdirecting, because no migration had failed. Filed and queued, not fixed here: changing a gate's own definition belongs in its own run with its own review.
- Diagnosis with full evidence: `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0152-diagnosis.yaml`. No requirement in `docs/requirements.yaml` maps to this change — it is test-infrastructure tooling, outside the product requirement set (`ENV-01`/`ENV-02` concern tenant-level isolation, not test-fixture isolation) — so no requirement status was moved.

### Fixed

**ISS-0170 — CI could not tell a GitHub Actions outage apart from a real failure** ([GitHub #497](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/497))

- **Run colour was the only signal, and run colour conflates two opposite things.** A platform outage renders on a PR as a red X visually identical to a failed test. On 2026-08-06 the Actions component went to `major_outage` at 15:22:49Z with webhook triggers throttled — runs stopped being created, queued jobs were never assigned a runner, and were cancelled ~15 minutes later. Of the last 40 runs, 31 succeeded, 6 were cancelled and 3 failed; **only those 3 were genuine code defects**, but nothing in the pipeline distinguished them. The outage was identified only because a human thought to open githubstatus.com by hand.
- **The root cause was a missing mechanical source of platform-state truth**, not a missing capacity setting. The attribution step existed solely as an unwritten habit, so it was skipped. That is also how this issue was *itself* first misdiagnosed: it was originally filed as "runner starvation" purely from runner-assignment evidence, and had to be corrected once the status page was finally read — the remedy changed from capacity tuning to outage attribution. The correction is recorded in `docs/issues/ISS-0170.json`.
