- **What changed.** `tools/check_github_status.py` and the `platform_status` job already existed from [#499](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/499); this closes the remainder rather than duplicating them. The `::warning::` annotation now names the incident's **start time**, impact and shortlink — naming the start time is the part that lets a reader correlate "my job was cancelled at T" with "the incident opened at T−x". A new `--summary` mode writes a durable section to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, because an annotation scrolls away with the log while the step summary is the surface reached from the PR checks tab.
- **The diagnostic is duplicated into the `build` job on purpose.** `platform_status` is a separate job on its own runner, so its annotation lands on a page nobody opens when `build` is the one showing red — and during the precise failure mode at issue (webhook throttling, jobs never assigned a runner) that sibling job is itself liable to be cancelled or never scheduled, losing the diagnostic exactly when it is needed. The pre-flight is therefore attached to the job whose red X actually gets investigated.
- **This is the mirror image of ISS-0171/[#498](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/498), and was built so as not to become it.** That defect ran the opposite way: `continue-on-error` **hid real failures** for months, letting a step log `##[error]Process completed with exit code 1` while the job reported `success`. The two failure modes are symmetric — one made platform noise look like a defect, the other made a defect look like success — so the fix for one must not create the other. The outage signal may *annotate* a failure and may **never suppress** one: `main()` returns 0 unconditionally, so the check cannot fail a build, and it is deliberately not wired to skip, retry or neutralise anything. Gating CI on platform status and auto-neutralising jobs during an incident were both considered in the diagnosis and **rejected** for exactly this reason.
- **Proof that nothing is masked, obtained programmatically rather than by reading.** An audit over every step in `ci.yml` confirms all **15 gating steps carry `continue-on-error=None`**. The only `|| true` introduced guards the diagnostic's own network read of a third-party status page — a step that runs no build, no test and no linter, and therefore has no verdict available to hide. Its purpose is to stop githubstatus.com being down from reddening a PR by itself, which would be a fresh instance of the very confusion this issue removes.
- **CLAUDE.md now makes the status-API query mandatory**, not advisory, before characterising any red or cancelled run, with a fourth outcome row for the unchecked case: *"you have not run it — you cannot report yet."* It also states explicitly that an open incident never converts a genuine failure into an acceptable one: a degraded platform explains a job that was cancelled or never started, and explains nothing about a step that ran and failed.
- **Verification:** live API returns `Actions=operational` at exit 0; an **injected `major_outage` payload** shaped like the real 2026-08-06 incident emits the annotation naming the incident and its 15:22:49Z start time, still at exit 0 — so the degraded path is exercised, not merely assumed reachable. `ci.yml` parses under `yaml.safe_load`; `zig build` exit 0; `lint_agent_docs.py` 0 findings.
- No requirement in `docs/requirements.yaml` maps to this change — it is CI harness observability, outside the product requirement set — so no requirement status was moved.

**ISS-0175 / ISS-0177 — a fresh clone could not build at all** ([GitHub #503](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/503), [GitHub #506](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/506))

- **`vendor/luajit/src/luajit_relver.txt` was git-ignored while being a required build input.** `vendor/luajit/src/.gitignore` is inherited verbatim from upstream LuaJIT, where the file is *generated* by the upstream Makefile. This repo replaced that Makefile with `vendor/luajit/build.zig`, which **consumes** the file (`genversion.addFileArg(...luajit_relver.txt)`) but never produces it. The file was therefore simultaneously required-as-input and excluded-from-version-control. Any checkout without a stale local copy failed before compiling anything with `error: failed to check cache: '.../luajit_relver.txt' file_hash FileNotFound` — an error that never names its own cause.
- **This was breaking CI, not merely theoretical.** `zig build test` depends on the Lua test binary, so every CI run on `main` failed at this step; the maintainer's own checkout built fine only because it held an untracked copy from an earlier local build. The fix **tracks the file**, which is what upstream itself intends: upstream's Makefile falls back to a tracked `../.relver` for non-git consumers, i.e. the relver is expected to travel with the source when the git-derived path is unavailable. Generating it from `build.zig` was the alternative and was rejected — it would re-derive at build time a value upstream already treats as a shippable constant.
- **`dynasm.lua` interpolated an unescaped absolute Windows path into a C string literal.** `vendor/luajit/dynasm/dynasm.lua:88` emitted `#line` directives by concatenating the input filename straight into a C literal. `build.zig` passes a `b.path(...)`, which Zig resolves to an **absolute** path — so on Windows every `\` separator became a C escape sequence. Fixed by escaping backslashes and double quotes; a strictly-correct quoting change that is a **no-op on POSIX paths**, which contain neither character.
- **Whether this broke your build depended only on the letters after the backslashes** — which is why it hid for so long. The maintainer's path yielded `\2 \R \v \s` (tolerated); a genuine clone into a path containing `\claude` and `\AppData` yielded `\c` and `\U`, producing **77 `unknown escape sequence` errors**. Same unescaped output in both cases; only luck differed. This is the works-on-my-machine class in its purest form.
- **Verified against a real `git clone`, not a `git worktree`.** The distinction mattered: ISS-0177 was originally suspected to be a worktree artifact, and only a true clone settled it. After both fixes, the fresh clone runs `zig build test-lua` to completion — **71/71**. The primary checkout shows no regression: `zig build test` **936/1000 passed, 0 failed**.
- ISS-0177 was found and fixed **inside** this run under the Unblock-Everything directive, because it blocked the fresh-clone verification that was ISS-0175's entire acceptance criterion — the two defects masked each other.
- **The follow-up is filed, not silently absorbed.** Both defects were invisible precisely because no CI job builds from a *clean* checkout — CI caches the workspace, so a file that exists only locally looks tracked. That gap is ISS-0178/[#507](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/507) and is queued, not fixed here.

**ISS-0179 — LuaJIT requested the system unwinder but never linked one, breaking every Linux build** ([GitHub #509](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/509))

- **Fixing ISS-0175 exposed a third defect that had been unreachable behind it.** With the relver error gone, CI got as far as *linking* for the first time and failed with 8 undefined symbols — `_Unwind_RaiseException`, `__register_frame` and friends — all referenced by `libluajit.a(lj_err.o)`.
- **Cause: a macro was set without its link input.** `vendor/luajit/build.zig` compiled the core with `-DLUAJIT_UNWIND_EXTERNAL` unconditionally, which switches `lj_err.c` from LuaJIT's free-standing internal unwinder to the *system* one. On Windows the CRT supplies those symbols, so it built. On Linux they live in `libgcc_eh`/`libunwind`, which `link_libc` does not pull in — so nothing provided them.
- **The macro was not removed.** Upstream enables it on Linux deliberately, gated on a probe that the toolchain emits unwind tables. Removing it would have made the linker stop complaining by changing what was being built; the real defect was a *missing link input*. The fix supplies the unwinder (and adds `-funwind-tables`, which upstream treats as the precondition, since EXT unwinding needs tables on every C frame between throw and catch — not just in `lj_err.c`).
- **Pre-existing since [#496](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/496)/ISS-0161, confirmed not caused here** by diffing `vendor/luajit/build.zig` against `origin/main` — this run had not touched the file. It survived because every prior CI run died earlier at ISS-0175. Fixed in-run under Unblock-Everything, since it blocked this run's own criterion of turning CI green.

### Security

**ISS-0169 tranche 1 — LUA-05/06/07** ([GitHub #495](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/495) — Lua capability enforcement did not exist; every host function ran unchecked)

- **The capability gate now actually fires. Before this change it did not fire at all.** Every `src/lua/host_api/*` `register()` discarded its `ExecutionContext` with `_ = context;` and installed a 0-upvalue closure, so at call time no `CapabilitySet` was reachable from inside any `lua_CFunction` — and consequently **no capability check ran anywhere in the subsystem**. This was verified as a live security hole rather than inferred from reading: an **empty** `CapabilitySet` permitted all gated `platform.*` calls. A sandboxed script had unconditional access to variable reads and writes, service calls, logging, event emission and instance state regardless of what its manifest declared. LUA-06 was an *absent security control*, not a missing test.
- **Mechanism — the context reaches the callee through `LUA_REGISTRYINDEX`.** The new `src/lua/host_context.zig` stores the `*ExecutionContext` as light userdata in the Lua registry at state-creation time and reads it back inside each host function; `requireCapability` then checks the declared `CapabilitySet` before the function does any work. All 6 gated `platform.*` functions call it. `call_service` is gated per service id, not merely per family.
- **Fail-closed is the rule, and it is tested.** An absent or unreadable registry entry denies the call — it does not fall through to permissive behaviour. Denials raise a structured Lua error naming the function, the capability required, and the set actually granted, so a denied script says *why* it was denied.
- **Verified by mutation, not by observation.** Neutering `requireCapability` turns exactly the capability tests red and nothing else; restoring it returns the suite green. The validator independently re-ran two mutations of its own — inverting an assertion, and making `requireCapability` fail-*open* on an absent context — and confirmed each turned the expected blocks red and no others. This distinguishes a load-bearing gate from a gate-shaped no-op, which is precisely the ISS-0153/ISS-0155 failure mode this issue exists to correct.
- **`luaopen_base` ordering invariant (SBX-1).** `loadSafeStdlib` never called `luaopen_base`, so `pairs`, `pcall`, `error`, `type`, `setmetatable` and `_G` were absent from any script run through `executeScript` — which both made realistic scripts unrunnable and made `stdlib.zig`'s `load`/`loadstring`/`loadfile` removals no-ops on that path (there was nothing there to remove). The order is now **open base, then prune**, and it is an invariant: pruning before opening silently un-prunes. Product and test state creation were unified behind a single `createSandboxedState` (SBX-2) so the two paths cannot drift apart again — the drift is what hid this.
- **The manifest hash now binds the script source (LUA-07, partial).** `manifest.zig` digests the script source together with its capabilities, sorted and `0x00`-separated so no capability list can be re-partitioned into a colliding pre-image. Added `parseManifest`, a constant-time `verifyManifestHash`, and `executeScriptWithManifest`, which rejects a hash mismatch **before any Lua state is created**. Fixed a leak in `ScriptManifest.deinit`.
- **Two files that never compiled now compile.** `instruction_limiter.zig` discarded `lua_sethook`'s `c_int`; `memory_limiter.zig` used `std.Thread.Mutex`, removed in Zig 0.16. Both were invisible because `src/lua_test_root.zig` pinned them with type-only references that force neither field-type resolution nor function-body analysis (that pinning defect is itself filed as ISS-0172/[#500](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/500)). **No limiter is installed by this change** — LUA-08/09/10 are tranche 2, and claiming them here would repeat the pattern being fixed.
- **Verification:** `zig build` exit 0. `zig build test-lua` **71/71**. `zig build test` **936/1000 passed, 0 failed**, 81/81 steps. Baselined against a clean worktree of `main` (`b3d406d`, 865/929): a per-binary diff shows exactly one difference — this branch adds one binary of 71 tests — and **every pre-existing binary reports an identical pass count on both sides**, so there are **zero regressions**. The 64 skips are pre-existing and identical on `main`. All 18 new blocks in `src/lua/capability_enforcement_test.zig` execute; no `error.SkipZigTest` anywhere in `src/lua/`.
- **Status set deliberately, and not further:** **LUA-05 and LUA-06 → RELEASED**. **LUA-07 stays `IN_PROGRESS`** — its first criterion (the hash binds source and capabilities) is met in `src/lua/`, but its second (the hash appears in the execution audit record) requires the **engine** to write the audit row, which is outside this tranche's scope. Marking LUA-07 RELEASED on the strength of the half that is done is exactly the ISS-0153 pattern this issue exists to correct, so it is not marked. The engine-side write is filed as ISS-0176/[#504](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/504).
- **LUA-08..16 remain `IN_PROGRESS`** and are unclaimed. ISS-0169 stays OPEN for tranches 2–4 (limiters; stateful host API; time/events).
- Design artefact: `src/design/lua-capability-enforcement.md`. Incidental defects found while doing this work were filed and forwarded rather than fixed here: ISS-0172/[#500](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/500), ISS-0173/[#501](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/501), ISS-0174/[#502](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/502), ISS-0175/[#503](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/503).

### Added

**ISS-0161** ([GitHub #485](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/485) — LuaJIT was not available in this repo, so `src/lua` executed no scripts)

- **LuaJIT 2.1 is now vendored and statically linked (LUA-01).** `vendor/luajit/build.zig` reimplements the upstream three-stage bootstrap (`minilua` → `genversion`+`dynasm` → `buildvm`) as `build.zig` steps. Two toolchain blockers had to be solved, both diagnosed empirically rather than assumed:
  - **The upstream Makefile cannot drive this build here.** GNU Make 3.81 fails with `CreateProcess(NULL, host/minilua.exe ...) / e=2` when invoking a just-built relative-path executable on Windows; the identical command run directly from a shell succeeds. Driving the bootstrap from `build.zig`, which invokes artifacts by absolute path, sidesteps it.
  - **`buildvm` must compile with UBSan off.** DynASM's `dasm_x86.h:143` does pointer arithmetic on a NULL `buf` during setup; Zig's clang traps it, so `buildvm` panicked and generated nothing. `-fno-sanitize=undefined` restores upstream C semantics.
- **The ISS-0153 stubs are gone.** `src/lua/luajit_bindings.zig` now declares the real symbols, plus `inline fn` wrappers for the parts of the Lua 5.1 API that are **C macros** (`lua_pop`, `lua_newtable`, `lua_tostring`, `lua_getglobal`/`lua_setglobal`, the `lua_is*` family) and therefore have no symbol to link against. `has_real_luajit` is now `true`. **No caller in `src/lua/` changed** — ISS-0153 kept the stub signatures faithful to the LuaJIT 2.1 ABI precisely so this would be a drop-in, and it was.
- **A live sandbox escape was found and fixed.** Linking a real interpreter immediately exposed that **`loadfile` survived `loadSafeStdlib`**: `luaopen_base` installs it as a global, and `stdlib.zig` removed `load`/`loadstring`/`dofile` but not `loadfile` — so a sandboxed script could read and compile an arbitrary file from disk, defeating both LUA-03 (no filesystem reach) and LUA-04 (the bytecode gate, since `loadfile` loads bytecode files happily). This was undetectable for as long as the bindings were stubs, because no script could run to observe which globals actually survived. The fix is mutation-checked: reverting the one-line change makes `TC-LUA-03-08..12` fail.
- **Three further latent bugs surfaced** the moment real compilation reached this code: `executor.zig` could not describe `OutOfMemory` (it is not in `LuaError`, so an allocation failure would have been mislabelled as a script error); `host_api/call_service.zig` still called `std.fmt.fmtSliceHexLower`, removed in Zig 0.16, having not compiled since 0.10.
- **Verification:** `zig build test-lua` 15/15 steps, 25/25 tests; `zig build test` 81/81 steps, **890 passed, 0 failed** (count rose 865 → 954 because the real bindings pulled previously-dead code into compilation). LUA-01's static-linking requirement checked directly — a static `luajit.lib` is produced and the linked binary contains **zero** Lua DLL references. The headline test executes `return 6 * 7` and reads back 42, which is impossible against the stubs.
- **LUA-01, LUA-02, LUA-03, LUA-04 restored to RELEASED** with evidence in `src/lua/execution_test.zig`. **LUA-05..16 remain IN_PROGRESS** — they are executable for the first time but not yet verified, and are forwarded as [#495](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/495)/ISS-0169 rather than being claimed. Given that the first sandbox rule ever actually exercised turned out to be broken, claiming the other twelve without tests would be unwarranted.

### Fixed

**ISS-0162** ([GitHub #486](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/486) — `entity_subsystem_test` flaky against the shared `TestHarness`: two cross-binary races)

- **Race B — the cause of both observed failures — is a lock *scope* defect, not a missing lock.** `TestHarness.init()` held the `bpm_test_migrations_public` advisory lock across migrations → `resetTestData()` → `ensureDefaultOidcSeeds()`, then **explicitly released it** before `conn.begin()`. That made init mutually exclusive only with *other inits*. `resetTestData()` issues unconditional `DELETE`s against `instance_projections`, `events`, `process_definitions` and nine other tables in the shared `tenant_default` schema that **every** integration binary provisions — so binary B's init deleted binary A's fixture rows while A's test was still running. A's per-test transaction does not protect it: it is READ COMMITTED, so each statement sees B's committed deletes. That produced both reported signatures — `StoreError.InstanceNotFound` (the synthetic entity-type instance vanishes) and `expect(r2.is_duplicate)` failing (the idempotency row vanishes, so the replay is no longer a duplicate). **Fix:** hold the lock for the whole harness lifetime — acquire in `init()`, release in `deinit()` after `rollback()` and before `close()`.
- **Race A — the unguarded ledger write.** `src/db/migrations.zig` was the **only** writer to `public.schema_migrations` without `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; `src/tools/migrate.zig`, `tests/integration/helpers.zig`, and both inserts in `GBL-133_*.sql` all have one. The ISS-0144 recheck-under-lock already closes the check-then-act window, but the bare `INSERT` turned any residual race into a **binary-wide cascade**: one `C23505` aborts the transaction, and every later `TestHarness.init()` then fails with `error.OutOfOrderMigration` — one race appearing as five test failures. Added the conflict guard as defence in depth.
- **Reproduced before fixing, verified after.** Serial runs were already stable (6/6 clean at 12/12) — the races are cross-binary and only appear under concurrency. Three concurrent `test-integration-exp` invocations reproduced both signatures immediately (**10/12, 11/12**, 12/12). After the fix: three concurrent runs **all 12/12 with zero race signatures**, and a 4-way stress run all 12/12 in 18s with no `55P03` lock timeouts.
- **Cost, stated plainly:** integration binaries sharing `tenant_default` now serialize fully rather than only through init. They were never safe to overlap; the narrow window only made it look that way. The existing 90s `lock_timeout` bracket already absorbs the queueing.
- Pre-existing failures in `test-integration-iss0602-cross` (2 pass/1 fail) and `test-integration-tm` (3 pass/11 fail) were measured **identically with and without this change** — they belong to the [#482](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/482)/ISS-0150 family of known mass integration failures on `main` and are untouched here.
- Design artefact: `src/design/iss0162_test_harness_cross_binary_races.md`.

**ISS-0163** ([GitHub #489](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/489) — dangling `implemented_in` references in `docs/requirements.yaml`; `reqctl validate` never checked them)

- **Writer bug fixed.** `reqctl set-status` routed `--implemented-in` straight into the YAML entry with no type coercion, so a programmatic caller passing a bare `str` stored a string where a list belongs — any consumer iterating it then yielded one character per entry. That is the origin of `ISS-204`/`ISS-206`'s character-split values. `cmd_set_status` now routes through `coerce_path_list()`.
- **Detection added.** `reqctl validate` now checks every `implemented_in` entry: it must resolve to a real path on disk and must not be a run/branch identifier. Reported **BLOCKER** for `RELEASED` requirements (a released requirement pointing at nothing is the harm this class causes), **MAJOR** otherwise. This is what makes the class non-recurring.
- **41 dangling references repaired**: 17 renamed migrations repointed (`GBL-105`→`133`, `GBL-081`→`120`, `GBL-082`→`121`), 8 fabricated source paths repointed to the real implementing files, 10 run-IDs moved out of `implemented_in` into `note` (they record *where the work happened*, not *what implements the requirement*), 7 frontend paths dropped, 1 stray `" (inferred)"` suffix removed. `reqctl validate` now reports **0** `implemented_in` findings and **0** BLOCKERs (was 43).
- **Regression cover**: new `reqctl selftest` subcommand exercises the coercion and run-ID detection. It was mutation-checked — reintroducing the writer bug makes it exit 1 — rather than merely observed to pass.
- **Two corrections to the filed report**, both verified rather than assumed:
  - The **"103 dangling references"** figure does not reproduce at any point in git history: 41 at the commit where this fix began, 44 before PR #490. The three root causes described in the issue are all real and were each confirmed independently; only the count was wrong.
  - The paths the issue described as *"genuinely deleted/moved source files"* (`src/instance/store.zig`, `src/scheduler/timer.zig`, `src/api/routes/repository.zig`, several `web/src/components/tasks/*.tsx`) have **zero commits in git history** — they never existed and were fabricated at write time. They were therefore dropped or repointed at the real implementing file, not traced to a successor. The one genuine move was `web/src/pages/LoginPage.tsx` (6 commits, removed in `8e7ea86`).
- Requirement bodies, titles, statuses, and IDs are byte-identical; the change touches only `implemented_in` and `note`. `docs/status/requirement_status.yaml` regenerated via `reqctl render-status`.

**ISS-0137** ([GitHub #439](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/439) — 55 test-bearing `.zig` files were reachable from no `b.addTest(...)` root; ~350 test blocks had never executed) — plus 12 further defects drained from this run's issue queue

- **Closes GitHub #439**: all 55 unreachable test-bearing files are now wired into a build target. `zig build test` executed test blocks went from **701 → 865 (+164)**, measured by TEST-RUNNER before and after on the same machine.
- **Root cause — three independent mechanisms**, not one:
  1. Production files exposed only as *named modules* in `build.zig`, with no `addTest` root importing them, so their in-file `test` blocks compiled into nothing.
  2. Integration test files physically present under `tests/integration/` but absent from `tests/integration/main_test.zig`'s `_ = @import(...)` list — the only thing that pulls them into the umbrella binary.
  3. Test files importing modules that `build.zig` declared for no test artifact at all, so the file could not be wired without first declaring its dependency.
- **Recurrence prevention**: `tools/lint_test_wiring.py` now exits 0 and is a **dependency of `zig build test`** — a newly-added test file that is reachable from no root fails the build rather than silently never running. The linter was also extended to verify that every test `Run` artifact is attached to an aggregate build step; previously a binary could be *built* and never *executed*, which was invisible to both the old linter and to `zig build test`'s summary. That extension is what surfaced ISS-0157 below.
- **Three real production bugs were found only because these tests started running** (ISS-0149, ISS-0155, ISS-0156). This is the substantive result of the change: the wiring fix is mechanical, but ~350 previously-dark test blocks immediately failed against real code, and those failures were genuine.

The following 13 issues were filed on GitHub, enqueued onto this run's `handoffs/WF03-gh439-testwiring-20260806/issue_queue.json`, and drained on this branch per `docs/agents/protocols/ISSUE_QUEUE.md`:

- **ISS-0147** ([GitHub #463](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/463)) — `src/wasm/` (12 files) was reachable from no `addTest` root and re-exported by neither `bpm.zig` nor `main.zig`, raising the question of whether it was dead code. Judged **LIVE**: 14 `RELEASED` requirements name it in `implemented_in`. Re-exported via `bpm.zig`, wired into a test root, and its Zig 0.16 rot fixed (`callconv(.C)` → `callconv(.c)`).
- **ISS-0148** ([GitHub #464](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/464)) — `tests/unit/event_store_test.zig` consisted of 25 `error.SkipZigTest` stubs, so ES-01..ES-08 had zero executing unit coverage. Replaced with 23 real tests.
- **ISS-0149** ([GitHub #465](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/465)) — 33 pre-existing `tests/integration` failures across 10 files, newly visible once the files were wired. **Three were real production defects, not test bugs**: (a) `src/engine/`'s `freeOwnedTransitionState` leaked on *every* instance create and advance; (b) `PARALLEL_GATEWAY` split leaked 4 allocations per split; (c) migrations GBL-125/126/127 were recorded as applied in `schema_migrations` but their bodies never ran, because the guard condition they were wrapped in always evaluated NULL.
- **ISS-0150** ([GitHub #466](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/466)) — ENV-01's 9 test blocks reported green while asserting nothing. Given real assertions; `entity_subsystem_test` attached to a build step.
- **ISS-0151** ([GitHub #468](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/468)) — `zig build test-env-verify` check C3 failed on `BPM_DB_URL` even though `.env` defined it; the check read the process environment only. Now resolves from `.env`.
- **ISS-0152** ([GitHub #469](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/469)) — `tools/lint_test_isolation.baseline.json` had 97 line-number-stale entries. Re-synced by line number only; the identity multiset of suppressed findings is provably unchanged (no finding newly suppressed, none dropped).
- **ISS-0153** ([GitHub #471](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/471)) — `src/lua/` (14 files) no longer compiled under Zig 0.16 (8 distinct rot categories) and was referenced by no build target. Rot fixed and the subsystem wired. **LuaJIT was then proven unavailable in this repo** — nothing links it — so the stubs return failure sentinels rather than pretending to execute scripts. Consequently **LUA-01..LUA-16 were downgraded `RELEASED` → `IN_PROGRESS`**: they had been marked released while backed by nothing executable. Linking LuaJIT is an infrastructure decision, filed separately as [#485](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/485).
- **ISS-0154** ([GitHub #472](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/472)) — 5 ES-01/event-store test cases had no coverage at either level. Writing them found and fixed a real defect: `Store.archive()` returned the count of *policies applied* where its contract (and every caller) expected the count of *rows moved*.
- **ISS-0155** ([GitHub #473](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/473)) — **ES-05 schema validation was a no-op**. `Registry.validatePayload` accepted the registered JSON Schema as a parameter and discarded it without ever validating against it, and `getType` returned a hardcoded `"{}"` placeholder rather than the stored schema — so every payload passed, including ones the registered schema forbids. Real validation implemented, reusing the existing `src/tools/json_schema.zig` validator rather than adding a second one.
- **ISS-0156** ([GitHub #475](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/475)) — **BLOCKER**: entity record create/update/delete was entirely broken against a real database. `getOrCreateEntityType` never minted the `instance_projections` row that every downstream read path joins against, so entity records were unreachable after creation. Fixed, with backfill migration `1137` for rows already written in the broken state.
- **ISS-0157** ([GitHub #476](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/476)) — 2 test binaries were built but attached to no aggregate step, so they were compiled and never executed (found by the linter extension described above). Attached; the `KNOWN_UNATTACHED` ledger is now empty.
- **ISS-0159 / ISS-0160** ([GitHub #480](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/480), [#481](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/481)) — `createRecord` ignored its own idempotency replay result and looked up the wrong row on duplicate; 3 allocation leaks; and record payload validation was an unimplemented `TODO`, making the declared `InvalidPayload` error variant unreachable. All fixed.

**Verification and its limits** — stated precisely, because the distinction matters:
- `zig build test` is **green**: exit 0, 865 executed test blocks (up from 701), no leaks reported.
- `python tools/lint_test_wiring.py` exits 0 — 254 test-bearing files, 73 `addTest` roots, all reachable, every Run artifact attached to a step.
- `zig build test-integration` is **NOT fully green**, and this change does not claim to make it so. The residual failures are the pre-existing cross-binary harness races already tracked as [GitHub #366](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/366) and [GitHub #454](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/454), plus the three deferred issues below. No new integration failure class was introduced by this run.

**Deferred — filed, cross-linked, and left OPEN on purpose** (each needs a decision this run had no authority to make):
- [GitHub #479](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/479) (ISS-0158) — 14 `effects_subsystem_test` blocks covering EXP-301/EXP-302 fail because `transition()`'s event-emission contract is genuinely ambiguous. Requires a design decision, not a code fix.
- [GitHub #485](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/485) (ISS-0161) — LuaJIT is not linked anywhere in this repo, so `src/lua` executes no scripts and LUA-01..16 cannot leave `IN_PROGRESS`. Linking it is an infrastructure decision.
- [GitHub #486](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/486) (ISS-0162) — `entity_subsystem_test` is flaky against the shared `TestHarness` under concurrent binaries. Same class as #366/#454 and belongs with them, not here.

**Requirement status changes made by this run:**
- **LUA-01..LUA-16**: `RELEASED` → `IN_PROGRESS` (see ISS-0153 above — they were released while backed by code that did not compile and links no Lua runtime).
- **ES-05**: remains `RELEASED`; `implemented_in` extended with `src/tools/json_schema.zig` now that validation is real rather than a no-op.
