No other requirement's status was altered — `docs/status/requirement_status.yaml` was regenerated via `reqctl.py render-status`, never hand-edited.

**Incidental finding, filed and deferred (out of scope for this run)**: [GitHub #489](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/489) (ISS-0163) — ENV-01's stale reference turned out to be one of **103 dangling `implemented_in` entries** across `docs/requirements.yaml`, and `tools/reqctl.py validate` never checks those paths at all. Three causes: leftover `GBL-105`-era migration names (same class as [#337](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/337)/[#458](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/458), which each fixed only one consumer); run-IDs written where file paths belong; and a bare string passed where a `list[str]` was expected, splitting `feature/WF02-iss204-206-20260611` into one-character entries for `ISS-204`/`ISS-206`. Fixing it is a registry-wide sweep plus a new validator check, unrelated to test wiring.

- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh439-testwiring-20260806`. Issues: `docs/issues/ISS-0137.json` and `ISS-0147`–`ISS-0163`.

**ISS-0148** ([GitHub #477](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/477) — `TC-TNT-01-01` fails intermittently with `42P01 relation "audit_entries" does not exist` during tenant schema provisioning) and **ISS-0151** ([GitHub #483](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/483) — all three `pg_advisory_lock` acquires were deleted from `tests/integration/helpers.zig` while the matching `pg_advisory_unlock` was left behind)
- **Closes GitHub #477 and #483**: `zig build test-integration-tnt` now exits 0 deterministically. Two independent defects shared the one symptom; both had to be fixed before the step went green.
- **ISS-0148 root cause** (confirmed by DDL-level PostgreSQL server logs, not inference): `build.zig` declared `clean_test_db.step` and the integration run artifacts as **sibling** dependencies of the same aggregating step. Zig imposes no ordering edge between siblings, so `tools/clean_test_db.py` was free to run *concurrently with* the test binaries it was meant to clean up for. Its orphan sweep enumerates every schema matching `tenant\_%` by name shape alone — it cannot distinguish a leaked schema from one a live test is actively migrating — and issued `DROP SCHEMA ... CASCADE` against an in-flight schema mid-migration. `Migrations.runForSchema()` then kept applying the remaining files against a schema that no longer existed. Because the drop landed at a random point in the ~80-file per-tenant sequence, **both the failing migration file and the named missing relation varied run to run** (observed files 028/040/050_xc01/081/094; observed relations `events`, `users`, `audit_entries`, `webhook_subscriptions`, `process_definitions`, `user_roles`, `event_type_registry`) — which is exactly the variability that had made the report look unreproducible. Smoking gun: a `psql`-issued (simple-protocol) `DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS tenant_37630918... CASCADE` on backend 57413 logged at 09:46:46.691 UTC while backend 57140 — the migration runner, extended protocol — was still applying migration 081 to that same schema; exactly one such DROP occurred in the run and it targeted precisely the schema that failed.
- **ISS-0148 fix, layer 1 (primary)** — `build.zig`: new file-local `addIntegrationRun()` helper folds the repeated create/`setCwd`/`BPM_MIGRATIONS_DIR` shape and attaches `clean_test_db` as a true ordering **predecessor**, applied at all 43 integration run-artifact sites. The edge attaches to the run **artifact**, not the aggregating step: 30 binaries reach the umbrella only via the `test-integration-others-internal` barrier, which carries no `clean_test_db` edge, so a per-step fix would have missed them — `dependOn` edges are a global property of a `Step`, so an artifact edge holds on every path. This also closes a pre-existing gap where `test-integration-iss0076` had **no cleanup edge at all**. All 56 step names and every pre-existing `dependOn` edge preserved verbatim; the ISS-0106 barrier is intact.
- **ISS-0148 fix, layer 2 (defence in depth)** — `tools/clean_test_db.py`: the orphan sweep now performs enumerate → validate → drop → ledger-delete as **one transaction in one `psql` session** (new `run_psql_script()` helper), holding `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('bpm.migrations.runForSchema')::bigint)` — the key taken verbatim from `src/db/migrations.zig:31` — with **enumeration performed under the lock**. The pre-existing `run_psql` spawns a fresh process per call, so a lock taken in one call is already gone by the next; a naive "lock, then drop" would have protected nothing. The sweep blocks rather than skips. The `^tenant_[0-9a-f]{32}$` injection guard moves server-side alongside the enumeration, with `format(%I)` quoting.
- **ISS-0151 root cause** (found by TEST-RUNNER during verification of the above, drained on the same branch per the issue-queue protocol): commit `db362fd` ("fix(test-infra): remove advisory locks, fix database cleanup hang", GH-402, 2026-08-03) deleted **all three** `pg_advisory_lock` acquires from `tests/integration/helpers.zig` — `runMigrations()`'s `bpm_test_migrations_public` acquire, `runMigrationsForSchema()`'s per-schema `hashtext($1)` acquire, and the widened ISS-0107 acquire in `TestHarness.init()` — but **left the matching `SELECT pg_advisory_unlock(...)` in place**. For three months the harness released a lock nobody ever took, and every concurrent test binary ran its migration check-and-apply completely unguarded. The ISS-0107 comment block describing the bracket in detail **survived the deletion of the code it documented**, so the file read as if the lock were still there. The stated rationale — "rely on migration idempotency" — is false, and `src/db/migrations.zig` refutes it in its own ISS-0144 comment: `run`/`runForSchema` do check-then-apply against an `applied` set read *before* the per-file transaction begins, so two binaries can both observe a file as unapplied.
- **ISS-0151 fix** — restored all three acquires, each bracketed with the proven ISS-0107-rework-1 `lock_timeout` pattern (90s for the acquire, restored to 5s afterwards) and released via `defer`. The per-schema lock is keyed on `hashtext(schema_name)` so different tenant schemas stay parallel and only same-schema work serialises, and it is taken **before** the already-migrated fast path, because that read is the check half of the check-then-apply race. This does not reintroduce the hang GH-402 was reacting to: that hang was never the lock itself, but ISS-0110's already-diagnosed ambient `lock_timeout='5s'` from `configureSessionTimeouts()` cancelling the acquire with `55P03` once enough binaries queued — the 90s bracket is the proven fix for it and was already in use at one acquire site before `db362fd` removed it.
- **Two red herrings ruled out by measurement**, both of which had previously misdirected diagnosis: (a) the `23505` on `schema_migrations_schema_version_uq` is `TC-TNT-02-05`'s **deliberate** duplicate insert, which that test asserts must fail — it occurs exactly once per run on runs that exit 0; (b) the ISS-0148 advisory-locked sweep was *not* the source of the residual hang — `pg_locks` sampling during live hangs showed zero ungranted locks and zero advisory locks held, because the locks had been *deleted*, not contended. The failing artifact was identified by mapping the `.zig-cache` hash in the failed-command line to its source via the PDB: the `19/19 tests passed` line and the non-zero exit came from **different binaries**.
- **Verification** (judged on exit code only, independently re-measured by ORCH rather than taken from the fixing agent's report): `zig build test-integration-tnt` exit codes `0, 0, 0, 0, 0` across 5 consecutive runs, `8/8 steps succeeded`, `34/34 tests passed` (19 isolation + 15 backfill); total `42P01` occurrences 0 and the `audit_entries` signature 0 in every run; step duration stabilised at 43–47s (from 46–88s). Pre-fix control on this branch: `1, 1, 1, 1`. Clean-`main` control: `0, 1, 1` — confirming the flake pre-dated this run. The ISS-0148 sweep lock was proven non-no-op: with a competing session holding the same key for 12s, the sweep blocked, took 14s and exited 0. `zig build` exits 0 with no `error set` output; `zig build test` exits 0; `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py` exits 0; regression spot-checks `test-integration-iss101/iss102/iss107/iss0121/iss0072/iss0076` all exit 0.
- **No test was weakened, skipped, deleted, or given a retry or sleep; no timeout was raised to hide a hang.** No changes were made under `tests/` (beyond restoring the deleted locks in `helpers.zig`), `migrations/`, or `src/db/migrations.zig`.
- **Filed while verifying, deliberately NOT fixed here** (both proven pre-existing by control runs against unmodified `main`, both left open as their own GitHub issues and `QUEUED` in this run's `issue_queue.json`): [GitHub #482](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/482)/ISS-0150 — `zig build test-integration-svc` has 63 failing blocks across 34 files from a missing `tenant_<uuid>.schema_migrations` ledger; [GitHub #484](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/484)/ISS-0152 — `zig build test-env-verify` fails unconditionally on `main`.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh477-20260806`. Design: `src/design/iss0148_clean_test_db_ordering_and_lock.md`. Issues: `docs/issues/ISS-0148.json`, `docs/issues/ISS-0151.json`.

**ISS-0147** ([GitHub #374](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/374) — integration linter tests invoke the Windows Store Python alias instead of the repository interpreter)
- **Closes GitHub #374**: `TC-TNT-02-03` and `TC-TNT-02-04` in `tests/integration/tnt_schema_isolation_test.zig` no longer observe exit 49 in place of `tools/lint_migration_schema.py`'s real exit codes, and a full run contains no `Python was not found` output.
- **Root cause**: interpreter selection was by bare name — both call sites spawned `argv[0] == "python"`. On Windows that name has three resolutions on PATH (a real `Programs\Python312` interpreter, the `WindowsApps` App Execution Alias stub, and an `AppData\Local\Python\bin` shim), and the alias stub prints `Python was not found; run without arguments to install from the Microsoft Store...` and exits **49**. Which resolution wins depends on the PATH the spawning process inherits, which differs between an interactive shell and a `zig build`-spawned test binary — so name-based selection cannot be made deterministic by reordering names. [ISS-0138](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/440) fixed the sibling `python3` case in `build.zig` that way; that fix does not reach these call sites, because they live in a test binary and because `python` carries the same alias one name over.
- **Fix**: added `tests/support/python_interp.zig` (named module `python_interp`), which resolves an interpreter through an ordered candidate list — `$BPM_PYTHON`, repo `.venv`, `$VIRTUAL_ENV`, `python3`, `python` — where every candidate must first pass a **liveness probe**: `-c "import sys; sys.stdout.write('BPM_PY_OK')"` must exit 0 *and* emit the sentinel on stdout. The probe is what rejects the alias stub; checking the sentinel rather than only the exit code keeps it robust against any future stub that exits 0 while doing nothing. `$BPM_PYTHON` is authoritative: if set but unusable, resolution fails loudly with `error.BpmPythonOverrideUnusable` rather than silently sliding back to PATH — a soft override would reintroduce exactly the non-determinism being removed. An empty value is treated as unset, so a blank CI variable is not a hard failure.
- **Not a gate edit**: both tests' assertions (`expectEqual(1, exit_code)` and `expectEqual(0, exit_code)`) are unchanged. The fix changes only *which binary produces* `exit_code`, restoring the measurement of the linter rather than suppressing it.
- **Verification**: `TC-TNT-02-03`/`TC-TNT-02-04` pass across 3 consecutive `zig build test-integration-tnt` runs with 0 occurrences of `Python was not found`; forcing `BPM_PYTHON` to the WindowsApps stub is correctly rejected with a diagnostic naming the offending path. `zig build`, `zig build test` (including 4 new resolver unit tests in `tests/unit/python_interp_test.zig`, wired into `test_step` so they cannot report green while running nothing), `tools/lint_design_artefact.py`, `tools/lint_sql_param_types.py`, and `tools/lint_test_wiring.py` all exit 0. `tools/lint_test_isolation.py` MAJOR count on `tests/integration` drops 8 → 6.
- **Filed while verifying, not fixed here**: [GitHub #477](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/477)/ISS-0148 — `TC-TNT-01-01` flakes on `main` (`42P01` on a varying business table during tenant provisioning). Confirmed pre-existing (reproduced 3× on `main` with none of these changes present) and rooted in the migration runner rather than interpreter resolution; full diagnosis recorded on the issue.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh374-20260806`. Design: `src/design/python_interpreter_resolution.md`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0147.json`.

**ISS-0145** ([GitHub #457](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/457) — `tests/integration` files relying on ambient `tenant_context` ordering cause severe standalone `-svc`/`-env` failures)
- **Closes GitHub #457**: `zig build test-integration-svc` and `zig build test-integration-env` no longer fail ~291/543 tests with `C42P01 relation "<table>" does not exist` against `tenant_default`-scoped business tables.
- **Root cause**: `tenant_context` (`src/api/tenant_context.zig`) is process-wide threadlocal state. `Pool.acquire()`'s `applyRequestStorageRouting()` silently routes to `search_path TO public` when no tenant context is set — never erroring — so a test file that never calls `tenant_context.set()`/`.clear()` itself implicitly depends on whatever value the immediately-preceding test in `main_test.zig`'s fixed declaration order happened to leave behind. Invisible under the umbrella `test-integration` target (lucky ordering); severely visible when `-svc`/`-env` compile the same source into standalone binaries with a different preceding test.
- **Audit**: all 88 `tests/integration/*.zig` files with a local `makePool()` helper were checked for `tenant_context.set()`/`.clear()` calls. 56 never called it at all; 3 more (`env02_test.zig`, `env05_test.zig`, `iss0129_migration_run_advisory_lock_test.zig`) called it only after fixture-setup `pool.acquire()` calls had already run under ambient state. Of the 56, 3 were excluded after reachability verification against `build.zig`/`main_test.zig`: `iss0076_secrets_table_test.zig` is safe as written (its raw `Pool.init()` runs immediately after `helpers.TestHarness.init()`, which already sets tenant context correctly); `oidc31_end_to_end_auth_suite_test.zig` and `oidc08_claim_mapping_config_test.zig` are unreachable dead code, never compiled into any binary — same defect class already tracked by [GitHub #439](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/439), which lists both plus 5 more OIDC files this audit incidentally confirmed unreachable.
- **Fix**: applied the existing house convention — `bpm.api_tenant_context.set("00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000")` (or the file's own `tenant_context` alias) as the first statement in `makePool()`, before `Pool.init()` — to the remaining 49 files (46 confirmed + 3 partial). Mechanical, no new mechanism; matches the pattern already used by the 29 files that were already correct.
- **Verification**: `zig build test-integration-svc` and `zig build test-integration-env` each run 3 consecutive times post-fix — identical 476/543 passed (7 skipped, 59 failed, 1 crashed) on both targets, every run, with byte-identical failing-test-name sets in every pairwise diff. The remaining 59 stable failures are a separate, pre-existing class (assertion mismatches, allocation leaks) unrelated to tenant_context and out of scope here. A same-session `zig build test-integration` umbrella run showed only 3 comparable `C42P01` occurrences, all against per-test dynamically-provisioned tenant schemas (expected), confirming `-svc`/`-env` now match the umbrella's own low rate.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh457-20260806`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0145.json`.

**ISS-0146** ([GitHub #458](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/458) — `tools/verify_schema_baseline.py --auto-fix` references renamed migration GBL-105 (now GBL-133), auto-fix always fails and loops)
- **Closes GitHub #458**: `tools/verify_schema_baseline.py --check-tenants --auto-fix` no longer fails on a stale filename and no longer recurses unboundedly.
- **Root cause**: the script hardcoded the literal filename `GBL-105_iss0112_schema_ledger_reconcile.sql` at four sites (docstring, `auto_fix()`'s lookup path and both its success/failure messages, argparse help text, and a runtime status message). That file was renamed to `GBL-133_iss0112_schema_ledger_reconcile.sql` in commit `db362fd` (PR #405, 2026-08-03) as part of a mass GBL-0xx renumbering — same anti-pattern class as [GitHub #337](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/337)/ISS-0088. Because the lookup always failed, `main()` — which called itself unconditionally via `return main()` whenever `failures and args.auto_fix`, with no depth counter or attempt cap, and without checking whether `auto_fix()` actually succeeded — recursed until Python's call-stack limit, opening one new Postgres connection per recursion level. Manual reproduction during ISS-0144/GH#454 verification observed 5 repeated failing retry blocks in a single invocation and briefly exhausted Postgres connections (`FATAL: sorry, too many clients already`).
- **Fix**: added `resolve_reconcile_migration()`, which resolves the reconcile migration dynamically by glob (`*_iss0112_schema_ledger_reconcile.sql`) instead of a hardcoded literal, so it survives future renumbering. Replaced the unbounded recursive retry with a bounded loop (`MAX_AUTO_FIX_ATTEMPTS = 2`: one initial check plus at most one post-auto-fix recheck) and changed `auto_fix()` to return a status so the caller can distinguish "fix applied" from "fix failed, stop immediately."
- **Verification**: two independent manual repros against a deliberately-orphaned `public.tenant` row on `db_test` (BACKEND-DEV Step 3 and TEST-RUNNER Step 5) — `--auto-fix` resolved and applied `GBL-133_iss0112_schema_ledger_reconcile.sql` dynamically, exactly 2 bounded check attempts, no `GBL-105` reference anywhere in source or output, and `pg_stat_activity` connection count unchanged before/after (no leak).
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh458-20260806`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0146.json`.

**ISS-0144** ([GitHub #454](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/454) — `zig build test-integration`'s ~19-binary concurrent suite has widespread non-deterministic failures: schema drift, cross-binary races, ~163/156/107 failures across 3 baseline runs)
- **Root-caused and fixed four distinct, independently-confirmed mechanisms**:
  1. **Cross-binary schema-readiness gap** (same class as [GitHub #364](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/364)/ISS-0106 and [GitHub #366](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/366)/ISS-0107): 6 standalone `test-integration` binaries (`tnt_backfill_export_cleanup_test.zig`, `tenant_config_realm_test.zig`, `sch303_timer_dlq_test.zig`, `onboarding_realm_guard_test.zig`, `exp103_instance_waits_test.zig`) never called `TestHarness.init()` and had no guarantee migrations had finished applying in a sibling binary before querying `public`/`tenant_default` tables. Fixed by adding `tests/integration/helpers.zig::ensureSchemaReady()` — a lightweight, non-transactional variant of `TestHarness.init()`'s locked migration path — called from each affected binary's `makePool()`.
  2. **Migration-runner TOCTOU race** (newly diagnosed, not previously documented): `src/db/migrations.zig::Migrations.runForSchema()` read its "already applied" snapshot once, before acquiring the per-file advisory lock. Two concurrent callers targeting the same `schema_name` could both see a file as pending, both reach `BEGIN`, and the second (once granted the lock) would re-attempt the DDL and `INSERT`, producing the exact `C23505 schema_migrations_schema_version_uq already exists` from the original report. Fixed by re-checking `(schema_name, version)` under lock protection immediately before doing any work.
  3. **C4 schema-baseline leak** (confirmed live: 345 orphaned `public.tenant` rows, 20+ orphaned empty Postgres schemas): `src/db/provisioning.zig::provisionTenantSchema()`'s `public.tenant` insert (Step 6a) was never deleted by `tests/integration/tnt_schema_isolation_test.zig::cleanupTenant()`. Fixed by adding the missing `DELETE FROM public.tenant`.
  4. **Two flawed test assertions misdiagnosed as concurrency bugs**: `TC-TNT-03-01`/`TC-TNT-03-03` asserted SCHEMA-mode routing for tenant IDs that were never provisioned (fails deterministically even with zero concurrency); `TC-ISS-0602-cross-02` asserted zero cross-owner idle-in-transaction connections exist anywhere on the shared database, which is false by design under real concurrent execution (every `TestHarness` holds an open transaction for its whole test). Both rewritten to test what they actually intend.
- **Also fixed while reproducing** (recurrence of [GitHub #338](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/338)/ISS-0089): `svc01_service_catalog_scope_test.zig` had regressed back to unqualified `service_catalog` references that resolve via `search_path` to a stale `tenant_default` copy lacking the `scope`/`owner_tenant_id` columns — the exact `column "scope" does not exist` symptom named in the original report. Also fixed: `svc01`/`svc03`'s `TenantNotFound` failures (tests referenced a "pre-committed fixture tenant" that was never actually inserted); a stale pre-rename migration filename reference (`GBL-077_tnt07_rls_cleanup.sql` → `GBL-116_tnt07_rls_cleanup.sql`, same class as [GitHub #337](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/337)/ISS-0088); and a Windows-only `python3` vs `python` subprocess-spawn mismatch in two linter-invoking tests.
- **Determinism evidence**: `zig build test-integration-tnt` (34 tests) went from originally-flaky to exit 0 across 3 consecutive clean runs. `zig build test-integration` (the umbrella target) went from the original 163/156/107 failures to 124/85 across 2 consecutive post-fix runs, with the specific `svc01`/`svc03`/`C23505` signatures from the original report no longer present in most runs.
- **Not fully resolved — follow-up filed**: [GitHub #457](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/457) (ISS-0145) — a separate, pre-existing architectural fragility was found while validating: several `test-integration` files never call `tenant_context.set()`/`.clear()` themselves and rely on whatever thread-local state the immediately-preceding test (in `main_test.zig`'s fixed declaration order) left behind. This produces a much more severe failure pattern when the identical source is compiled standalone (`zig build test-integration-svc`/`-env`, ~291/543 failures) than under the actual `test-integration` umbrella target (2 occurrences of the same signature) — filed separately as it requires a file-by-file audit beyond this fix's minimal scope.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh454-20260806`. Issues: `docs/issues/ISS-0144.json` (partially resolved), `docs/issues/ISS-0145.json` (new, open).

**ISS-0141** ([GitHub #445](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/445) — `definition_retrieval_test.zig` `TC-PD-07-*` tests fail non-deterministically depending on `--seed`)
- **Closes GitHub #445**: `tests/unit/definition_retrieval_test.zig`'s `TC-PD-07-*` tests now pass deterministically regardless of concurrent test activity against the shared `BPM_TEST_DB_URL`.
- **Root cause (corrects the issue's original hypothesis)**: the original report assumed `--seed` was reordering test execution within the binary — it is not; Zig's `std.testing` runner executes `test` blocks in declaration order. The actual mechanism is genuine **cross-process** contention: every `TC-PD-07-*` fixture used a hardcoded literal name (e.g. `"TC-PD-07-01-def"`) and fixed version `"1.0.0"` against the shared test database. When two `zig build test` invocations run concurrently — reproduced by launching 3–5 truly parallel `zig build test-bpm-main` OS processes — the second process's `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (`src/definition/store.zig::create()`) silently no-ops on the `uq_definition_tenant_version (tenant_id, name, version)` unique constraint, returning `DuplicateNameVersion`. Separately, `activate()`'s deprecate step (`UPDATE process_definitions SET status='DEPRECATED' WHERE name=$1 AND status='ACTIVE'`) is scoped only by name, not by row id, so one process's `activate()` can deprecate a different process's freshly-activated row of the same name before that process's own `getActiveByName()` reads it back — reproducing the exact `DefinitionNotFound` signature originally reported for `TC-PD-07-01`.
- **Fix**: added a `uniqueName()` helper that appends a fresh UUID v4 (via `bpm.api_trace.generateUuidV4` — `std.crypto.random` no longer exists in Zig 0.16, and this file compiles against `src/main.zig`, which does not re-export the separate `uuid` module used by `tests/integration/helpers.zig`) to every `TC-PD-07-*` fixture name, following the same per-test-isolation pattern as `TestHarness.newUuid`/`newUuidString`.
- **Verification**: 6 consecutive `zig build test` runs (full 38-step suite, 617/701 passing, 0 failures each time) plus 8 truly-concurrent `zig build test-bpm-main` invocations (3-way and 5-way parallel OS-process launches against the same `BPM_TEST_DB_URL`) all passed cleanly post-fix — versus reliable `DuplicateNameVersion`/`DefinitionNotFound` failures under the identical 3-way parallel launch pre-fix. CI: `Build Summary: 38/38 steps succeeded` on PR #451.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh445-flaky-pd07-20260805`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0141.json`.

**ISS-0142** ([GitHub #446](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/446) — `lint_test_isolation.py` T010 BLOCKER count is 217 across 127 files, far above ISS-0121/GH #387+#402's reported "<10"; baseline suppression file stale)
- **Closes GitHub #446**: `python tools/lint_test_isolation.py tests/integration` now exits 0 (BLOCKER=0, MAJOR=0, 225 suppressed from baseline). `zig build test-env-verify` check C5 (test isolation lint) now PASSES.
- **Diagnosis**: the reported 217 BLOCKER / 25 MAJOR (with baseline active) was mostly line-number drift, not new violations. Diffed the current `--no-baseline --json` scan (266 findings) against the 2026-05-29 baseline's 524 entries by `(file, code, message)` — ignoring line number, since UUID literal text is stable even when unrelated edits shift line numbers. **218 of 266 (82%) matched an existing baseline entry exactly except for line number** — legitimate baseline maintenance, not new violations. Only **48 were genuinely new** (37 BLOCKER + 11 MAJOR) across 21 files.
- **Fixed in source (41 of 48)**: replaced hardcoded UUID literals with per-test-generated values across `exp103_instance_waits_test.zig`, `exp401_exp402_comp_restore_test.zig`, `ext01_service_task_test.zig`, `ext03_plugin_integration_test.zig`, `idn02_group_management_test.zig`, `idn03_role_access_test.zig`, `instance_error_test.zig`, `iss601_state_snapshots_test.zig`, `sch02_timer_polling_test.zig`, `svc01_service_catalog_scope_test.zig`, `svc03_definition_activation_scope_test.zig`, and `exp601_tier_quota_test.zig` (20 of the 48, the largest single cluster) — using `TestHarness.newUuidString()`/`newUuid()`, `bpm.uuid.generateUuidV4BytesInto()`/`newUuidV4()`, or each file's own existing `fillRandom` helper, following the established pattern from `adp03_tenant_context_resolution_test.zig` (ISS-0139). Also fixed one genuine T020 (module-level shared mutable counter in `entity_subsystem_test.zig`, replaced with a per-call random suffix), converted one never-mutated module `var` to `const` (`onboarding_realm_guard_test.zig`'s `dummy_ctx_byte`, with `@constCast` added at its two `*anyopaque` call sites to keep it compiling), and added explicit `BPM_TEST_DB_URL` doc-comment mentions to 5 files that use `TestHarness` (which reads the var internally) without literally naming it (`entity_subsystem_test.zig`, `exp601_tier_quota_test.zig`, both `iss0602_cross_process_child_{a,b}.zig`, `iss0602_same_process_isolation_test.zig`), satisfying T050 honestly rather than gaming the string match.
- **Triaged as non-fixes, added to the regenerated baseline (7 of 48)**: `env01_test.zig:129` and `tm01_tenant_list_test.zig:45/57` are in-memory `AuthContext.user_id` actor markers never persisted as DB rows (same accepted pattern as several already-baselined files, verified no `INSERT` ever references them); `env01_test.zig:463` is a deliberate non-null sentinel for a negative validation test that `executeSaga()` rejects before any DB write occurs; `helpers.zig:344/349/350` are an atomically-synchronized, well-documented process-local cache in the shared test-harness infrastructure file (not a test block itself), the same class already accepted for `helpers.zig:34`'s `names` var. No genuinely-new violation was suppressed to make the number look better — see `docs/issues/ISS-0142.json`'s `resolution` field for the full per-finding rationale.
- **Baseline regenerated**: `tools/lint_test_isolation.baseline.json` rebuilt (225 entries, version 2) from the current `tests/integration/` file/line state, replacing the stale 2026-05-29 snapshot (524 entries).
- **Incidental fix (Unblock-Everything)**: `zig build test-env-verify` check C4 (schema baseline) was failing on 2 leaked `public.tenant` rows (`storage_mode='SCHEMA'`, no matching Postgres schema) left over from an unrelated earlier test run — deleted; C4 now PASSES.
- **Follow-up issue filed, not fixed here (out of scope for this change)**: [GitHub #454](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/454) (ISS-0144 — `zig build test-integration`'s ~19-binary concurrent suite has widespread non-deterministic failures from cross-binary schema/tenant races, discovered while verifying this fix did not regress anything; confirmed pre-existing via an isolated A/B comparison against the unmodified `main`-branch `svc01`/`svc03` test files on the same live `db_test` container).
- **Verification**: `python tools/lint_test_isolation.py tests/integration` exits 0. `zig build test-env-verify` → all 7 checks PASS, `verify_test_env: HEALTHY`. `zig build` and `zig build test` exit 0 with no `error set` output. `python3 tools/lint_sql_param_types.py src tests` exits 0.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh446-lint-baseline-20260805`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0142.json`.

**ISS-0143** ([GitHub #448](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/448) — CI `zig build test` retry loop could report PASS despite a misleading `failed command:` line)
- **Closes GitHub #448**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s bounded-retry loop for `zig build test` (added for [#436](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/436) / ISS-0136) treated `$status -eq 0` as sufficient proof of a pass. Observed on PR #447's CI run: an attempt printed a `failed command: .../test --cache-dir=...` line, and the loop's `status -eq 0` check let it through as PASS without further scrutiny.
- **Two-round fix** — the first attempt was itself wrong: assuming `failed command:` meant a real build-graph failure, an initial fix made the loop fail whenever that line appeared. Re-running that fix for real on its own PR (with `--summary all` added for diagnostics) showed `failed command:` appears on **every** CI run regardless of outcome — always right after a `+- run test w` (waiting) live-progress-bar line — while `Build Summary: 38/38 steps succeeded` printed moments later with every leaf genuinely `success`/`cached`. It is a stale frame from Zig 0.16's non-TTY progress renderer (overwritten in place on a real terminal, preserved as text in a piped CI log), not a failure signal.
- **Corrected fix**: `--summary all` is now always passed, and the loop parses the `Build Summary: N/N steps succeeded` line — PASS requires that line to exist AND its two numbers to be equal. This ignores the cosmetic artifact while still catching genuine partial failures (N < N) and genuine hangs (`timeout(1)` kills the process before any Build Summary line prints, matching #436's real concurrency issue).
- **Verification**: local bash simulation of all relevant scenarios; validated against 3 real CI runs during development (PR #447's original discovery, plus two live re-runs on PR #449 that caught and then corrected the first fix's own mistake).
- **Process note**: the first fix's defect was caught only because its own CI run was watched end-to-end and its raw log read in full, rather than trusting the checkmark — the same discipline that caught the original bug.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh448-ci-retry-loop-false-pass`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0143.json`.

**ISS-0140** ([GitHub #443](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/443) — `verify_schema_baseline` reports drift: 2 tenant schemas missing Postgres schema objects) and **ISS-0139** ([GitHub #442](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/442) — residual T010 hardcoded-UUID violation in `adp03_tenant_context_resolution_test.zig`)
- **Closes GitHub #443 and #442**: `zig build test-env-verify` now reports HEALTHY (all 7 checks PASS) on `db_test`.
- **ISS-0140 root cause**: `src/db/provisioning.zig::provisionTenantSchema()` writes the `public.tenant` row (`storage_mode='SCHEMA'`, the ISS-0114/GH-377 promotion step) in a separate statement *after* `bpm_provision_tenant_schema()` creates the Postgres schema and its `public.tenant_schemas` registration. A provisioning run racing against a concurrent `tools/clean_test_db.py` sweep (same class as [GitHub #364](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/364) / ISS-0106) can leave a `public.tenant` row claiming `storage_mode='SCHEMA'` with no matching `tenant_schemas` row and no schema ever created — a state no existing cleanup rule targeted, so it survived indefinitely and permanently failed `verify_schema_baseline.py`'s tenant-consistency check.
- **ISS-0140 fix**: reconciled the current `db_test` database (removed the 2 orphaned rows) and hardened `tools/clean_test_db.py` with a new self-healing sweep — `DELETE FROM public.tenant` for any `storage_mode='SCHEMA'` row with no matching `tenant_schemas` registration — so any future recurrence clears on the next test-DB cleanup instead of accumulating.
- **Incidental fix (same root cause class, found while verifying)**: `tools/verify_test_env.py`'s C3 check matched `error`/`failed`/`already exists` as a bare substring anywhere in `zig build migrate`'s output. Migration filenames `081_iss101_timers_failed_status.sql` and `092_iss303_timer_fire_error_count.sql` legitimately contain those words in their own names, so every clean `info: skip <filename>` line produced a permanent false BLOCKER on C3 — independent of any real migration health. Fixed to match only genuine `std.log` `error:`/`warning:` line prefixes or an explicit `already exists` error line, per the "fix the detector's condition, not weaken it" rule (`docs/anti-patterns.md`).
- **ISS-0139 root cause**: `tests/integration/adp03_tenant_context_resolution_test.zig` had 5 hardcoded UUID literals — a module-level `tenant_b` constant reused across 6 of the file's 7 tests (both as a JWT claim value and a DB tenant-scoping value) plus 3 more `actor`/`definition_id`/`instance_id` fixture literals — none of the residual instances ISS-0121/GH #387's original T010 cleanup pass caught.
- **ISS-0139 fix**: every DB-touching test now generates its own fixture UUIDs via `TestHarness.newUuidString()` (the established per-test-UUID helper from ISS-0121). The one pure, non-DB test (`TC-ADP-03-02`) generates its UUID via `bpm.uuid.newUuidV4()` directly rather than through `TestHarness.init()`, which opens a real Postgres connection — using it there would have silently turned a previously-DB-free test into one that requires `BPM_TEST_DB_URL`.
- **Verification**: `python tools/verify_schema_baseline.py --check-tenants` → all checks OK. `python tools/lint_test_isolation.py tests/integration/adp03_tenant_context_resolution_test.zig` → no isolation issues. `zig build` exits 0. `zig build test-env-verify` → C1–C7 all PASS, `verify_test_env: HEALTHY`.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh443-tenant-schema-drift`. Issues: `docs/issues/ISS-0140.json`, `docs/issues/ISS-0139.json`.

**ISS-0138** ([GitHub #440](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/440) — `zig build test-env-verify` / `test-wiring-check` fail on Windows when `python3` resolves to the WindowsApps App Execution Alias stub)
- **Closes GitHub #440**: `build.zig`'s `test-env-verify` and `test-wiring-check` steps now shell out to `"python"` instead of `"python3"`, matching the convention already used by `lint_test_table_refs` and `clean_test_db`. On Windows machines where `python3.exe` resolves to the broken WindowsApps App Execution Alias stub ahead of a real interpreter, both steps previously failed immediately with `Python was not found ... error code 49` regardless of actual environment health.
- **Scope**: interpreter selection only — no change to either script's detection logic.
- **Verification**: both steps now correctly invoke the real interpreter and produce genuine tool output (Infrastructure Health Checklist results / test-wiring scan results) instead of the App Execution Alias error, confirmed on the affected Windows machine. `zig build` exits 0 with no `error set` output.
- **Follow-up issues filed, not fixed here (surfaced incidentally while re-verifying this fix, out of scope for it)**: [GitHub #442](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/442) (ISS-0139 — residual T010 hardcoded-UUID violation in `adp03_tenant_context_resolution_test.zig:12`, keeps `test-env-verify` check C5 failing); [GitHub #443](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/443) (ISS-0140 — `verify_schema_baseline` reports 2 tenant schemas missing Postgres schema objects on `bpm_dev`, keeps checks C3/C4 failing).
- Branch: `feature/WF03-ISS-0138-issue-440`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0138.json`.

**ISS-0133** ([GitHub #428](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/428) — transition.zig's 26 in-file tests never ran, wired into no build target)
- **Closes GitHub #428**: `src/engine/transition.zig`'s full 30-test suite now runs under `zig build test-transition`, `zig build test-engine`, and `zig build test` — 30/30 pass, zero leaks, zero crashes.
- **Test-side fixes**: crash fixtures (`TC-EE-02-04`, `TC-EE-02-06`) rebuilt to use heap-owned token strings instead of literals (new `dupeTokenSlice` helper) so freeing the mutated token no longer double-frees. Leak fixtures (`TC-EE-02-07/08`, `TC-EE-06-01..05`, `TC-EE-07-01..04`) now `defer freeInstanceState`/`freeTransitionResult`/`freePendingEvents`, with new helpers (`emptyOwnedStrSlice`, `freePendingEventPayload`) since stack-array fields crash if freed the same way as heap slices. `TC-EE-02-02` was a stale assertion (not an engine defect) — corrected to match `TC-EE-02-03`'s already-correct expectation that the last token reaching END clears tokens and sets `COMPLETED`. `TC-EE-07-04` had a fixture bug (wrong `cancelled_branch_ids`).
- **Genuine production defects found and fixed** while resolving the leak fixtures: `transition()`'s token deep-clone silently dropped `waiting_child_instance_id` on every call (not just `sub_process_completed`), breaking sub-process resume matching in production; three `processNodeEntry` paths (END completion, `ALL_BRANCHES_CANCELLED` cascade, `PARALLEL_GATEWAY` join fire) leaked discarded tokens on every real instance completion/cancellation/join; `parseTimerConfig`/`parseSubProcessConfig` collapsed `OutOfMemory` into their domain error, defeating `std.testing.checkAllAllocationFailures`; and a latent use-after-free in the `PARALLEL_GATEWAY` split's recursive per-token loop (iterating a token slice captured before the loop body reassigned the owning `InstanceState`) — exposed, not created, by the leak fixes, and caught by a previously-passing unrelated test (`TC-EE-03-05`) once the suite was wired in. Fixed by snapshotting target node_ids before recursing.
- **Recurrence prevention**: new `tools/lint_test_wiring.py`, exposed as `zig build test-wiring-check`, scans for test-bearing `.zig` files unreachable from any `addTest` root (same defect class as [GitHub #360](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/360) / ISS-0102). Verified via negative control. Not wired as a hard gate yet — see follow-up issues.
- **Follow-up issues filed, not fixed here (out of scope for this change)**: [GitHub #439](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/439) (ISS-0137 — 55 pre-existing unwired test-bearing files found by the new tool, including all 6 from #360); [GitHub #440](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/440) (ISS-0138 — `zig build test-env-verify`/`test-wiring-check` fail on Windows when `python3` resolves to the WindowsApps App Execution Alias stub instead of a real interpreter).
- **Verification**: `zig build test-transition` 30/30 pass; `zig build test-engine` 46/46 pass; `zig build` exit 0 with no `error set` output; `zig build test` 617/701 pass, 0 failed, 84 skipped (unchanged baseline) — independently re-run and confirmed.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh428-transition-tests`. Commit: `6902fb5`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0133.json`.

**ISS-0135** ([GitHub #433](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/433) — std.time.nanoTimestamp missing on x86_64-macos target)
- **Closes GitHub #433**: `zig build -Dtarget=x86_64-macos` and `-Dtarget=aarch64-macos` now compile cleanly.
- **Root cause**: `std.time` has no timestamp API in Zig 0.16.0 — it moved behind `std.Io.Clock.now(io, clock)`, which needs an `Io` instance unavailable in the two affected helpers. The missing call (`std.time.nanoTimestamp()`) only sat in the platform-fallback branch of a UUID/random-byte generator, reachable only on targets without a dedicated CSPRNG path (Linux and Windows both take one above the fallback).
- **Fix**: `fillRandom()` in `src/admin/tenant_migration.zig` and `src/api/routes/onboarding.zig` now seeds its fallback PRNG from a per-process atomic call counter mixed with `std.Thread.getCurrentId()` and the destination buffer's address — no timestamp or `Io` instance required.
- **Verification**: `zig build`, `zig build test` exit 0. Cross-compile exits 0 (no output) for `x86_64-macos`, `aarch64-macos`, `x86_64-linux-gnu`, `aarch64-linux-gnu`.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-ISS-0135-macos-nanotimestamp`. Commit: `751ec9c`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0135.json`.

**ISS-0129** ([GitHub #419](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/419) — migration runner DROP TRIGGER vs audit trigger C40P01 deadlock on TC-TNT-01-01)
- **Closes GitHub #419**: the C40P01 deadlock on TC-TNT-01-01 re-provisioning is resolved by acquiring `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('bpm.migrations.runForSchema')::bigint)` inside `Migrations.runForSchema()` (`src/db/migrations.zig`) — as the first SQL statement on the migrate connection, inside the wrapping BEGIN. Concurrent `runForSchema()` calls now serialize on a stable lock whose keyspace is disjoint from the audit chain-hash trigger's per-tenant advisory lock, so concurrent audit INSERTs remain unblocked while DROP TRIGGER (migration 1107's per-tenant DO block) no longer collides with the trigger function's predecessor row-lock waits.
- **Design**: `src/design/iss0129_migration_runner_advisory_lock.md`. **Diagnosis**: `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0129-migration-deadlock-diagnosis.yaml`.
- **Regression test**: `tests/integration/iss0129_migration_run_advisory_lock_test.zig` (5 cases; 3 stable PASS). RT-1 and RT-2 are flaky due to secondary issues filed as [GitHub #423](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/423) (ISS-0130 — C42P01 race in migration backfill) and [GitHub #424](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/424) (ISS-0131 — distinct C40P01 lock graph).
- **Branch**: `feature/WF03-gh419-20260805`. Commits: `8fae434` (diag), `c4f4404` (design), `b1fc794` (fix). Test report: `tests/reports/report-20260805-WF03-gh419.yaml`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0129.json`.

**ISS-0602** ([GitHub #414](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/414) — killIdleConnections() cross-process termination BLOCKER)
- **Closed GitHub #414**: `killIdleConnections()` in `tests/integration/helpers.zig` no longer terminates idle connections owned by concurrent test binaries on the shared `bpm_test` database.
- **Root cause**: the helper issued `pg_terminate_backend(pid)` against every `idle in transaction` connection in the database, with no scope to the calling process. Concurrent test binaries terminated each other's connections non-deterministically, causing `error.ConnectionFailed` / `terminating connection due to administrator command` failures across the integration suite.
- **Fix**: every test connection now declares an `application_name` of the form `bpm-test-<owner-tag>` via `TestHarness.init()` → `setTestApplicationName()`. `killIdleConnections()` filters by `application_name = $1` (exact equality against the caller's owner tag), binding the tag as a parameter. SQL changed from unconditional broadcast to per-process scope.
- **New helpers in `tests/integration/helpers.zig`**: `Tag` type, `generateOwnerTag()` (returns `uid_<12hex>`), `setTestApplicationName()`, defensive cross-owner verification before issuing `pg_terminate_backend`.
- **Regression tests** (4, all PASS, zero leaks):
  - `tests/integration/iss0602_cross_process_isolation_test.zig` — 3 tests covering same-process scope, cross-process isolation, and cross-process same-DB coexistence.
  - `tests/integration/iss0602_same_process_isolation_test.zig` — 1 test covering single-process tag scoping.
- **`build.zig`** — added `test-integration-iss0602-cross` and `test-integration-iss0602-same` targets.
- **Verification**: `zig build test` exits 0; `zig build test-integration-iss0602-{cross,same}` exits 0 (4/4 PASS).
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh414-20260804`. Commits: `b8163cb` (initial), `5b52a46` (rework 1 — compile fix), `db5c2c7` (rework 2 — runtime/test logic + memory leak fix), `b8eae6f` (Step 5 PASS evidence). Test report: `tests/reports/report-20260804-WF03-gh414-final.yaml`.

**ISS-0113** ([GitHub #376](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/376) — event-store idempotency fixture isolation, verification pending)
- **Deterministic UUID collision mitigation**: removed deterministic-seed UUID generation from `tests/integration/iss203_idempotency_keys_test.zig` and `tests/integration/iss202_merge_atomicity_test.zig`; affected fixtures now use `TestHarness.newUuid()` so shared-database test runs do not reuse event and idempotency identifiers.
- **Regression prevention**: added lint rule **T011** to `tools/lint_test_isolation.py`, which flags deterministic-seed UUID helper definitions and calls in integration tests.
- **Applied commits**: implementation `195d08586db40b71f27edb5df10afaa2e8bad8d9`; Step 5 verification evidence `b645a84`.
- **Verification deferred**: build and unit checks passed, but integration verification is pending because the pre-existing `killIdleConnections()` implementation terminates idle connections owned by concurrent test binaries. The infrastructure BLOCKER is tracked separately as [GitHub #414](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/414). ISS-0113 remains `IN_PROGRESS` until that blocker is fixed and the integration suite is rerun.

**ISS-0112** (GitHub #375) — Fixed missing webhook_subscriptions.secret_ref and secret_key_id columns in SCHEMA-mode tenant schemas. Migration 1134 adds these columns to all tenant schemas, resolving EXT-02 webhook dispatch test failures. Root cause: GBL-128 only added columns to public schema (which was later dropped by GBL-073), and GBL-133 reconciliation missed per-tenant schemas.

**ISS-0114** ([GitHub #377](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/377) — Tenant schema and search_path state)
- **Resolved across three layers** for the multi-tenant storage_mode routing regression that originally cascaded into 7 failing integration TCs and 107 downstream test failures:
  1. **`src/db/pool.zig` resolver fallback**: `resolveAndCacheStorageMode` now consults `public.tenant_schemas` when `public.tenant` returns 0 rows for the requested tenant_id. A presence-in-`tenant_schemas` row is treated as authoritative evidence of SCHEMA mode rather than falling through to `LEGACY_RLS`.
  2. **`src/db/provisioning.zig` Step 6a authoritative INSERT**: the `INSERT INTO public.tenant` now populates all 7 NOT NULL columns — `id`, `slug` (derived as `tenant-` || replace(id::text, '-', '')), `display_name`, `status=ACTIVE`, `tenant_type=production`, `storage_mode=SCHEMA`, `created_at=NOW()`, `updated_at=NOW()` — eliminating the C23502 (null value in column slug) cascade that broke every fresh tenant provisioning.
  3. **Migration 1135 (`1135_iss0114_backfill_public_tenant_storage_mode.sql`)**: idempotent UPSERT for existing rows that have a `tenant_schemas` entry but no `public.tenant` row. Aligns the backfill SQL with the Step 6a authoritative INSERT.
- **Plus**: `src/db/migrations.zig` `runForSchema` now asserts `SHOW search_path` post-condition before returning; `tests/integration/helpers.zig` `tenant_context.clear()` is called per-test to reset the threadlocal `storage_mode` cache (eliminating sticky mode-cache contamination across tests in the same binary).
- **Verification**: net **+35 passes / −35 fails, no regressions**. 3 of the 7 previously failing TCs now pass: **TC-TNT-01-03, TC-TNT-02-02, TC-TNT-03-05**. The 4 still-failing TCs are **distinct downstream defects** filed as separate follow-up issues: ISS-0127 / D1 (resolver fallthrough on TC-TNT-03-01/03), ISS-0128 / D2 (`StoreError.InstanceNotFound` on TC-DB-03-01), ISS-0129 / D3 (migration 1135 DROP TRIGGER deadlock C40P01 on TC-TNT-01-01).
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh377-20260804`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0114.json`. Diagnosis report: `docs/issue-reports/ISS-0114-WF03-gh377-20260804.yaml`.

**ISS-0205** (GitHub #400 — webhook_deliveries.status case alignment)
- **Closes GitHub #400 (ISS-0205)**: `src/webhook/dispatcher.zig` was writing `webhook_deliveries.status` in lowercase (`pending`, `failed`, `delivered`, `retrying`) but the CHECK constraint defined in `migrations/085_iss106_webhook_deliveries_outbox.sql` requires UPPERCASE values (`PENDING`, `FAILED`, `DELIVERED`, `RETRYING`). Every INSERT in the dispatcher failed with `C23514 webhook_deliveries_status_check`.
- **Fix**: 8 INSERT/UPDATE sites in `src/webhook/dispatcher.zig` (lines 88, 172, 207, 251, 295, 371, 406, 440) aligned to UPPERCASE status literals. The `PAUSED` literal at line 419 and the 5 `ACTIVE` sites were left unchanged (they already match the constraint).
- **Test fixtures aligned**: `tests/integration/iss205_webhook_outbox_test.zig` (TC1 line 194 `pending`→`PENDING`, TC2 line 278 `failed`→`FAILED`) and `tests/integration/iss106_webhook_outbox_test.zig` / `tests/integration/ext02_webhook_dispatch_test.zig` SELECT queries updated to match UPPERCASE.
- **Idempotency fix**: TC1/TC2/TC3 in `iss205_webhook_outbox_test.zig` use `ON CONFLICT (email) DO NOTHING` (was `ON CONFLICT (id)`) so re-runs without a fresh test DB no longer fail with `C23505 users_email_key`.
- **Verification**: `zig build test` exits 0 (247/247). `zig build test-integration-iss205` exits 2/3 PASS — TC1+TC2 PASS prove the case-alignment fix is correct (both fail with C23514 on main). TC3 fails on a pre-existing schema/code mismatch (column `s.secret_ref` does not exist, C42703) — `webhook_subscriptions.secret_ref` is referenced in `src/webhook/dispatcher.zig` but the migration GBL-128 ADD COLUMN block is guarded by `to_regclass(webhook_subscriptions)` which returns NULL in the public schema where the migration runs (the table lives in per-tenant schemas). Tracked separately at GitHub #375 (ISS-0112 schema drift cluster). Out of scope for GH #400.
- Design: `src/design/iss205-webhook-status-case.md`. Issue: `docs/issues/ISS-0205.json`. Test report: `tests/reports/report-20260803-WF03-gh400.yaml`. Inner report: `docs/issue-reports/WF03-gh400-20260804-step-5-test-runner-INNER-REPORT.yaml`.
- Branch: `feature/WF03-gh400-20260804`. Commits: `98dff26`, `6d40475`, `e373a4c`, `fe2ece0`, `f5e13f2`, `9af552d`, `13bd19a`.

### Fixed (ISS-0601 / GitHub #401 — iss601_state_snapshots_test baseline-snapshot alignment, 6/9 pass)
- **Closes GitHub #401 (ISS-0601)**: `tests/integration/iss601_state_snapshots_test.zig` now passes 6/9 (was 4/9). The four originally-failing tests (TC-ISS-601-01, TC-02, TC-05, TC-08 v2) were written before the baseline-at-`seq=1` snapshot contract introduced by ISS-601 in `src/engine/instance.zig InstanceStore.create()` (~line 860). Assertions were updated to expect the baseline snapshot row per the design in `src/design/iss601-test-baseline-snapshot-fix.md`.
- **Test contract changes**: TC-01 now expects 1 snapshot at `seq=1` after `create()` (was 0); TC-02 expects 2 snapshots after `create()` + 49 events + explicit `takeSnapshot(..., 25)` (seq=1 baseline + seq=25); TC-05 accounts for the baseline seq=1 row in its state/token assertions; TC-08 v2 expects 3 snapshots (seq=1 + 1000 + 2000) and includes the baseline in the latest-of-multiple reconstruction.
- **Cleanup (MINOR)**: moved `SET session_replication_role = 'replica'` earlier in `tests/integration/helpers.zig TestHarness.init()` so the misleading `POSTGRES ERROR: audit_entries is immutable` log noise during `resetTestData()` is silenced. Trigger `bpm_audit_immutable_guard()` from migration 020 no longer fires because the per-test `replica` setting is now active before `deleteTableBestEffort('audit_entries')` runs.
- **Verification**: `BPM_TEST_DB_URL=postgres://bpm:bpm@localhost:5434/bpm_test zig build test-integration-iss601` → 6 pass, 1 skip, 2 fail. Pre-cycle and post-cycle gates green. Test report: `tests/reports/report-20260803-WF03-gh401-20260803.yaml`.
- **2 remaining failures are out of scope**: TC-ISS-601-01 and TC-ISS-601-05 hit a pre-existing memory leak in `src/definition/store.zig:1356 parseGraphJson` (the `create()` path accumulates a parse-allocator handle that is not freed when the baseline snapshot's `state_blob` is later dropped by reconstruction). Filed separately as GitHub #406 / ISS-0601-LEAK-001. The WF-03 design goal — align the four tests with the documented baseline-at-seq=1 contract — is achieved.

- Closes #387.

### Fixed (ISS-0122 / GitHub #388 — Audit chain UTF-8 resilience)
- Resolved SQLSTATE 22021 'invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8"' exception when audit entries contain non-UTF-8 `resource_id` values.
- **Migration 1107** (`fix_audit_chain_text_resource_id.sql`): Widened `resource_id` parameter from `UUID` to `TEXT` in all chain-hash computation functions. Pre-normalizes non-UTF-8 bytes to `'<invalid-utf8:N>'` format before hashing. Wraps hash computation in `EXCEPTION` block returning 64-zero sentinel `'0'*64` on failure. Applied to both `public` and all `tenant_*` schemas.
- **Migration 1108** (`iss0122_validator_skip_zero_sentinel.sql`): Updated validator function to skip rows with zero-sentinel chain_hash. Normalizes `resource_id` via `BEGIN/EXCEPTION` block before recomputing hash (critical fix for validation accuracy). Matches trigger's normalization logic.
- **Migration 1109** (`iss0122_apply_chain_hash_lock_cast.sql`): Added explicit `::bigint` cast on advisory lock key to prevent SQLSTATE 42P08 "inconsistent types deduced" errors in concurrent non-UTF-8 inserts.
- **Migration 1110** (`iss0122_validator_zero_sentinel_per_tenant.sql`): Applied validator zero-sentinel handling to every `tenant_*` schema via per-tenant DO loop.
- Added regression test suite in `tests/integration/audit_chain_utf8_test.zig`: TC-ISS-0122-01 (ASCII happy path, PASS), TC-ISS-0122-02 (non-UTF-8 no exception, PASS), TC-ISS-0122-03 (validator skip zero-sentinel, PASS), TC-ISS-0122-04 (concurrent non-UTF-8 inserts, deferred — Zig test infrastructure issue).
- Verified: Business cases TC-EE-10-06 and TC-EXT-01-INT-07 remain PASS.

### Fixed (ISS-0123 / GitHub #389 — DLQ table rename + audit trigger isolation)
- Renamed `dead_letter_queue` → `dead_letter_items` across 22 source/test files (post-072 canonical name).
- Wrapped `trg_bpm_test_fail_audit_insert` installation in `obs05_dlq_test.zig` in a SAVEPOINT with `errdefer ROLLBACK` for unconditional cleanup.
- Added `tools/lint_sql_table_refs.py` to detect legacy table names in `src/` and `tests/integration/`.
- Added regression tests in `tests/integration/iss0123_regression_test.zig`: TC-ISS-0123-01 source assertion and TC-ISS-0123-02 audit-insert smoke test.
- Confirmed `migrations/072_tnt01_rename_legacy_tables.sql` as canonical.

### Fixed
- **ISS-0124 / GitHub [#390](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/390) (MAJOR)**: Resolve all PostgreSQL C42883 `operator does not exist: text = integer` / `uuid = text` errors. Fixed asymmetric SQL type casting in 8 source files (`subscription_store.zig`, `identity/registry.zig`, `identity/onboarding.zig`, `obs/timeline.zig`, `oidc/jit_provisioning.zig`, `entities/projector.zig`, `api/middleware/auth.zig`, `tasks/store.zig`) and corrected TC-TNT-07 migration version key format. All C42883 errors eliminated; TC-TNT-07 tests now 15/15 passing. ([#396](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/pull/396))

- **ISS-0112 / GitHub [#375](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/375) (MAJOR)**: Migration/schema drift and shared database state break 42 integration tests across `ext02_webhook_dispatch_test` (10), ADP/OIDC migration checks (8), `env03` (5), `exp201_202_entities_test` (3), service catalog (6), `env01`/`env02` (2), and 8 other cases. The shared `db_test` baseline had drifted: `public.schema_migrations` ledger reported 1 row while `migrations/` contained 97 SQL files (all of which the prior `zig build migrate` log reported as skipped), the schema probe had only `public` and `tenant_default` schemas with 51 public tables while `tenant_rows.txt` listed 10 tenants, and bare `public.tenant` fixtures (including the `ensureDefaultOidcSeeds` default tenant) omitted `tenant_type='test'` so `tools/clean_test_db.py`'s filter left production-defaulted fixtures behind. Fixed in 5 changes per `src/design/fix-iss0112.md` §6:
  1. **`migrations/GBL-105_iss0112_schema_ledger_reconcile.sql`** created — `-- reapply_on_drift: true` header, 97-row ledger backfill for `public.schema_migrations` and per-tenant schemas (skipping `GBL`-prefixed rows already present), `webhook_subscriptions.secret_ref` column add, `public.tenant_realm_binding` table create, per-tenant `entity_type_instances` create, idempotent CHECK constraint upgrades (guarded by `pg_constraint` existence check).
  2. **`src/db/migrations.zig` + `src/db/provisioning.zig`** updated — `Migrations.runForSchema` now takes `force_reconcile: bool` (5th arg) and reads the first 1 KiB of each migration file to detect the `-- reapply_on_drift: true` header; `provisioning.zig:112` call site updated to pass `false` by default.
  3. **`tests/integration/helpers.zig`** refactored — canonical default-tenant `INSERT` now sets `tenant_type='test'` with `production_tenant_id` self-reference (satisfying `ck_tenant_type_fk_coherence`); 9-row `SVC-01..04` fixture block removed (per-test fixtures now required); new `pub fn cleanupTestTenant()` helper for `defer`-block use; 3 call sites updated for the new `force_reconcile` parameter.
  4. **`tools/clean_test_db.py`** — `--include-fixtures` CLI flag added; gated extra `DELETE`s for `LEGACY_RLS`-without-`tenant_type` fixtures (operator-initiated contamination cleanup).
  5. **`tools/verify_schema_baseline.py`** created — psycopg2-based 4-invariant consistency checker (ledger count, per-migration row presence, tenant schema consistency, expected CHECK constraints), with `--check-tenants` and `--auto-fix` flags, exit codes 0/1/2.

  Affects integration tests for: `TC-EXT-02` (webhook_subscriptions.secret_ref baseline), `TC-TNT-01..03` (per-tenant schema and search_path isolation), `TC-ISS-503` (pre-flight guard consistency), `TC-ADP-02`/`04a`/`04b`/`05`/`06`/`09`/`10` (tenant/audit/identity columns at baseline), `TC-EXP-201`/`202` (entity_type_instances and entity command API), `TC-SVC-01`/`04` (service catalog fixture hygiene), `TC-ENV-01`/`02` (tenant_type field and per-test isolation).

- **ISS-0125 / GitHub [#391](https://github.com/tvolodi/R-Co/issues/391) (MAJOR)**: `process_definitions` DELETE blocked by FK constraint violation on `instance_definition_snapshots` (PostgreSQL C23503 error) in 43 integration test runs. Migration `004_definitions.sql` declared `instance_definition_snapshots.definition_id` with `REFERENCES process_definitions(id)` but without `ON DELETE CASCADE`, defaulting to `NO ACTION`. Fixed by creating migration `migrations/1106_iss0125_instance_definition_snapshots_cascade.sql` to add the `ON DELETE CASCADE` referential action, plus cleanup helper reordering in `tests/integration/helpers.zig` and regression tests in `tests/integration/iss0125_cascade_test.zig`.
